# codegraph — Implementation Checklist

> **Note (2026-09-30):** `bench-reports/`, the retrieval-only suites (`benchmarks/llm_tasks_*.yaml`), the old benchmark scripts and all pre-2026-09-30 benchmark results were removed; the one benchmark that counts is `benchmarks/dataset/` (`run-sot.sh`, see `CLAUDE.md` "Benchmarking"). File names below that no longer exist are recoverable from git history at commit `5d413ea`. Numbers below were measured on the retired suites: history, not evidence.

> Track every deliverable from plans/00 through plans/11.
> Format: `- [ ]` = not started · `- [~]` = in progress · `- [x]` = done
>
> **Gap review applied 2026-05-11**: Plans 02–10 patched; Plan 11 added covering startup, AppState wiring, config discovery, health endpoint, error propagation, graceful shutdown, queue overflow, data retention, privacy audit, and call resolution accuracy.
>
> **Status updated 2026-05-12 (gap review + refactor pass)**: Gap analysis vs all 12 plans completed. Major gaps closed:
> - `find_relevant_code` now uses the real `RetrievalPipeline` (was returning empty stub)
> - `full_index()` now called on normal startup (was only called with `--index-only`)
> - HTTP server now binds BEFORE `full_index()` so `/health` returns 503 during initial index
> - REST API fully implemented: all 8 endpoints (`/api/graph`, `/api/search`, `/api/symbol/:id`, `/api/symbol/by-name/:name`, `/api/file/*path`, `/api/status`, `/api/languages`, `/api/impact/:id`)
> - CORS layer added to both MCP (`/mcp`) and REST (`/api/*`) routers
> - Config discovery implemented: upward CWD search + `CODEGRAPH_CONFIG` env var + Git-root fallback
> - `--clean` flag implemented (deletes data dir and exits)
> - `CODEGRAPH_MODEL_PATH` env var support for air-gapped environments
> - Graceful shutdown: SIGINT + SIGTERM handled via `axum::serve().with_graceful_shutdown()`
> - File rename handling added to watcher (`Rename{Both}` pattern)
> - Config validation improved: warns on unknown model names + port < 1024
> - `QueryPipeline` dyn-compatible trait added (enables `Arc<dyn QueryPipeline>` in MCP + REST state)
> - `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` now clean (0 errors)
> - New `GraphDb` methods: `top_symbols_by_in_degree()`, `edges_between()`, `language_counts()`
> Smoke test: 43 files, 294 symbols, `/health` 200, all 5 MCP tools working, all 8 REST endpoints responding.
>
> **Status updated 2026-08-03 (competitive audit + hardening)**: A full audit against Tool A produced a hardening plan; Initiatives 1–6 implemented and verified, Initiative 7 partial — see **Plan 14** below for the full breakdown. Highlights: full-index parsing parallelized (~6.1× measured on a synthetic corpus), C fn-ptr dynamic-dispatch edge synthesis added, memory-budget-aware pool sizing added, Kotlin re-enabled + Lua added (13 → 15 languages), and a real pre-existing bug found and fixed — `default_languages()`/`languages_to_extensions()` had drifted from `LanguageRegistry`, so most supported languages (csharp, ruby, kotlin explicitly wrong; several others simply missing) were never actually watched by default. An independent code-reviewer pass over the whole diff then found and fixed two further real bugs *introduced by this session's own changes* (synthesized-edge duplication on every restart; a memory-streaming regression against Initiative 5's own goal) plus two minor issues — see Plan 14's "Code review" note. `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` both clean throughout, including after the review fixes.

## Plan 07 — File Watcher & Incremental Indexing
*Ref: [plans/07-file-watcher.md](plans/07-file-watcher.md)*

- [x] `IndexStatus` + `IndexState` enum (fresh / indexing / stale / error)
- [x] `FileWatcher::new()` — notify-rs recursive watcher on workspace root
- [x] `FileWatcher::next_batch()` — debounce + drain window
- [x] `FileWatcher::should_index()` — extension filter + glob ignore patterns
- [x] `IndexWorker::run()` — async loop consuming batches
- [x] `IndexWorker::index_path()` — ordered: SQLite → invalidate → chunks → embed → LanceDB (used by the live watcher loop)
- [x] Chunk bootstrap order: SQLite upsert BEFORE `build_chunks()` so callers/callees queryable
- [x] File deletion: calls `StoreCoordinator::delete_file()`
- [x] `full_index()` — walkdir + serial embed+store (thin wrapper over the two steps below; used by CLI one-shot index and overflow recovery)
- [x] **Perf fix (2026-07-31)**: slow-indexing complaint root-caused and fixed. Measured on this repo (80 files / 1368 symbols, copied to an isolated scratch dir, real BGE-small ONNX model):
  - Before: full startup index took **~110s** before the project was marked `Fresh` (nothing usable until then).
  - Root cause #1 (dominant, ~50s): `scip.auto_run` (`true` by default) ran `rust-analyzer scip .` as a **plain blocking `std::process::Command::status()` call directly inside the async `full_index` path** — not wrapped in `spawn_blocking`, so it also stalled a tokio runtime worker thread for its whole duration, and it sat before the point where `state` flipped to `Fresh`.
  - Root cause #2 (secondary): `BatchEmbedder::embed_chunks` was only ever called with one file's chunks at a time (a handful of symbols), so most ONNX forward passes ran far under the configured `batch_size` (32) — paying fixed per-call overhead per file instead of amortizing it. `LanceDB::upsert` was likewise one call per file. `GraphDb::callers_of`/`callees_of` used `conn.prepare()` instead of `prepare_cached()`, recompiling SQL on every one of the 2×symbols-per-file calls made while building chunks.
  - Fix: `worker.rs` split into `full_index_graph()` (walk + parse + SQLite write only, no embedding, no SCIP) and `embed_backlog()` (builds chunks + embeds + writes vectors for a whole backlog of files in cross-file batches sized to `batch_size`). `run_scip_enrichment()` extracted out of the graph pass entirely, its subprocess call now dispatched via `spawn_blocking`. `callers_of`/`callees_of` switched to `prepare_cached`.
  - Server startup (`src/server.rs`) now calls `full_index_graph()`, marks the project `Fresh` immediately after (graph-based MCP/REST tools usable at this point), then runs `run_scip_enrichment()` and `embed_backlog()` concurrently (`tokio::join!`) as a fully independent background task. `IndexStatus.semantic_pending` reports embedding progress until it drains to 0; `find_relevant_code` results are incomplete until then.
  - After: same repo, same conditions — **graph phase (→ Fresh) measured at ~9s**, down from ~110s to reach any usable state before. Embedding (~50s) and SCIP enrichment now overlap in the background and no longer block anything.
  - `full_index()` (thin `full_index_graph` → `run_scip_enrichment` → `embed_backlog` wrapper) is kept for the CLI one-shot `codegraph index` and watcher overflow-recovery paths, which still want "fully done" blocking semantics.
  - Follow-up done (2026-08-03): `full_index_graph` now parses in parallel via `Extractor::extract_many()` (rayon, fresh parser per file — no shared-`Mutex<Parser>` contention) and writes graph data in chunked (200-file) `GraphDb::upsert_batch()` transactions instead of the old per-file sequential loop; still one shared writer `Mutex<Connection>` (a separate connection-pool/WAL-multi-reader option remains undone — only transaction batching was pursued). Measured **~6.1× wall-clock speedup** on a synthetic corpus (500–5,000 files) — see `bench-reports/parallel-index-synthetic-2026-08-03.md`. The live watcher loop (`run()`) and `flush_pending()` were also changed to batch `flush_embed_batch()` once per debounced/drained set of paths instead of once per file.
  - **Code review (code-reviewer agent, 2026-07-31)** on this fix found and fixed:
    - [x] **Correctness (was silent data corruption)**: `flush_embed_batch`'s checksum-sentinel guard originally protected only the final `set_file_checksum` commit, not the preceding `vector.upsert` call — since `embed_chunks` for a full batch is real multi-second ONNX work, a concurrent `index_path()` call from the live watcher could re-index the same file with newer content and commit newer vectors, only for this batch's stale `vector.upsert` (delete-then-insert by `file_path`) to land afterward and silently overwrite them, with SQLite still reporting the file fresh (checksum matched) and LanceDB holding stale embeddings with no path back to a re-index. Fixed by re-checking each file's checksum immediately before `vector.upsert` and dropping any item that's moved on, narrowing the race to the (now `.await`-free) gap between that check and the upsert call.
    - [x] **Observability bug**: `run()`'s per-batch status update unconditionally cleared `IndexStatus.last_error`, which would erase the "semantic indexing degraded" signal `embed_backlog` sets as soon as any unrelated file save landed. Fixed: `run()` now only clears `last_error` if it isn't a `"semantic indexing:"`-prefixed message.
    - [x] **Hardening**: added `IndexWorker::try_begin_full_index()` / `FullIndexGuard` so a watcher-overflow-triggered `full_index()` can't run concurrently with the startup graph+embed sequence (or vice versa) — closes a second, independent path to the same stale-vector race above, and skips with a log line instead of racing.
    - [x] Benchmark re-run (2026-07-31), verifying the embedding-input-change flagged above didn't regress quality: built the release binary, indexed the real `lexical` checkout (`~/Documents/Workspace/Learn/lexical`, 1066 files / 5985 symbols — same size as the 2026-07-29 baseline in `bench-reports/lexical-find-relevant-code-2026-07-29.md`) in a fully isolated environment (scratch `HOME` so registry/daemon/data-dir never touched the real `~/.codegraph` or the live production daemon; `CODEGRAPH_MODEL_PATH` pointed read-only at the already-downloaded model to skip re-downloading), waited for `semantic_pending` to reach 0, then ran `scripts/llm-task-eval.py --tasks benchmarks/llm_tasks_lexical.yaml --server ... ` with `EVAL_BACKEND=string` (objective, no LLM/API-key needed — checks whether `expected_function` appears in the top-N `find_relevant_code` results). Result: **35/40 (87.5%) @top_n=5**, **38/40 (95.0%) @top_n=8** — both within one task of the 2026-07-29 baseline (36/40 @5, 38/40 @8). Confirmed via per-task diff that the specific misses **fully swapped** (the 4 tasks that missed before are now hits; 5 different tasks miss now) rather than a fixed set of queries getting systematically worse — consistent with the expected embedding-input change (chunks now always have complete post-`resolve_edges` cross-file context) shuffling rankings, not a regression from this fix. No further action needed before tagging.
- [x] Watcher spawned as tokio task after app start
- [x] `Arc<RwLock<IndexStatus>>` shared with MCP and REST handlers
- [x] Status counts (total files/symbols) updated after each batch
- [x] File rename handling (Rename{Both} pattern)
- [x] Watcher event queue overflow → trigger `full_index()` (already implemented before 2026-08-03; `CLAUDE.md`'s "known gaps" list had gone stale claiming otherwise — corrected)
- [x] `IndexWorker::flush_pending()` on shutdown (already implemented before 2026-08-03; same stale-doc correction as above)
- [x] Memory-budget-aware parse pool sizing for the full-index path (2026-08-03): `codegraph_watcher::memory_budget::resolve_pool_size()` derives a rayon thread-pool size from live `sysinfo` available memory (or an explicit `[watcher] memory_budget_mb` override for containers `sysinfo` can't see a cgroup limit for), wired into `full_index_graph_blocking` via a scoped `rayon::ThreadPoolBuilder`
- [x] Dynamic-dispatch edge synthesis (2026-08-03): `codegraph_parser::synthesis::c_fnptr` recovers C/C++ function-pointer dispatch-table call edges (`dispatcher->field(...)` → registered handler) the 3-stage resolver structurally can't see; runs as an additive post-pass after `resolve_edges()` in `full_index_graph`, tagged `EdgeSource::Synthesized`; gated by `[watcher] synthesis_enabled` (default on)

### Tests
- [x] Test: create file → appears in SQLite + LanceDB (within a generous timeout — see `incremental_sync.rs` doc comment on why the literal 500ms target isn't asserted in this sandboxed test environment)
- [x] Test: modify file → only that file re-indexed (verifies the *other* file's symbols are byte-for-byte unchanged)
- [x] Test: delete file → symbols removed from both stores (SQLite cascade + LanceDB row count both checked)
- [x] Test: ignored path (e.g., `node_modules/`) → not indexed
- [x] Benchmark: 100 rapid saves → no duplicates (hard assertion); p95 latency measured and printed but asserted against a looser bound than 500ms — see `rapid_saves_p95_latency_and_no_duplicates` in `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/incremental_sync.rs` for why, and the live-loop embed-batching fix (Initiative 1 follow-up above) that measurably improved it
- [x] `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/dispatch_synthesis.rs` — end-to-end proof the C fn-ptr synthesis pass is wired into `full_index_graph` (not just unit-tested in isolation), plus a `synthesis_enabled=false` no-op check
- [x] `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/memory_constrained_index.rs` — an extreme `memory_budget_mb` override (forcing a size-1 pool) still indexes every file correctly
- [x] `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/language_config_sync.rs` — regression test for the `default_languages()`/`LanguageRegistry` drift bug found 2026-08-03 (see Plan 02 and "Known bug fixed" notes)

## Ship Gate Checklist

All of the following must be true before tagging v0.1.0:

- [x] `cargo build --release` produces a single binary (40 MB stripped — `opt-level="z"`, `lto=true`, `strip=true`)
- [x] `cargo test --workspace` passes (73 tests: 22 MCP, 25 parser, 24 store, 7 pipeline, 4 integration + 8 embed tests)
- [x] `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean
- [x] MRR ≥ 0.50 on benchmark suite (oracle: 1.0; real model needed for production MRR)
- [x] Token reduction ≥ 8× average (oracle synthetic corpus: **30.4×** — grep avg 29 975 tok vs CG avg 985 tok; gate_pass=true; `--bench-tokens --assert-reduction 8.0` passes)
- [x] Query p95 ≤ 100 ms (p95 ≈ 2 ms in oracle benchmark)
- [x] Incremental re-index p95 ≤ 500 ms (p95 ≈ 80 ms with ONNX embed; SQLite-only ≈ 48 ms)
- [ ] Cold-start ≤ 2 s on 100k LOC repo (warm machine, model cached)
- [~] Binary size ≤ 50 MB — **macOS ARM64: 63 MB** (gate exceeded; growth from 14 tree-sitter grammars vs 5 at gate-set time; Linux/musl expected ~40-45 MB; gate should be revised to 70 MB for macOS or measured on target platform)
- [x] Call resolution rate ≥ 90% (100% on synthetic 3-file corpus; `--bench-resolution --assert-call-rate 0.90`)
- [x] Zero outbound network calls during indexing (privacy audit 2026-05-29: all crates scanned — no TCP/HTTP/DNS calls; `ureq_get` in `main.rs` only used by `cmd_status` CLI display command hitting `localhost`)
- [x] All 7 MCP tools verified working end-to-end — 5 live-verified with Claude Code 2026-05-29; all 7 (incl. `find_implementations`, `summarize_module`) re-verified live 2026-07-29 via direct JSON-RPC against the release binary post-fix, on both the `codegraph` project itself and a real 5985-symbol TypeScript codebase (Lexical) — see `bench-reports/lexical-find-relevant-code-2026-07-29.md`
- [~] Web UI works in Chrome, Firefox, Safari (no CDN) — UI implemented, assets embedded; needs manual browser smoke test
- [x] `/health` endpoint verified by at least one MCP client integration test (`health_returns_503_while_indexing_and_200_when_fresh` in `tests/integration.rs`)
- [x] README has: install, first-run guide, config example, Claude Code + Cursor integration, benchmark results (updated 2026-05-29: stale stub references removed, language table, benchmark table, endpoint table, correct test count)

### Plan 20 — Remove Vector Search (local + cloud embedding, LanceDB)

*See `plans/20-remove-vector-search.md` for full scope inventory, phase order, and
rationale. User-confirmed 2026-09-25: permanently remove vector search (no local
ONNX/BGE embedder, no cloud embedding model, no LanceDB); retrieval becomes
lexical (FTS5/BM25) + graph expansion + personalized PageRank + rerank only —
the code path that already exists today as "no-semantic"/`embedding_degraded`
mode, made permanent rather than a fallback. The user confirmed the *removal*;
they have not separately confirmed accepting the resulting no-semantic ceiling
(100% lexical suite, 85%/34-40 keycloak, vs. semantic's 100%/100%, per
`bench-reports/keycloak-cross-repo-generalization-2026-08-20.md`) as permanent
— closing that gap is scoped as optional Phase 8 (cloud-judge rerank signal),
not written off.*

- [x] Phase 1 — decoupled `flush_chunk_batch` (renamed from `flush_embed_batch`) FTS5 writes from the vector-upsert path (`codegraph-watcher/src/worker.rs`, `src/server.rs`); checksum-commit gate now tied to the FTS5 write succeeding, not vector-write. **Decision made on the graph-only-pass-vs-Fresh race the plan flagged as open**: re-reading the actual code found it was a false alarm — the live-loop path (`run()`) already calls `flush_chunk_batch` synchronously *before* marking Fresh, and the only async path (`full_index_graph` at startup) populated FTS5 and LanceDB at the exact same call site/timing in the pre-removal code, so removing vector search doesn't introduce a new empty-search window; it was already equally present (and equally benign) in "semantic" mode. No architecture change needed beyond the straightforward decouple.
- [x] Phase 2 — collapsed `RetrievalPipeline<G, V>` → `RetrievalPipeline<G>`: deleted `VectorStore`, `EmbeddingAdapter`, HyDE, `alpha`/`vec_score`, inlined the (formerly degraded-only) lexical+graph branch as the only path. `expand_graph` rewritten around a `HashSet<SymbolId>` candidate pool (no more vec-score map) seeded purely from `lexical_search`.
- [x] Phase 3 — deleted `codegraph-embed` crate entirely, `codegraph-store/src/vector_db.rs`, `StoreCoordinator`'s vector field (now `StoreCoordinator::new(graph)`, one arg), `ort`/`lancedb`/`arrow-*`/`ndarray`/`tokenizers` workspace deps. `EmbeddedChunk` renamed to `IndexedChunk` (vector field dropped).
- [x] Phase 4 — CLI/config surface stripped: `--local-embedder`, `prompt_model_selection`, the whole `codegraph config embedding {show,set}` subcommand, `ensure_model`, `validate_model_name`/`KNOWN_MODELS`, `project_embedding_model`/`codegraph status`'s model column, `scripts/download-model.sh` all removed. `EmbeddingConfig` deleted from `codegraph-core::config`. Stray `[embedding]` blocks cleaned up in this repo's own `.codegraph/config.toml`. `codegraph compare`'s mechanism kept (querying two live projects, comparing MRR/Recall/latency is generic) with its framing reworded from "different embedding models" to "the same corpus under two different `[retrieval]` configs."
- [x] Phase 5 — MCP/API/UI surface: `watch_status`'s `embedding_degraded` field removed; tool descriptions (`find_relevant_code`, `find_implementations`, `watch_status`) rewritten to state retrieval is lexical+graph, full stop; REST/UI audited clean (no `vec_score` or embedding references found in `codegraph-api`/`codegraph-ui`). **`find_implementations`'s "Strategy 2: semantic" deleted entirely** (was running real vector queries) rather than left degraded — Strategy 1 (graph edges: Implements/Inherits/References) is now the sole strategy; `codegraph-scip`'s unrelated `EdgeSource::Semantic` untouched, called out explicitly in the module doc comment so the two meanings of "semantic" don't get conflated again. 7-tool invariant verified still holds (`tools_list_returns_exactly_7_tools` passes).
- [x] Phase 6 — docs rewritten: `CLAUDE.md` (core thesis, crate map, invariants, retrieval-pipeline stage order, ship gate) and `README.md` (architecture diagram, pipeline diagram, CLI reference, config example, the old "Running without a semantic model" section replaced with "How retrieval works without embeddings," benchmarks table, macOS Intel status, license line) both updated; `.codegraph/config.toml`'s stray `[embedding]`/stale `alpha` comment cleaned up; `scripts/bench-popular.sh`/`scripts/live-demo.sh` stale `download-model.sh`/ONNX references fixed. Real measured stripped release binary: **~77 MB** (down from Plan 19's 115.5MB), comfortably under the 200MB gate — `CLAUDE.md` updated with the real number, not a projection. Note on manually reclaiming orphaned `~/.codegraph/<hash>/vectors/` LanceDB directories added to `CLAUDE.md`'s Config section.
- [x] Phase 7 — regression benchmark gate, closed 2026-09-25. Initial run (same day) against a live daemon (`--server` mode, real `find_relevant_code` calls, `EVAL_BACKEND=string`): lexical 40/40 (100%, exact baseline reproduction), keycloak 33/40 (82.5%, one below the documented 34/40 baseline), deterministic across 2 runs. That run's own diagnosis (a razor-thin 0.0007 rank-6-vs-5 margin on `kc-sg-02`, attributed to corpus-size drift from codegraph's own extractor evolving) was independently **re-examined and superseded** by a differential test, prompted by an Opus 5.5 review that questioned the "apples-to-apples wasn't possible" framing rather than accepting it: built the pre-Plan-20 binary (`5fa9887`) in an isolated `git worktree`, indexed keycloak fresh with it, and compared directly against HEAD's fresh index. **Result: exact match** — identical symbol count (82,469), edge count (501,578), 33/40 score, and the same 7 semantic_gap misses (`kc-sg-02,03,06,07,08,12,15`), reproduced twice per binary. **Plan 20 changed nothing about keycloak retrieval quality; the 33/40 predates it entirely.** The 34→33 drop happened earlier, in `a708d81..5fa9887` — bisected specifically for `kc-sg-02` (adjacent-commit build+index+eval, `a86bfb2`=win vs. `a236957`=miss, reproduced twice each after a methodology hazard — concurrent indexing of unrelated registered projects silently timing out the eval's HTTP client — was caught and worked around by trimming the daemon's registry to keycloak-only): **`a236957`** (`fix(languages): populate EdgeKind::Inherits and EdgeKind::References (were dead code)`) is confirmed as the commit where `kc-sg-02` flips, via `getConsentsStream` falling out of the top-40 candidate pool entirely at that commit (worse than HEAD's rank-6 miss). Plausible mechanism (not independently re-confirmed at the per-score-term level): the newly-unioned `References` edges flood high-fan-in seed types' neighbor sets into `expand_graph`'s hash-ordered, first-come 100-candidate cap. **Honest scope boundary**: the broader semantic_gap category swung non-monotonically in this same window (`a86bfb2`=2/15, `a236957`=1/15, `5fa9887`/HEAD=8/15) — `a236957` alone doesn't explain most of that swing; a likely-but-unverified further contributor is `c170145`'s stopword-filter tokenizer fix, flagged as open follow-up, not chased down (out of scope for a `kc-sg-02`-focused bisect). Full account, including a real process-error correction (an earlier draft of the bisect write-up named `a236957` before the bisect was actually run — caught and rewritten against the real, reproduced measurement) and the concurrent-indexing hazard: `bench-reports/plan20-post-removal-regression-check-2026-09-25.md`. **Gate re-pinned and closed** against this measurement: codegraph `904d4a2`, keycloak `0b9e007d`, 82,469 symbols, 501,578 edges, lexical 40/40, keycloak 33/40 — the honest, now-attributed baseline, not a stale 34/40 target.
- [x] Phase 8 — built 2026-09-25, user-requested. **Fetched TypeSafe AI's real API docs first** (`https://docs.typesafe.ai/api` via WebFetch, not the secondhand LangChain blog summary the plan was originally scoped from) before writing any request/response code, per the plan's own explicit requirement: `POST /v1/systemone`, bearer auth, `state`/`model`/`questions` request shape, `noul`/`choice`/`score` question types, `answers` response map. Used the batched-questions-over-one-state design: one API call judges the whole top-N candidate pool at once (each candidate a separate `"noul"` yes/no question against a shared `state` containing the query + all candidates), not one call per candidate.
  - **Mid-implementation restructure, user-suggested**: originally built as `crates/codegraph-jev`; user asked whether a more general `codegraph-decision-engine` crate with Jev as one adapter inside it would let a future second provider be added without a new crate/workspace member each time. Agreed and restructured before any other code referenced the old name — `codegraph-decision-engine` is now the crate, `jev` a sibling module inside it (`codegraph_decision_engine::jev::JevJudge`, re-exported as `codegraph_decision_engine::JevJudge`), matching exactly the shape the old `codegraph-embed` crate used for `BgeSmallModel`+`VoyageModel` before Plan 20 removed it.
  - `codegraph_core::relevance_judge::RelevanceJudge` trait (provider-agnostic — the "what the pipeline needs" contract, not TypeSafe's wire shape): `async fn judge_relevance(query, candidates: &[(SymbolId, String)]) -> HashMap<SymbolId, f32>`. Explicit doc-comment contract: partial results are expected (a missing id means "no judgment," not "zero relevance"), and **implementations own their own timeout** — `codegraph-core` stays tokio-free (existing invariant), so the pipeline can't wrap the call in `tokio::time::timeout` itself; `JevJudge` enforces `timeout_ms` via `reqwest::Client::builder().timeout(...)`.
  - `RetrievalConfig.jev: JevConfig` (`enabled` off by default, `api_key`/`CODEGRAPH_JEV_API_KEY` env fallback, `base_url`, `model`, `top_n`, `weight`, `timeout_ms`). `weight` is an additive bonus outside the `beta+gamma≈1.0` invariant (same pattern `name_match_bonus` already uses), not a new term inside it — deliberate, since Jev is a categorically different kind of signal. `Config::validate()` warns (doesn't hard-fail) if `enabled=true` with no resolvable key.
  - `RetrievalPipeline<G>` gained `pub jev: Option<Arc<dyn RelevanceJudge>>`. New Stage 5 in `query()` (only when `config.jev.enabled && jev.is_some()`): widen `rerank()`'s kept pool to `max(final_n, jev.top_n)`, call the judge once on that whole pool, add `weight * relevance` to each judged candidate's score (unscored/missing candidates keep their pre-Jev score, not zeroed), re-sort with the same SymbolId tiebreak, truncate to `final_n`, renumber ranks. A judging error/timeout never fails the query — falls back to the pre-Jev lexical+graph ranking unchanged.
  - `src/server.rs`'s `build_jev_judge()`: constructs `JevJudge` only when enabled AND a key resolves (config or env); logs and returns `None` otherwise rather than constructing a judge that would fail on every query.
  - `watch_status`/`/api/status` gained a `jev_active` field (`QueryPipeline::jev_active()`, default `false`, overridden by `RetrievalPipeline`) — same spirit as the removed `embedding_degraded` field: an agent-visible signal for a mode that varies, not a new MCP tool (still exactly 7). `watch_status`'s tool description updated to explain it.
  - **8 new tests**: 4 in `codegraph-core/tests/pipeline.rs` with a `MockJudge` (disabled-never-invokes-the-judge; can-promote-a-candidate-ranked-just-outside-final_n; failure-falls-back-without-failing-the-query; partial-results-leave-unscored-candidates-untouched) and 4 in `codegraph-decision-engine/src/jev.rs` pinning the hand-written request/response wire format against the documented schema (can't test against a real account — no API key available in this environment).
  - **Follow-up same day, 2026-09-25: verified working end-to-end against a real account.** User supplied a real OpenRouter API key (`typesafe/jev-1.13` via OpenRouter, not a direct TypeSafe account) — stored in this repo's own gitignored `.codegraph/config.toml` (confirmed via `git check-ignore`, never committed, never echoed back in chat), not in a shell/global env var, since the project's own config file was already the more robust mechanism (no reliance on shell-session persistence for a daemon process). OpenRouter documents `/api/v1/systemone` as schema-compatible with TypeSafe's own endpoint ("for redirecting the TypeSafe SDK") — confirmed via `WebFetch` on `https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/community/jev` before assuming compatibility, so this needed only a `base_url`/`model`/`api_key` config change, zero code changes to `JevJudge`. An ad-hoc `curl`+`sed` smoke-test script hit a `sed` regex bug that captured the whole config line instead of just the key value, and a follow-up attempt to inspect the raw extracted string via `od -c` was correctly blocked by the auto-mode credential-materialization guard — resolved by testing through the real Rust binary instead (which parses the TOML correctly via `serde`, no such bug), not by working around the block. **Verified via**: `watch_status`'s `jev_active: true`; a real `find_relevant_code` call's latency jumping from ~5ms (`explain_symbol`, a non-pipeline control) to ~850-880ms (repeatable across 2 calls) — consistent with an actual network round-trip, not a silent fallback; zero errors/warnings in `daemon.log`; sensible-looking ranked results. `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, and the crate's own doc comments updated to replace the earlier "not verified" caveat with this real result. **Also rebuilt the release binary before testing** — the first daemon restart attempt was still running a pre-Phase-8 release build (missing `jev_active` from `watch_status` entirely), caught and fixed before drawing any conclusion from it.
  - **Follow-up same day, user-requested: benchmarked against keycloak, not just smoke-tested.** Set up keycloak's own project config (`<workspace>/keycloak/.codegraph/config.toml`, outside this repo) with `[retrieval.jev] enabled = true` — **the first write attempt (via a heredoc containing the raw API key) was rejected by the user**, read as "don't put the secret in a file outside this repo's working directory." Adjusted: wrote the config with `enabled`/`base_url`/`model` but no `api_key`, and supplied `CODEGRAPH_JEV_API_KEY` as a transient environment variable only in the single `codegraph start` command — never persisted to any file outside this repo. Ran the same live-daemon `--server` eval 3 times: **33/40 → 36/40 (90%), identical misses every run** (`kc-sg-06`, `kc-sg-07`, `kc-sg-08`, `kc-sg-12`), fully deterministic. The 3 newly-passing tasks (`kc-sg-02`, `kc-sg-03`, `kc-sg-15`) match the predicted mechanism exactly: `kc-sg-02` was previously diagnosed (`bench-reports/keycloak-cross-repo-generalization-2026-08-20.md`) as a scoring-stage miss (reaches the pool, loses to a JUnit test-helper's name-coincidental similarity) — precisely what a relevance judge should fix, and it did. `kc-sg-12`, previously diagnosed as a recall-stage miss (never enters the candidate pool, cut by `lexical_search`'s `top_k` before rerank runs), stayed a miss — exactly as architecturally expected, since `apply_jev` only reranks what `rerank()` already kept, it can't widen recall. Full writeup: `bench-reports/plan20-phase8-jev-keycloak-benchmark-2026-09-25.md`. **Not done**: individually re-diagnosing the 3 still-open misses (`kc-sg-06/07/08`), and a regression check of the lexical suite (already 40/40 without Jev) with Jev enabled. Daemon stopped and both projects' local configs left as configured (keycloak's `.codegraph/config.toml` has `enabled = true` with no stored key — needs `CODEGRAPH_JEV_API_KEY` set again to actually activate on a future run).
  - **Second follow-up same session: tried to close the remaining 4 misses, measured a regression, reverted.** Direct SQLite FTS5 instrumentation (raw `bm25()` ranking, bypassing the pipeline) found `kc-sg-12` alone sits close to reach (raw lexical rank ~29) while the other 3 are hundreds of ranks out (~160, ~173, ~1279) — genuinely honest target was 37/40, not 40/40. Implemented a Jev-gated `lexical_search` recall-widening (`lexical_top_k = max(req.top_k, jev.top_n)`, only when Jev is active — the ungated version of this was already tried and reverted twice on this exact corpus in Plan 16), raised `jev.top_n`'s default 20→50, added 2 new pipeline tests confirming the gating. **Measured: 36/40 → 34/40, reproduced identically across 2 runs** — a real regression, not noise. The specifically-targeted `kc-sg-12` still didn't flip, and 2 previously-passing tasks broke. Most likely cause, per real-world Jev operational guidance the user relayed mid-investigation (32K token context, pay-per-input-token, "keep chunks tight" when scoring many candidates at once): sending 50 full-snippet candidates in one batched judging call instead of 20 plausibly strained judgment quality across the whole batch, not just the new arrivals. **Reverted `jev.top_n` default back to 20** — confirmed this exactly restores 36/40 (same 4 misses), not just "close." Kept the underlying widening mechanism (config-gated, off by default at 20 == no-op vs. pre-widening behavior) since it isn't structurally wrong, just not something a flat number bump alone fixes — a genuine next attempt would need tighter snippets, Jev's `"score"` question type instead of per-candidate `"noul"`, or batching a wide pool into several smaller judged calls, each requiring the same before/after multi-run validation this finding used. Full account: `bench-reports/plan20-phase8-jev-keycloak-benchmark-2026-09-25.md`. Full workspace re-verified clean after the revert (build/clippy/`cargo test --workspace`).
  - **Third follow-up, same session: fixed the actual cause, re-tested, shipped.** User's colleague's real-world Jev guidance (relayed mid-investigation) pointed directly at payload size — `apply_jev` was sending every candidate's *full*, untruncated snippet (`SourceLoader::snippet` has no length cap; easily 500-1500+ chars for a real method), so a 50-candidate batch was a much bigger single judging call than a 20-candidate one, not just "20 plus 30 more." Added `JEV_CANDIDATE_SNIPPET_CHARS = 300` truncation in `apply_jev` (UTF-8-safe `.chars().take(...)`, 1 new regression test against a real temp-file symbol). Re-tested `top_n=50` with truncation: **36/40 → 38/40, identical across 3 runs** — `kc-sg-07`, `kc-sg-08`, `kc-sg-12` all flipped to wins (more than the earlier rank-based diagnosis predicted — those hand-approximated FTS5 probe terms weren't the real `tokenize_query()` output), `kc-sg-06` (rank ~1279) correctly stayed unreachable, and one new single-task loss (`kc-sg-15`) was traded for 3 gains. **Also ran the lexical-suite regression check that was flagged as not-yet-done in the first Phase 8 benchmark**: 40/40 with Jev on, unaffected, reproduced twice — closing the one explicitly-open gap from that earlier report. **Shipped**: `jev.top_n`'s default raised 20→50 for real this time, backed by a measured, reproduced, dual-corpus result rather than a hopeful guess. Full arc, each step measured not assumed: 33/40 (no Jev) → 36/40 (Jev, working) → 34/40 (Jev, naive top_n=50, reverted) → **38/40** (Jev, top_n=50 + truncated snippets, shipped). Full account: `bench-reports/plan20-phase8-jev-keycloak-benchmark-2026-09-25.md`. Full workspace re-verified clean (build/clippy/`cargo test --workspace`, 36 result blocks, zero failures).
  - **Fourth follow-up, same session: investigated a one-candidate-per-call rewrite per TypeSafe's own docs, benchmarked, reverted on user's call.** Reading `docs.typesafe.ai/llms.txt` in full (not just the API reference) surfaced a real mismatch: the shipped design batches all N candidates into one shared `state` with N `"noul"` questions in one call, but TypeSafe's own reranking cookbook validates one-candidate-per-call fired concurrently (their own example: 12-worker pool, 1,200 calls), motivated by the model's documented "irrelevant state detail" weakness. Rewrote `JevJudge::judge_relevance` accordingly (`tokio::task::JoinSet` + `Semaphore`, 12 concurrent). **Measured: identical 38/40 keycloak (same 2 misses), identical 40/40 lexical — no accuracy change — but latency rose from ~850-880ms to ~2.08s per query** (50 individual HTTP round-trips vs. one batched call). Presented as a genuine tradeoff (architectural correctness + a latent-risk hedge for larger future `top_n` vs. real latency cost) — **user chose to keep the faster shipped design**; the rewrite was reverted (`git checkout -- crates/codegraph-decision-engine/src/jev.rs`), not merged. Full account, including the doc-reading rationale: `bench-reports/plan20-phase8-jev-keycloak-benchmark-2026-09-25.md`'s fourth follow-up section. Worth reconsidering only if `jev.top_n` is raised well past 50 with an actual benchmark showing the batched design degrading — not without one.
  - Full workspace verified clean after every step: `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo test --workspace` (zero failures), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (zero warnings).
- [x] Open decision resolved: new default `beta`/`gamma` = 0.667/0.333 (carried over today's renormalized values, as recommended)
- [x] Open decision resolved 2026-09-25: re-swept the direct-hit `graph_credit` damping (`DIRECT_HIT_GRAPH_CREDIT_DAMPING`, now a named constant — was an unnamed `0.5` literal) now that it's unconditionally active, not gated. Swept 0/0.25/0.5/0.75/1.0, dual-corpus (lexical + keycloak, Jev off), scored on pass@5 *and* summed MRR over top-30 (pass count alone is too coarse — the lexical suite saturates near 36-40/40 across several values and can't show movement), 2 reproduced runs per value. **Result: monotonically increasing on both corpora, not a plateau centered on 0.5** — keycloak MRR: 7.65 (0.0) → 8.90 (0.25) → 10.54 (0.5) → 14.28 (0.75) → 14.82 (1.0); lexical MRR: 15.94 → 25.40 → 28.03 → 29.60 → 29.60 (same pattern). 0.75 and 1.0 form a genuine plateau and both beat 0.5 outright (at least as good on pass count, strictly better summed MRR, both corpora) — **shipped at 0.75**, the smaller value in that plateau, the same precedent `TEST_FILE_PENALTY` set landing at 0.15 inside its own 0.15-0.25 plateau. `kc-sg-02` was not used to pick the value (per the sweep's own ground rule) and its outcome was not tracked task-by-task in this pass. A real methodology gap was found and fixed mid-sweep: `codegraph status`'s `state: "fresh"` can report before `watch_status`'s `semantic_pending` (the real FTS-flush backlog) reaches 0 on a large corpus like keycloak — one early measurement (contaminated, not reproducible) was caught by this, and every measurement kept in the final table was re-verified against `semantic_pending: 0`, not `state` alone. Full account and the complete sweep table: `bench-reports/plan20-post-removal-regression-check-2026-09-25.md`'s "Item 2" section. Full workspace re-verified clean after shipping (`cargo build --workspace`, `cargo test --workspace` zero failures, `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` zero warnings).
- [x] Open decision resolved: `EmbeddedChunk` renamed to `IndexedChunk`; `StoreCoordinator` kept (now a thin single-arg wrapper — `codegraph-watcher` still calls through `coordinator.graph.*` directly for most operations, `StoreCoordinator::index_file`/`delete_file` remain for single-file callers)
- [x] **Real bug found and fixed along the way, not part of the plan's original scope**: `chunk_fts` (SQLite FTS5 virtual table) had no real foreign-key cascade from `symbols` — `GraphDb::delete_file()` only ever did `DELETE FROM files` (relying on `ON DELETE CASCADE` for `symbols`/`edges`, which FTS5 virtual tables don't participate in), so a deleted file's chunk text lived in the FTS5 index forever. Harmless-ish pre-removal (LanceDB's own `delete_by_file` at least cleaned up the vector side, and `enrich()` silently skips a stale FTS hit whose `symbol_by_id` lookup now returns `None`), but a real, growing leak now that FTS5 is the sole retrieval channel and the only cost of the leak is now permanent disk growth plus corpus-statistics pollution (`avg_doc_len` in live BM25). Fixed: `delete_file()` now deletes matching `chunk_fts` rows by rowid (`== symbol_id`) in the same transaction as the file delete. New regression test `delete_file_also_removes_fts_rows` in `codegraph-store/tests/graph_db.rs`.
- [x] **Full verification**: `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo test --workspace` (100% pass, zero failures across every crate — parser/store/watcher/mcp/api/ui/scip/core/benchmarks), and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (zero warnings) all clean as of 2026-09-25. `cargo build --release --bin codegraph` also clean.
- [x] Implementation split across parallel work: this session handled `codegraph-core`/`codegraph-store`/`codegraph-watcher` (incl. all their test suites) and the full docs pass directly; three forked agents handled `benchmarks/` (4797-line crate, oracle-vector-search → oracle-lexical-search translation for MRR/comparison benchmarks, an honesty pass on prose implying real embedding-based "understanding"), `codegraph-mcp`+`codegraph-api`+`codegraph-ui`, and `src/main.rs`'s CLI surface, respectively — each independently verified (build+clippy+test clean) before being trusted, then re-verified together as a whole workspace.

| 16 | Lexical Fallback Retrieval Hardening | done, re-run ten times total 2026-08-07 — see `bench-reports/lexical-semantic-vs-no-semantic-2026-08-07.md` (final) for the full arc. No-semantic mode: 55%→55%→55%→75%→**80%** (truncated eval, 22/40→32/40) / **92.5%** (full-text ceiling, 37/40) once stemming, synonym expansion, personalized-PageRank graph-centrality, and a `tokenize_query`/`tokenize_name` tokenizer-unification fix all landed. Semantic mode: 85%→90%→95%→92.5%→92.5% (stable). A follow-up "specificity re-scoring" lever was implemented, measured to regress the full benchmark (80%→72.5%) despite fixing its targeted anecdote, and reverted — confirmed current numbers are unchanged from before that attempt. One precisely-diagnosed hard case remains open (`readEditorState`), documented as a real limitation, not silently dropped. **2026-08-17**: investigated fixing it via a rerank-stage tiebreak, found it structurally impossible (candidate excluded before rerank runs), fixed two unrelated stopword-leak bugs found along the way (benchmark-neutral, no-semantic 32/40 identical 8 misses, semantic 37/40 identical misses) — but the investigation's bigger finding was that `readEditorState`'s prior rank numbers (incl. this row's own "#157") were computed against the full question text, not what the eval harness actually sends: `llm-task-eval.py` silently truncated every query to `question[:120]` chars, and for this specific question that sliced the identifier in half by coincidence of phrasing. **Fixed same day on explicit sign-off** — removed the truncation (`scripts/llm-task-eval.py`, both `--server` and `--project-dir` paths now use the full question). Result: no-semantic **80.0%→95.0%** (32/40→38/40, misses dropped from 8 to 2), semantic **92.5%→100%** (37/40→40/40). `readEditorState` and a second, independently-discovered truncation casualty (`onCompositionStart`) are both now hits. A `bm25` column-weight rebalance tested during the investigation showed a promising single-run result but was deliberately NOT shipped — its weighting was picked from a pre-screen that used the since-fixed truncated queries and was never re-validated. **2026-08-18**: a multi-agent workflow (Fable) diagnosed the 2 remaining no-semantic misses with hard evidence, then independently implemented and verified 4 candidate fixes (each in an isolated worktree, each validated against the real full 40-task benchmark). `createTextNode` was a genuine, narrow, deterministic miss (score gap of 0.0045 from the cutoff — caller-side-only graph neighbor gets zero credit from `personalized_pagerank`'s callees_of-only walk); fixed by bumping `RerankConfig::name_match_bonus` 0.15→0.22, verified across 3 repeated full-benchmark runs (40/40 every time) and stable at a neighboring value (0.20) — zero regressions, **shipped**. A graph-score caller-credit candidate also fixed it but regressed 6 other tasks (33/40→28/40) — the same class of failure as the Plan-16-reverted fix — **not shipped**. `getActiveEditorState` turned out NOT to be a semantic-gap case at all: it's a genuine, diagnosed, currently-**unresolved** pipeline nondeterminism bug — repeating the identical query 20× against an unchanged index produced 16 hits/4 misses, because the stub embedder makes `vector_search`'s top-k selection unstable over a fully-tied all-zero-vector corpus, and `expand_graph`'s order-dependent 100-candidate cap (already documented as order-dependent) genuinely binds for this query, so which graph neighbors get admitted — and whether a loosely-related hub function outranks the correct answer — varies by chance. Two candidate fixes (skip vector-search seeding when degraded; process lexical seeds before vector seeds when degraded) were each tried: the first made results fully deterministic but broke `createTextNode` (which needs a vector-seeded neighbor to enter the pool at all) and still didn't fix the target; the second was a clean null result. Neither shipped — left open as a real, unresolved bug (not a semantic-gap limitation) for future work. **Final verified state (own build): no-semantic 38/40→39/40 (95.0%→97.5%), semantic unaffected at 40/40 (100%).** **2026-08-18 (second follow-up):** per-candidate instrumentation (temporary, reverted) found the target is never absent from the pool — only the pool *size* varies (103/106/128 across 20 calls), jittering every score via live-BM25's `avg_len`. A Fable workflow re-tested `drop-vector-seed-when-degraded` alone (fully deterministic but landed on the miss side, 0/20, unchanged 39/40 — not shipped) vs. the same fix plus a damped, `embedding_degraded`-gated graph-score credit for direct hits (20/20 hit at rank 4, margin ≈0.043 vs. the old ≈0.0105; 40/40 × 3 no-semantic; 40/40 semantic, unaffected by construction). Independently re-verified on the real working tree (20/20, 40/40 × 3, 40/40 semantic) and swept the 0.5 coefficient against 0.25/0.75 on the same settled index (binary-swap) — all three land in a stable region (rank 5/4/3, all 40/40), not a knife-edge fit. **No-semantic is now at parity with semantic: 40/40 (100%) both modes.** Full account, including the worktree/uncommitted-changes coordination issue several agents caught and worked around, in `bench-reports/lexical-readeditorstate-tiebreak-investigation-2026-08-17.md`. **2026-08-20 (cross-repo generalization):** pointed the same 40/40 binary at keycloak (Java, 79,760 symbols, zero keycloak-specific tuning) — measured 32/40 no-semantic (the honest generalization gap), 40/40 semantic (unaffected). Diagnosed the 8 misses: 6 have no textual bridge in the source (unfixable model-free); 2 do. A 3-candidate Fable workflow tested a relative score floor and test-file de-prioritization (both borrowed from a competitor tool's validated techniques) against **both** lexical (regression gate) and keycloak (improvement target) — the first fix validated on a second repo before shipping. A naive floor implementation regressed keycloak (32→29, not shipped); a disciplined floor implementation was a pure no-op on independent re-verification against the correct baseline (the agent's self-reported "+3" did not reproduce — a second instance of this failure mode); combining floor+penalty and re-tuning regressed to 26/40. The test-file penalty alone (`TEST_FILE_PENALTY=0.15`, its own implementing agent validated 0.10/0.25 neighbors before dying to an environment interruption — diff recovered, independently rebuilt and re-verified from scratch) is a clean **+2, zero regressions**: keycloak no-semantic 32/40→**34/40** (3 identical trials), lexical/keycloak-semantic/determinism all unaffected. **Shipped.** The remaining recall-stage miss (`kc-sg-12`, never enters the candidate pool) stays open — the floor mechanism that targets it works as designed but doesn't clear the top-5 window on its own, and pushing harder costs more than it gains. Full account in `bench-reports/keycloak-cross-repo-generalization-2026-08-20.md` |
| 20 | Remove Vector Search | Phases 1-7 done, verified 2026-09-25 (workspace build/test/clippy all clean, ~77MB release binary); Phase 7 closed — lexical 40/40 exact baseline, keycloak 33/40 **confirmed via differential test to be the true pre-Plan-20 score, not a Plan-20 regression** (34→33 predates Plan 20, bisected to commit `a236957` for the `kc-sg-02` task specifically — see `bench-reports/plan20-post-removal-regression-check-2026-09-25.md`); Phase 8 (optional Jev cloud-judge rerank signal) done 2026-09-25 — `codegraph-decision-engine` crate (renamed mid-build from `codegraph-jev` on user request, to hold multiple future provider adapters). Full arc, each number measured: 33/40 (no Jev) → 36/40 (Jev, first working config) → 34/40 (Jev, naive `top_n` 20→50 widening, regressed, reverted) → **38/40** (Jev, `top_n=50` + candidate-snippet truncation fixing the regression's real cause — shipped as the new default), with the lexical suite confirmed unaffected (40/40 with Jev on) — see `bench-reports/plan20-phase8-jev-keycloak-benchmark-2026-09-25.md`, `plans/20-remove-vector-search.md`, and this file's own "Plan 20" section above. **Post-Phase-8 follow-up, same day**: the direct-hit `graph_credit` damping open decision resolved — dual-corpus sweep found 0.5 was under-tuned (monotonic improvement through 0.75/1.0 on both corpora), shipped at **0.75** (`DIRECT_HIT_GRAPH_CREDIT_DAMPING` in `pipeline.rs`). **Note: the 33/40 (no Jev) / 38/40 (Jev) keycloak figures above were measured under the old 0.5 damping and are now stale relative to this change** — the fresh re-run against 0.75 flagged as a natural next step here was done the same day (user-requested clean-slate re-baseline, only `keycloak`+`erpnext` registered, all other projects' indexed data deleted): **keycloak 35/40 (87.5%, Jev off)**, up from 33/40 at the old damping — and a first-ever current-HEAD baseline for **erpnext 23/90 (25.6%)** on the 90-task suite, not directly comparable to three different older erpnext_90 figures (10/90, 19/90, 26/90) each measured under a materially different pipeline state (pre-Plan-20, different retrieval mode, or a since-deleted comparison project) — see `bench-reports/post-plan20-graph-credit-075-baseline-2026-09-25.md` for the full breakdown and the non-comparability reasoning. **Jev-enabled re-run at 0.75, done same day**: keycloak **38/40 (95%)**, exact same 2 misses (`kc-sg-06`, `kc-sg-15`) as the original Phase 8 benchmark under the old 0.5 damping — the new damping value raised the no-Jev floor (33→35/40) without changing which tasks Jev itself can/can't rescue. |
| 21 | Retrieval/Indexing Audit Fixes | in progress — user requested an independent Opus 5.5 code-level audit of the Jev-off pipeline (2026-09-26): retrieval, indexing, and the 7 MCP tools. Full plan at `plans/21-retrieval-audit-fixes.md`. **Headline finding: the benchmark metric itself has been overstating quality** — `EVAL_BACKEND=string` counts a substring-in-context hit, not ranking; keycloak's reported 35/40 is a strict pass@5 of 24/40 with mean MRR ≈0.36, below the CLAUDE.md MRR≥0.50 ship gate, which was never checked this way. Real code-verified bugs found upstream of rerank tuning: ~36% of keycloak Java classes misfiled as `Function` (constructor/class id collision), call edges credited to the wrong enclosing symbol for several languages, cross-file name resolution creates false hubs (68% of ambiguous keycloak definitions get zero call edges; one `getId()` overload absorbs 4,270 misrouted calls), SCIP never actually run on either benchmark corpus, live-edit leaves orphaned FTS rows and silently re-routes other files' call edges, body/snippet text isn't tokenized the same way queries are (`chunk_text:"creat"` matches 11 rows vs. `chunk_text:"create"` matching 5,778), and `find_implementations` returns 0 real implementations for `RealmModel` (top 15 all `References`-sourced noise). 16 items across 3 tiers (cheap correctness fixes → structural fixes incl. a `qualified_name` format change per originally-unimplemented Plan 02 §8 → retrieval experiments needing the full multi-run/three-corpus validation bar). **Tier 1 (8 items) done 2026-09-26**: added a strict rank-based scorer (`scripts/strict-rank-eval.py`, pass@1/pass@5/true MRR on the real `symbol` field, not substring-in-context) and re-measured the CLAUDE.md ship gate honestly — all three corpora (keycloak MRR 0.336, erpnext 0.093, lexical 0.341) sit well below the 0.50 gate, corrected in CLAUDE.md rather than left pointing at the disproven old figures. Fixed: call-edge attribution now picks the narrowest containing span, not the first found (`extractor.rs`, regression-tested); `resolve_edges` now requires same-language candidates before its in-degree tie-break (regression-tested); `find_implementations` drops `References`-sourced noise and now walks `Inherits`/`Implements` transitively via a recursive CTE (verified live: keycloak's `RealmModel` went from 0 real implementors to 7, including a genuinely transitive one — 3 new regression tests including a cycle-safety check); both FTS5 upsert paths (live-watcher's `delete_symbols_for_file`, the parallel full-index `upsert_batch`) now clean up `chunk_fts` rows before re-inserting a file's symbols, closing the same class of orphaned-row leak `delete_file` was already fixed for (regression-tested); `lang_filter` is now actually wired into `RetrievalPipeline::query` instead of being silently dead (regression-tested); `explain_symbol`/`find_relevant_code` cap callers/callees output at 10 with an honest count + truncation flag; `trace_impact`'s tool description no longer overclaims completeness (softened to note unresolved edges are invisible to it); CLAUDE.md's "expand_hops ≥ 2" claim corrected (confirmed via SQL-level read: `neighbors()` is 1-hop only, the constant only changes score decay) and its JS/TS string-keyed-callback claim corrected (the pass exists and fires correctly — measured 0 edges on all 3 corpora because none of them use the `.emit()` half of the pattern, confirmed by direct grep against `lexical`, not because the pass is broken). **Aggregate strict-scorer effect: roughly a wash** (keycloak pass@5 23→22/40, erpnext 15→14/90, lexical 17/40 unchanged but MRR 0.34→0.30) — expected and explicitly anticipated by the plan itself, since `rerank()`'s constants are still tuned against the old, buggier graph; Tier 1's real value is the directly-verified per-tool correctness above, not aggregate movement, which is Tier 3's explicit job once Tier 2 also lands. Full workspace clean throughout (`cargo build/test/clippy --workspace`, 7 new regression tests added). Full account: `plans/21-retrieval-audit-fixes.md`. **Tier 2 items 9+10 done 2026-09-27** (item 11/SCIP deferred — see below): `qualified_name` format changed to `"{path}::{EnclosingType}::{name}"` with stable `#N` overload disambiguation (original Plan 02 §8 design, never implemented until now) — fixes the constructor/class id-collision bug directly (sanity-checked live: keycloak's `DefaultKeycloakSession` now has both a real `Class` symbol and a separate constructor `Function` symbol). `resolve_edges` rewritten to be import-aware: exactly-one-candidate resolves directly, multiple candidates check the calling file's `Imports` edges (extracted since Plan 12 A3, never consulted before this) before falling back, and a still-ambiguous name with no import signal now stays **unresolved** rather than guessing by in-degree (deliberate precision-over-recall trade, regression-tested both directions: the old in-degree-guess test was corrected to assert the new stays-unresolved behavior, and a new test proves an import signal wins over a deliberately-higher-in-degree decoy candidate). Also fixed: FTS/symbol deletion on file edit now only removes symbols actually gone from the edit instead of delete-then-reinsert-everything, which was cascading `ON DELETE SET NULL` onto *other files'* edges and letting `resolve_edges` silently re-route an unrelated file's already-correct caller on every unrelated save. Three background-agent attempts at this tier stalled on a 600s no-progress watchdog (likely a long-blocking command with no intermediate output); the third's in-progress work was salvaged and finished directly rather than risking a fourth stall. Full fresh re-index required (item 9 changes every `SymbolId`) — symbol counts rose across all three corpora (keycloak +10.6%, erpnext +5.1%, lexical +10.5%), consistent with previously-collided symbols now counted separately. **Strict-scorer result vs. the true pre-Tier-1 baseline**: keycloak pass@5 23→**24**/40 (MRR 0.336→**0.362**), erpnext pass@5 15→**16**/90 (MRR 0.093→**0.111**), lexical pass@5 17→16/40 (MRR 0.341→0.296, a small single-run dip flagged for Tier 3, not yet chased down — plausibly because JS/TS doesn't have the constructor-collision problem item 9 targets). Lenient eval: keycloak/lexical unchanged, erpnext **23→29/90 improved**. Full workspace clean (build/test/clippy, zero failures). Full account: `bench-reports/plan21-tier2-qualified-name-and-resolution-2026-09-27.md`. **Item 11 (SCIP) attempted 2026-09-27**: real `.scip` files generated for both corpora (erpnext 54MB via `scip-python`, keycloak 305MB via `scip-java` after a ~28min cold Maven build). Found and fixed two independent copies of the same PATH-joining bug (`src/main.rs`'s CLI `extended_scip_path()` and `codegraph-watcher`'s daemon-side `extended_path()` — both pushed the whole already-`:`-joined `$PATH` as one element into `join_paths`, which fails on any real multi-entry PATH, silently swallowed into an *empty* PATH by `.unwrap_or_default()`, so `which_in` could never find the indexer binaries despite `codegraph scip detect`'s separate `.exists()`-based check correctly reporting them present). **Live ingestion into the running graph not confirmed** — ground-truth-checked via direct DB query (zero `Semantic` edges, no `scip_ingested_at` meta). Initially suspected daemon-scheduling/hard-kill interruption, but a follow-up isolation test (calling `merge_scip` directly against a copy of erpnext's real DB + its real 54MB `.scip` file, in a standalone `cargo test`, fully outside the daemon) found **it did not complete within 25+ minutes even with a dedicated CPU core and zero other daemon activity** — meaning `merge_scip`/`merge_index` itself likely has a real, independent performance problem on realistic file sizes, not (or not only) a daemon-scheduling issue. Keycloak's file is ~5.6x larger; if the cost scales proportionally, its merge would plausibly take multiple hours. Flagged as the real next thing to profile (`codegraph_scip::merger::merge_index`'s per-symbol/per-occurrence DB write pattern and transaction batching) before touching the daemon side further. Also found, not fixed: SCIP auto-run always re-executes the external indexer before checking for an existing valid file to ingest (check order should be reversed). **Tier 3 items 12-15 done 2026-09-27, item 16 partial**: body/snippet text now tokenized the same way as queries (shared `tokenize_name`, migration `008_fts_stemmed_body.sql`) and the live-BM25 reranker's doc-side tokenization fixed to match; `split_words` gained acronym-run and digit-to-uppercase boundary rules (migration `009_fts_acronym_digit_tokenization.sql`, 2 new pinned-stem regression tests); synonym-expanded FTS matches down-weighted 0.4x relative to original query terms (`SYNONYM_TERM_WEIGHT`, not independently swept, provisional like the damping constant was); `expand_graph`'s candidate admission is now round-robin across seeds instead of exhausting one high-fan-in seed's neighbors first. **Real measured result: erpnext pass@5 16→28/90 (MRR 0.111→0.172, nearly doubled)**, lexical pass@5 unchanged 16/40 (MRR 0.296→0.281, small dip not chased down. Item 16 (joint β/γ/damping sweep) scoped down after an automated sweep script caused real process chaos (concurrent daemons on one port, a background-task lifetime limit silently killing an in-progress run, and one cleanup `git checkout` that briefly discarded items 12-15's own uncommitted work — caught and fully reconstructed from context, re-verified clean, no data lost) — replaced with a single hand-run comparison (β=0.5/γ=0.5 vs shipped 0.667/0.333): small consistent MRR gain on both corpora, **not shipped**, a 2-point comparison doesn't meet this project's validation bar. Full workspace clean throughout (build/test/clippy, zero failures, including the previously-flaky `rapid_saves_p95_latency_and_no_duplicates` confirmed load-induced not a regression). Full account: `bench-reports/plan21-tier3-and-scip-2026-09-27.md`. **Item 11 (SCIP) — FULLY WORKING as of later the same day, full account in `bench-reports/plan21-scip-fully-working-2026-09-27.md`.** The "merge_scip performance problem" flagged above was root-caused precisely: `merger::merge_index` resolved each SCIP *occurrence* (not document) via an unindexable leading-wildcard `files.path LIKE '%' || relative_path` scan — genuine O(occurrences × files). Fixed by resolving `file_id` once per *document* via the indexed exact-match `file_by_path`, with `find_symbol_at`/`enrich_symbol_from_scip` now taking `file_id` directly. **Measured: 25+ minutes (incomplete) → 32.2 seconds** for the same real 54MB file. A second real bug (auto_run always re-running the external indexer before checking for an existing valid file — caught live re-triggering keycloak's ~28min Maven build a second time) was also fixed, gating auto_run on the `.scip` file's mere existence rather than the ambiguous `should_ingest_scip` check (first attempt at this fix conflated "nothing new" with "no file," still had to be corrected). A third issue (`detect_project_language` misdetects erpnext, Python with a root `package.json`, as TypeScript) was found but not fixed — worked around via `[scip] auto_run = false` per-project (plus a config-schema gotcha: `ScipConfig::scip_path` has no serde default, so a partial `[scip]` override needs it spelled out explicitly). **End-to-end verified live on all three corpora**: keycloak 153,938 symbols enriched, erpnext 35,371, lexical 10,357 — real, confirmed `Semantic` edges. **Retrieval-quality impact, real and corpus-dependent**: keycloak strict pass@5 24→**32/40 (80%)**, MRR 0.362→**0.557** — the first result in this project's history to clear the CLAUDE.md MRR≥0.50 ship gate. Erpnext dipped slightly (28→25/90, reproduced twice, not noise); lexical mixed (16→17/40 pass@5, MRR 0.281→0.236) — not chased down at the per-task level, flagged as a real open question (should `Semantic` edges be weighted differently in `rerank()`?). Full workspace clean throughout every fix (build/test/clippy, zero failures). |

| 35 | Python dotted-path string dispatch edges | implemented 2026-11-03 (`py_string_dispatch`, 2 unit tests, clippy clean); 26 synthesized edges on ERPNext; agent benchmark 30q x 3: 69.0% vs 69.6% before (tie), kept as a correctness fix — see `plans/35-python-string-dispatch.md` |

| 37 | Loss audit + edge resolution tag | implemented 2026-11-09 (migration 018, 2 unit tests); audit: no lost fact is a missing edge; two 1-repeat runs 68.7% and 69.1% vs baseline 71.6% and 70.1% (CIs span 0) — see `plans/37-edge-resolution-tag-and-loss-audit.md` |

### End-to-end smoke test (2026-05-12, gap-review pass)

```
full_index (codegraph project itself):
  43 files found and parsed
  294 symbols extracted (Rust)
  LanceDB embeddings upserted (zero vectors — model not downloaded)
  /health                   → 200  {"ready":true,"state":"fresh","total_files":43,"total_symbols":294}
  tools/list                → 5 tools returned correctly
  find_relevant_code("embedding model loading", top_n=3) → 3 ranked results
  /api/status               → {"state":"fresh","total_files":43,"total_symbols":294}
  /api/languages            → [{"language":"rust","file_count":43}]
  /api/graph?limit=5        → 5 symbols, 3 edges
  /api/search?q=embedding   → 3 ranked results with symbol + file + snippet
  Graceful shutdown         → SIGTERM received, "shutdown complete"
```

## Plan 14 — Competitive Hardening
*Motivation: a full audit against Tool A, a graph+lexical competing tool with no vector search, identified concrete production-hardening gaps unrelated to retrieval quality.*
*Implemented 2026-08-03*

Every item below was implemented and verified (`cargo test --workspace` and
`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean after each
change, not just at the end) before being checked off.

### Initiative 1 — Parallelize extraction/indexing
- [x] `Extractor::extract_many()` — rayon fan-out, fresh `tree_sitter::Parser` per call (no shared-parser contention across threads parsing the same language)
- [x] `GraphDb::upsert_batch()` / `GraphDb::all_file_checksums()` — chunked (200-file) transactional batch writes instead of one commit per file per table
- [x] `full_index_graph` rewritten: bulk checksum-filter → parallel parse → chunked batched SQLite write → `resolve_edges()`
- [x] Live watcher loop (`run()`) and `flush_pending()` also batch `flush_embed_batch()` once per debounced/drained path set, instead of once per file (`index_path`, kept as a still-valid single-file API, is no longer called by either)
- [x] Measured: **~6.1× wall-clock speedup** on a synthetic 500–5,000-file corpus (`bench-reports/parallel-index-synthetic-2026-08-03.md`); real large-repo number not obtained (Initiative 7)
- [x] Tests: `crates/codegraph-parser/tests/parallel_extraction.rs` (4), plus existing suites re-verified after the `worker.rs` rewrite

### Initiative 2 — Incremental re-sync hardening
- [x] Corrected `CLAUDE.md`'s stale "known gaps" claims — queue-overflow → `full_index()` and `flush_pending()` on shutdown were already implemented; the real remaining gap is `extract_incremental()` being unused (watcher always full-reparses)
- [x] Implemented all 5 previously-`[ ]` Plan 07 watcher tests (create/modify/delete/ignored-path/rapid-saves) in `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/incremental_sync.rs`
- [x] Root-caused and fixed a real test-harness bug found along the way (not a production bug): un-canonicalized tempdir roots don't match the symlink-resolved paths macOS FSEvents reports, causing every path-keyed DB lookup to silently miss

### Initiative 3 — Language coverage expansion
- [x] Kotlin re-enabled via `tree-sitter-kotlin-ng` (maintained fork, ABI-compatible with our `tree-sitter = "0.24"`); all 4 previously-`#[ignore]`d Kotlin tests now pass un-ignored, plus 1 new test
- [x] Lua added via `tree-sitter-lua` pinned to `=0.2.0` (the newer 0.5.0 generates an incompatible ABI 15); 3 new tests including a `require(...)` import-edge check
- [x] **Found and fixed a real, independent bug**: `codegraph-core::config::default_languages()` only listed 5 of the (then) 13 registered languages, and a separate hand-maintained `languages_to_extensions()` match in `codegraph-watcher` had drifted from the parser's real extensions for csharp/ruby/kotlin (mapping to the literal language name instead of `cs`/`rb`/`kt`) — meaning most supported languages were never actually watched by default even before this session's language additions. Fixed by deriving `languages_to_extensions()` directly from `LanguageRegistry::for_language(...).file_extensions()` (added `LanguageRegistry::registered_languages()` for this) instead of a second hand-maintained list, and syncing `default_languages()` to cover every registered language — both changes covered by `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/language_config_sync.rs` and a `watcher.rs` unit test so this can't silently drift again
- [ ] Dart, Vue/Svelte SFC delegation — not done (out of scope for this session)

### Initiative 4 — Dynamic-dispatch synthesis layer
- [x] `EdgeSource::Synthesized` variant added (exhaustive `as_str()`/`FromStr` — no silent catch-all that could downgrade provenance)
- [x] `codegraph_parser::synthesis::c_fnptr` — heuristic C/C++ function-pointer dispatch-table edge recovery (designated **and** positional initializers); additive post-pass wired into `full_index_graph` after `resolve_edges()`, gated by `[watcher] synthesis_enabled` (default on)
- [x] Tests: 4 unit tests in `c_fnptr.rs` (including the exact fixture from the original success criterion) + 3 end-to-end tests in `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/dispatch_synthesis.rs` (2 originally, +1 from the code review below) proving it's actually wired into `full_index_graph`, not just unit-tested in isolation
- [ ] String-keyed JS/TS callback (`EventEmitter`-style) pattern — not done (C fn-ptr only)
- **Code review (code-reviewer agent, 2026-08-03)** on Initiatives 1–6 found and fixed:
  - [x] **Correctness (was silent data corruption)**: `run_dispatch_synthesis` called `upsert_edges` without first clearing previously-synthesized edges. `edges` has no unique constraint beyond its own row id, so `INSERT OR REPLACE` never actually replaced a matching row — every full-index run (including every normal server restart, since `full_index_graph` runs on startup) appended duplicate synthesized edges instead of refreshing them, permanently inflating `in_degree` (and therefore rerank score, β term) for repeatedly-detected dispatch targets. Fixed by adding `GraphDb::delete_synthesized_edges()` (mirroring the existing `delete_semantic_edges()` precedent from the SCIP layer) and calling it unconditionally at the start of `run_dispatch_synthesis`, before recomputing. Regression test added: `repeated_full_index_does_not_duplicate_synthesized_edges` (runs `full_index_graph` 3× on an unchanged repo, asserts exactly 1 matching edge, not 3).
  - [x] **Memory regression against Initiative 5's own goal**: `full_index_graph_blocking` read every changed file's full source bytes into one `Vec` and ran `extract_many` once for the *entire* corpus before any SQLite write — meaning peak memory scaled with total corpus size (source bytes held twice: once in the read buffer, once inside each `FileExtraction`), regardless of how small `[watcher] memory_budget_mb` was set. This is exactly the failure mode `memory_budget.rs`'s own doc comment says it exists to avoid. Fixed by restructuring to read+parse+write in bounded `FULL_INDEX_CHUNK_SIZE` (200-file) chunks — the scoped rayon pool is built once and reused across chunks via `pool.install(...)`, so parallelism within a chunk is unaffected, but at most one chunk's worth of source bytes/extractions is resident at a time instead of the whole corpus. Sanity-checked post-fix: 1500 synthetic files indexed via the full production `full_index_graph` path in ~6.3s (release build) — no regression from the earlier ~6.1× synthetic-corpus speedup measurement (which exercises `extract_many`/`upsert_batch` directly, not this chunking wrapper).
  - [x] **Consistency**: `flush_pending()` (shutdown drain) built embed chunks without first calling `resolve_edges()`, unlike `run()`'s live loop (which the same session's rewrite added `resolve_edges()` to) — meaning files drained at shutdown could get chunk context built from stale cross-file edges. Fixed by adding the same `resolve_edges()` call before `flush_embed_batch` in `flush_pending()`.
  - [x] **Doc hygiene**: a stale doc comment for `symbols_in_files` was left attached to the newly-inserted `symbols_by_language` above it — fixed, each method has its own comment now.
  - Reviewed and accepted as pre-existing, intentionally-scoped limitations (not fixed, per the review's own recommendation): the C fn-ptr synthesizer's field-name matching is unqualified by struct/module (documented in the module's own header comment); synthesized edges for a live-edited C/C++ file go stale until the next full re-index, since the synthesis pass only runs in `full_index_graph`, not the live per-file `run()` loop.

### Initiative 5 — Low-memory / adaptive-concurrency mode
- [x] `codegraph_watcher::memory_budget` — `sysinfo`-based available-memory read, `parse_pool_size()`/`resolve_pool_size()` sizing math, `[watcher] memory_budget_mb` config override for containers `sysinfo` can't see a cgroup limit for
- [x] Wired into `full_index_graph_blocking` via a scoped `rayon::ThreadPoolBuilder`
- [x] Tests: 7 unit tests in `memory_budget.rs` + `crates/codegraph-watcher/tests/memory_constrained_index.rs` (an extreme override forcing a size-1 pool still indexes every file correctly)

### Initiative 6 — MCP flagship-tool evaluation (groundwork only)
- [x] `handle_tools_call()` logs which tool was called per query (`tracing::info!(target: "codegraph_mcp::tool_call", ...)`)
- [ ] The actual mis-pick-rate eval and any resulting 8th tool — not done; gated on running that eval first

### Initiative 7 — Real large-repo indexing benchmark
- [x] `bench_parallel_index()` / `--bench-parallel-index` — synthetic-corpus sequential-vs-parallel speedup, see Initiative 1 above and `bench-reports/parallel-index-synthetic-2026-08-03.md`
- [~] Real `apple/swift` (~27k files) / `torvalds/linux` (~70k files) runs — superseded by Plan 15's Initiative 12: attempted for real on `apple/swift`, found and fixed 2 real bugs instead of reaching a clean number; see `bench-reports/large-repo-indexing-2026-08-04.md`

### Do NOT copy (architectural choices deliberately not adopted from Tool A — unaffected by this session)
- [x] No separate background daemon process was introduced
- [x] No retreat from vector search / hybrid retrieval
- [x] No second (Node/WASM-style) extraction engine was introduced — Kotlin/Lua both added as ordinary tree-sitter grammars in the one Rust binary
- [x] No hand-rolled cooperative scheduler — parallelism uses `rayon`/`tokio` primitives directly

## Plan 15 — Competitive Hardening v2
*Motivation: a re-audit against Tool A after Plan 14 landed, prioritizing its remaining win points.*
*Implemented 2026-08-03/04*

Every item below was implemented and verified (`cargo test --workspace` and
`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean after each
change) before being checked off, following the same process as Plan 14.

### Initiative 8 — Framework-aware SFC delegation (Vue + Svelte)
- [x] `Language::Vue`/`Language::Svelte` added; `vue_lang.rs`/`svelte_lang.rs` thin `LanguageSupport` stubs (never parsed directly — delegation only)
- [x] `Extractor::try_sfc_delegate()` regex-slices the first `<script>` block out of a `.vue`/`.svelte` file, delegates to the existing TS/JS `LanguageSupport`, offsets resulting symbol spans (byte + line) back into the original file's coordinates — deliberately tagged with the delegate language (TypeScript/JavaScript), not Vue/Svelte, so downstream passes (docstrings, Initiative 9's JS-callback synthesis) work with zero changes
- [x] `default_languages()` extended to include `vue`/`svelte`
- [x] Tests: `crates/codegraph-parser/tests/sfc_delegation.rs` (5 tests) — span-offset correctness, cross-function call resolution, no-script-block graceful handling, Svelte coverage, default-watch coverage
- [ ] Rare dual `<script>` + `<script setup>` Vue pattern — only the first block is delegated, documented known limitation

### Initiative 9 — Dynamic-dispatch synthesis: JS/TS callback pattern
- [x] `codegraph_parser::synthesis::js_callback` — heuristic `.on`/`.addEventListener` registration → `.emit` call-site edge recovery, mirroring `c_fnptr.rs`'s structure
- [x] Wired into `run_dispatch_synthesis` alongside the existing C fn-ptr pass
- [x] Tests: 4 unit tests + `full_index_synthesizes_js_callback_edges` end-to-end watcher test

### Initiative 10 — cgroup-precise memory reads on Linux
- [x] `memory_budget::cgroup_available_bytes()` — cgroup v2 (`memory.max`/`memory.current`) then v1 (`memory.limit_in_bytes`/`memory.usage_in_bytes`) fallback; `available_bytes()` tries this before the `sysinfo` host-level read
- [x] Not `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`-gated — the `/sys/fs/cgroup` paths simply don't exist on macOS/Windows, so the read naturally no-ops there without needing a platform check (a real cfg-gating mistake was caught and reverted mid-session: it caused a genuine `dead_code` clippy failure on this macOS sandbox)
- [x] Tests: 7 new unit tests against synthetic cgroup files (limit parsing, "max"/huge-sentinel-as-unlimited, missing/garbage files, limit-minus-usage arithmetic)

### Initiative 11 — REST API staleness gap
- [x] `ApiResult<T>` now carries an HTTP status code; new `respond()` helper maps `IndexState::Stale`/`Error` → 503, else 200
- [x] All 6 general handlers (`graph`, `search`, `symbol_detail`, `file`, `languages`, `impact`) updated; `/api/status` deliberately stays 200 always (it's how a client discovers staleness in the first place)
- [x] Discovered and fixed a real test-infrastructure gap along the way: `codegraph-api` had zero existing tests — added `async-trait`/`tower` dev-deps and a `StubPipeline`/`state_with()` harness
- [x] Tests: `crates/codegraph-api/tests/staleness.rs` (7 tests)

### Initiative 12 — Real large-repo indexing benchmark
- [x] Attempted for real against `apple/swift` (26,824 eligible C/C++/Swift files) — not scoped down preemptively; ran until it hit real problems
- [x] **Found and fixed Bug 1**: `run_dispatch_synthesis` materialized every C/C++/JS/TS file's full text into one `HashMap` before synthesis, reaching ~17GB resident memory on the real corpus. Fixed by splitting `c_fnptr`/`js_callback`'s `synthesize()` into two explicit phases callable per bounded 200-file chunk (`build_field_handlers`+`scan_call_sites`, `build_event_handlers`+`scan_emissions`); 2 new cross-chunk regression tests prove handler resolution still works across chunk boundaries
- [x] **Found and fixed Bug 2**: `GraphDb::resolve_edges()`'s correlated subquery re-ran a full-table `GROUP BY` in-degree aggregation once per unresolved edge — an O(unresolved_edges × total_edges) pattern invisible at synthetic-benchmark scale (500–5,000 files) but severe at ~27k real files. Fixed by materializing the aggregation into an indexed temp table once per call; 3 new tests including a performance regression guard (3,000 symbols/6,000 edges resolves in well under 5s)
- [~] **Full corpus wall-clock number: not obtained.** After both fixes, a third run was still killed by macOS's `memorystatus` killer at ~81GB *compressed* memory (not resident — `ps -o rss=` stayed under 800MB throughout), nearly matching the original pre-fix figure. Two follow-up probes (repeated pure-parse-only passes; repeated full-pipeline passes with a fresh in-memory `GraphDb` each time) did not reproduce comparable growth at smaller scale, so the exact mechanism is not pinned down — deferred rather than continuing open-ended multi-hour investigation, per explicit user direction after being asked. Full write-up: `bench-reports/large-repo-indexing-2026-08-04.md`
- [ ] `torvalds/linux` (~70k files) — not attempted, given `apple/swift` did not reach a clean completion
- [x] Ran under `caffeinate -i` after discovering macOS idle-sleep (76 sleep/wake cycles overnight, confirmed via `pmset -g log`) was inflating wall-clock by 3-4x in earlier attempts — real CPU time vs. elapsed time was the tell
- [x] Scope deviation from the original plan documented: measured on host hardware (8-core/16GB), not the planned `--cpus=2 --memory=6g` container — building the full dependency tree fresh inside a Linux container was judged impractical this session
- **Code review (code-reviewer agent, 2026-08-04)** on the Initiative 12 fixes (Bug 1/Bug 2 above) found and fixed:
