  - [x] **Correctness (real within-run edge duplication)**: `synthesize_dispatch_edges()` (the new chunked driver replacing the single-shot `synthesize_all()` call) accumulated edges directly from `scan_call_sites`/`scan_emissions` without the `(from_id, to_id)` dedup `synthesize_all()` used to do before returning. A function with two textual call/emit sites through the same field/event name produced two identical edges, both landing as distinct rows (`upsert_edges`'s `INSERT OR REPLACE` is keyed on SQLite's own rowid, not edge identity) — inflating in-degree for repeatedly-called targets, feeding both the rerank β term and `resolve_edges()`'s new in-degree tie-breaker. Fixed by sorting + `dedup_by` on `(from_id, to_id)` before `upsert_edges`, matching `synthesize_all()`'s prior behavior. Regression test added: `two_call_sites_through_the_same_field_do_not_duplicate_the_edge`.
  - [x] **Robustness (temp-table cleanup gap)**: `resolve_edges()`'s `_edge_in_degree` temp table lives on `GraphDb`'s single long-lived connection (held behind a `Mutex` for the process's whole lifetime) — if `CREATE INDEX` ever failed after a successful `CREATE TABLE` (e.g. transient temp-store pressure, exactly the resource-constrained conditions this fix targets), every subsequent `resolve_edges()` call would fail forever with "table already exists", silently breaking cross-file edge resolution with no logged symptom (two of the three call sites discard the result). Fixed with a leading `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` (self-healing regardless of what a prior call left behind) and by logging (not propagating in place of the `UPDATE`'s own result) a failure on the trailing cleanup drop. Regression test added: `resolve_edges_can_be_called_repeatedly_without_error`.
  - [x] **Documentation**: noted a real, non-blocking semantic difference the reviewer found — the new query removes an order-dependency the old correlated-subquery version had (ambiguous edges in the same `resolve_edges()` call could no longer influence each other's in-degree tie-break based on unspecified SQLite row-iteration order) — arguably a latent-bug fix, not a new behavior change, but not byte-for-byte identical to the prior query. Documented in `resolve_edges()`'s own doc comment.

### Do NOT copy (architectural choices deliberately not adopted from Tool A — unaffected by this session)
- [x] No background daemon process was introduced
- [x] No retreat from vector search / hybrid retrieval
- [x] No second (Node/WASM-style) extraction engine — Vue/Svelte delegation reuses the existing TS/JS engine
- [x] Dart remains explicitly out of scope (ABI-incompatible tree-sitter grammar, verified empirically)
- [x] No 8th MCP tool / agent installer pursued

## Plan 16 — Lexical Fallback Retrieval Hardening
*Motivation: `bench-reports/lexical-semantic-vs-no-semantic-2026-08-05.md` measured a ~30pp retrieval-quality gap (85% semantic vs. 55% no-semantic/stubbed-embedder). Root cause: lexical/BM25 signal only ever reranked whatever `vector_search` returned, and a separate bug had the direct-hit rerank branch discarding BM25 entirely.*
*Implemented 2026-08-06, hardened through several 2026-08-07 follow-ups. Full measurement history (five rounds, including one implemented-then-reverted fix) is in `bench-reports/lexical-semantic-vs-no-semantic-2026-08-07.md` — this section tracks what changed in the codebase, not the benchmark narrative.*

- [x] `GraphStore::lexical_search(terms, top_k)` — SQLite FTS5 candidate generation (migration `003_lexical_fts.sql`, `chunk_fts` virtual table), unioned with `vector_search` in `expand_graph()` rather than only reranking its output; default `Ok(vec![])` so other/mock implementors keep compiling
- [x] Bug fixes: direct-hit rerank branch was discarding BM25 (`vec_score + name_score*bonus` → `vec_score + gamma*keyword + name_score*bonus`); `vec_score` NaN sanitized to `0.0`; nondeterministic `HashMap`-iteration tie-break replaced with an explicit `SymbolId` tiebreak; `lexical_search`'s `bm25()` sign clamped before normalizing
- [x] `EmbeddingAdapter::is_stub()` + degraded-mode rerank adaptation (`alpha`→0 renormalized onto `beta`/`gamma`, `expand_hops` widened) so a stubbed embedder's meaningless constant `vec_score` doesn't dilute the score
- [x] `IndexWorker::flush_embed_batch` writes `chunk_fts` alongside the LanceDB vector write; `watch_status` exposes `embedding_degraded`
- [x] **Backfill gap found and fixed pre-ship (advisor review)**: `chunk_fts` only populates going forward, so an already-indexed project would get zero benefit until every file was re-touched. `run_migrations` sets an `fts_backfill_needed` flag the first time a content-affecting migration lands; `codegraph_watcher::backfill_fts_index()` does a one-time catch-up pass, wired at both startup call sites (`src/main.rs`, `src/server.rs`)
- [x] `tokenize_name`/`tokenize_query` unified into one `split_words` function (camelCase, underscore, and `$`-prefix all treated as boundaries) — migrations `004`-`007` are successive content-format bumps as the tokenizer was corrected (full-qualified-name decomposition → bare-name-only → stemmed → shared with the query side); each bump reuses the same backfill mechanism as `003`
- [x] Stemming (`rust-stemmers`, Porter/Snowball) and a curated ~13-group synonym table (create/build/make…, delete/remove/destroy/clear/wipe/erase/reset…) in `tokenize_query`
- [x] `EnrichedCandidate.graph_score` — personalized PageRank seeded from the query's own direct hits, `callees_of`-only **directed** walk (deliberate: an undirected version measurably made hub-dominance worse — see the bench-report), replaces raw `in_degree` in `rerank`'s weighted branch
- [x] Tests: ~30 new tests across `codegraph-store`/`codegraph-core`/`codegraph-watcher` covering FTS5 candidate generation, the backfill path, camelCase/underscore/`$`-prefix tokenization, stemming, synonym expansion, and PPR graph-centrality (including a regression test pinning the directed-vs-undirected walk choice)
- [x] `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean after every change
- [x] **A "specificity re-scoring" lever (reward matching a higher fraction of a candidate's own tokens) was implemented, fixed its one targeted case, regressed the full 40-task benchmark at two different tuning weights, and was reverted.** The synthetic unit test for the targeted case passed throughout — only the full benchmark re-run caught the regression. Durable lesson: don't trust a reranking/candidate-generation change on its targeted anecdote alone. Full story in the bench-report.
- [x] **Result** (full per-round numbers in the bench-report): no-semantic 55%→80% (`llm-task-eval.py`) / 92.5% (untruncated full-text check); semantic 85%→92.5%
- [x] `readEditorState` — resolved by the 2026-08-17 harness-truncation fix below (the "#157" figure was itself an artifact of the truncation bug, not a real ranking problem).
- [x] **2026-08-17 follow-up investigated the rerank-stage-tiebreak idea from the bench-report's open-items list, found it structurally non-viable, fixed two real stopword-leak bugs, discovered the eval harness itself has been silently truncating every query, and — on explicit sign-off — fixed the harness** — full account in `bench-reports/lexical-readeditorstate-tiebreak-investigation-2026-08-17.md`. `readEditorState` has zero graph edges (verified directly against the live indexed graph), so it's unreachable by graph expansion and any rerank-stage change is structurally a no-op for it — that part holds. But the "raw rank #157" figure quoted in this row and the 08-07 report was computed against the **full** question text; `scripts/llm-task-eval.py` (both `--server` and `--project-dir` paths) actually truncated every query to 120 characters before sending it for retrieval, and no task in any `benchmarks/*.yaml` set `query_for_retrieval` — so this had been happening silently across every lexical benchmark run to date. For `readEditorState`'s question the 120-char cutoff fell mid-identifier ("...Which function readEd"), destroying `editor`/`state`/`callback`/`readonly` before they ever reached the pipeline. Along the way, fixed two real, separate stopword-leak bugs in `tokenize_query` (`get` leaking back in via synonym expansion since it's also a `SYNONYM_GROUPS` member; inflected stopwords like `"needs"` slipping past a pre-stem filter) — shipped, benchmark-neutral on their own. **Then fixed the harness truncation** (removed the `[:120]` slice entirely — a fixed cutoff at any length risks the same coincidental mid-identifier slicing for some future question). Full-benchmark result: no-semantic **32/40→38/40 (80.0%→95.0%)**, semantic **37/40→40/40 (92.5%→100%)**. `readEditorState` and a second, independently-discovered truncation casualty (`onCompositionStart`) are now hits. Also tested a `bm25()` column-weight rebalance (`3.0/2.0/1.0`→`8.0/1.0/0.3`) that measured a single-run net positive under the *old, truncated* harness — deliberately **not shipped**: its weighting was picked from a pre-screen using the since-fixed truncated queries and was never re-validated.
  - [x] **2026-08-18 follow-up (multi-agent, Fable):** diagnosed and closed the last 2 no-semantic misses with hard evidence, not guesses. `createTextNode` was a genuine narrow miss (0.0045 score gap from the rank-5 cutoff — discovered only as a caller-side graph neighbor, which `personalized_pagerank`'s callees_of-only walk gives zero credit) — fixed by bumping `name_match_bonus` 0.15→0.22, verified across 3 repeated full-benchmark runs (40/40 every time) and a neighboring value (0.20), zero regressions — **shipped**. A graph-score caller-credit alternative also fixed it but regressed 6 other tasks (33/40→28/40, the same class of failure as the reverted specificity-rescoring fix above) — **not shipped**. `getActiveEditorState` turned out to be a genuine, unresolved **pipeline nondeterminism bug**, not a semantic-gap limitation: repeating the identical query 20× against an unchanged index gave 16 hits/4 misses, because the stub embedder's all-zero vectors make `vector_search`'s top-k selection unstable, and `expand_graph`'s order-dependent 100-candidate cap (confirmed to bind for this query) means which graph neighbors get admitted — and whether a loosely-related hub function outranks the correct answer — varies by chance. Two fixes tried (drop vector-search seeding when degraded; process lexical seeds first when degraded): the first made results fully deterministic but broke `createTextNode` and still didn't fix the target; the second was a clean null result. Neither shipped — left open as a real bug for future work. **Final verified state: no-semantic 38/40→39/40 (95.0%→97.5%), semantic unaffected at 40/40.** Full account in `bench-reports/lexical-readeditorstate-tiebreak-investigation-2026-08-17.md`.
  - [x] **2026-08-18 second follow-up: `getActiveEditorState` resolved, no-semantic 40/40.** Per-candidate instrumentation (temporary, reverted before shipping) overturned part of the prior diagnosis: the target is *never* absent from the candidate pool (20/20 present) — what varies is the pool **size** (103/106/128 across 20 identical calls), which leaks into live-BM25's `avg_len` and jitters every score by enough to flip a ~0.0105 margin. A Fable workflow re-tested `drop-vector-seed-when-degraded` alone (now fully deterministic, but landed on the miss side — 0/20, unchanged 39/40, **not shipped**) versus the same fix plus a damped (half-weight), **`embedding_degraded`-gated** graph-score credit for direct hits (20/20 hit, rank 4, margin ≈0.043 — 4x the old gap; 40/40 × 3 no-semantic; 40/40 semantic, unaffected by construction since the gate makes the change a no-op outside degraded mode — **shipped**). Independently re-verified on the actual working tree (not just the agent's worktree): 20/20 determinism, 40/40 × 3 no-semantic, 40/40 semantic. The 0.5 half-weight coefficient was swept against 0.25/0.75 on the same already-settled index (binary-swap, confirmed valid via unchanged `total_symbols`) — all three land in a stable region (rank 5/4/3 respectively), all 40/40, not a knife-edge fit. **No-semantic (model-free) is now at parity with semantic: 40/40 (100%) both modes.** Full account in `bench-reports/lexical-readeditorstate-tiebreak-investigation-2026-08-17.md`.
- [ ] Synonym table is curated/incomplete by design (a query outside its ~13 groups gets no expansion) — the honest ceiling of a model-free technique, not a bug.
- [ ] PPR's directed (`callees_of`-only) walk means "what calls into this" gets no graph-centrality boost the way "what this calls" does — a known asymmetry, not measured as a problem on this task set.
- [ ] `gamma`'s weight on a lexical-only direct hit is low in non-degraded mode by design — the lexical-candidate-generation recall win is concentrated in stub/degraded-embedder mode, not a general reranking improvement when a real embedder is present.
- [x] **2026-08-20 cross-repo generalization test (keycloak, Java, 79,760 symbols — never tuned for) + `TEST_FILE_PENALTY` shipped.** Pointing the already-shipped 40/40 lexical binary at an unrelated, much larger, differently-languaged repo with zero keycloak-specific tuning measured 32/40 (80%) no-semantic, 40/40 semantic — the honest generalization gap between "tuned for this corpus" and "works on an arbitrary corpus." Diagnosed the 8 no-semantic misses: 6 have no textual bridge in the source at all (unfixable by any model-free technique); 2 do — `kc-sg-12` (recall-stage: never enters the candidate pool, cut by the hard `top_k` `LIMIT` before rerank runs) and `kc-sg-02` (scoring-stage: a JUnit test-helper's name-paraphrase outranks the real implementation). A 3-candidate Fable workflow tested fixes borrowed from a competitor tool's validated techniques (relative score floor; test-file de-prioritization) on **both** lexical (regression gate) and keycloak (improvement target) — the first fix in this project's model-free history validated on a second repo before shipping. A naive/unbounded floor implementation regressed keycloak (32→29); a disciplined, position-aware floor implementation was a pure no-op when independently re-measured against the correct 32/40 baseline (the agent's own self-reported "+3" did not survive re-verification — a second instance of this failure mode after `getActiveEditorState`); combining the floor with the penalty and re-tuning the penalty to 0.22 regressed to 26/40. The test-file penalty alone (`TEST_FILE_PENALTY=0.15`, validated by the implementing agent against neighboring values 0.10/0.25 before it died to an environment interruption — diff recovered and independently rebuilt/re-verified from scratch) is a clean, deterministic **+2** with zero regressions: keycloak no-semantic 32/40→**34/40** (3 identical trials), lexical unaffected (40/40 both modes ×3/×1), keycloak semantic unaffected (40/40), `getActiveEditorState` determinism unaffected (10/10 stable). `kc-sg-12`'s recall-stage problem remains open — the floor mechanism that targets it works as designed (rank #8, up from absent) but doesn't cross the benchmark's top-5 window on its own, and pushing it harder (via the penalty combination or further tuning) costs more than it gains on this benchmark. Full account in `bench-reports/keycloak-cross-repo-generalization-2026-08-20.md`.

## Plan 17 — `find_relevant_code` Compound-Question Usability Hint
*Motivation: `benchmarks/dataset/` (ERPNext head-to-head vs. graphify/code-review-graph) surfaced 4 real-embedding `max_turns` failures (Q05, Q13, Q21, Q23), all with the identical transcript signature — 1-2 `find_relevant_code`/`explain_symbol` calls, then 16-25 unguided Grep/Read calls, `num_turns=15` cut off mid-tool-call with zero final answer. Root cause confirmed by direct transcript inspection for Q05 (a compound "how is X computed, and what prevents Y" question): all 10 of the single query's top-ranked results covered only the X half (`work_order/`); the Y half's actual answer (`_validate_no_excess_transfer` in the sibling `stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`) never entered the candidate pool at all. A single query embedding anchors to one semantic neighborhood — it can't be expected to cover two genuinely distinct aspects of a compound question in one call.*

- [x] `concentration_note()` in `crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/find_relevant_code.rs` — when ≥80% of a query's top results (n≥3) cluster in one directory, `find_relevant_code`'s response gets an additive `"note"` field suggesting a follow-up call with a narrower/different intent, rather than the agent falling back to unguided Grep after one miss. Tool `description` in `server.rs` carries the same guidance up front.
- [x] Purely additive (new JSON field + description text) — does not touch `vector_search`/`expand_graph`/`rerank` or any scoring weight, so it carries none of the regression risk the Plan 16/keycloak history warns about.
- [x] **Two implementation bugs caught only by live-testing against the real ERPNext queries, not by unit tests alone — a durable lesson matching this project's own history of self-reported fixes not surviving re-verification.** (1) Exact-immediate-parent-directory grouping never fired on real data: a doctype's root file (`work_order/work_order.py`) and its `services/` submodule (`work_order/services/required_items.py`) counted as different directories, capping Q05's real candidate pool at 5/10 — below the 0.8 threshold — for a case that should obviously fire. (2) The first fix for that (grouping by "first N path components") passed all unit tests (which used bare relative fixtures like `"work_order/a.py"`) but was live-verified against the rebuilt binary to fire on **100% of every query, including two that shouldn't have** — `RetrievalResult::file_path` is an absolute path in production, so "first N components" always resolves to the constant filesystem prefix (`/Users/name/project/...`), identical across every result regardless of content. Root-caused by dumping raw `find_relevant_code` output over the real erpnext MCP endpoint for all 4 target queries, not by trusting the passing test suite. **Shipped fix**: group by directory **ancestor containment** (`work_order/` is an ancestor of `work_order/services/`, so they merge; `work_order/` and `sales_order/` aren't related, so they don't) — this needs no project-root plumbing and works identically on absolute or relative paths. Verified against the actual live daemon for all 4 real transcript queries: Q05 fires (8/10 = 0.80, the real motivating case), Q13/Q21 don't (0.4 each — genuine multi-module coverage, correctly not flagged as a miss), Q23 doesn't (0.7 — a documented near-miss, left as-is rather than lowering the threshold for one anecdote). A 7th unit test (`absolute_paths_do_not_falsely_concentrate`) pins the second bug so it can't silently regress.
- [x] A related, unrelated-to-this-fix daemon robustness issue found along the way: overwriting `~/.local/bin/codegraph` **in place** (`cp` over an existing path, same inode) while the old daemon process was still mid-shutdown (cancelling a background SCIP-indexing task) corrupted the kernel's code-signing validation for that inode — every subsequent exec of that exact path got silently `SIGKILL`'d (exit 137, zero output) even though the file's content/hash/codesign metadata were all identical to a working copy elsewhere. Not a bug in this session's code — a macOS pitfall for anyone hot-swapping a running daemon's own binary. Fixed by `rm`-then-`cp` (fresh inode) instead of overwrite-in-place, and by explicitly polling for full process exit after `codegraph stop` rather than trusting its immediate return.
- [x] Full validation after both fixes: `cargo test -p codegraph-mcp` (7/7), `cargo test --workspace` (36/36 suites, 0 failures), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (clean), plus live verification against the real erpnext MCP endpoint — not simulation.
- [x] **Now measured for two of the four original `max_turns` failures** (Q01, Q23 — from a later, separate 30-question `-fixed` re-run; see the follow-up section below). Result: the note/description guidance alone was necessary but not sufficient — the agent kept calling `find_relevant_code` once and falling back to 16+ unguided Greps regardless. Fixed only after (1) raising the harness's `MAX_TURNS` (14→25, `benchmarks/dataset/harness/run.py` — a shared constant applied identically to every arm, not a codegraph-specific advantage) and (2) further strengthening `find_relevant_code`'s own description to explicitly reframe "reaching for Grep again" as a signal to call the tool again instead. Live re-test: Q01 0%→66.7% coverage (turn-cap raise alone fixed it, 27 turns, no further Grep-fallback pattern change needed); Q23 0%→100% coverage, but only after *both* changes — the turn-cap raise alone still hit `max_turns` again at 26 turns with the same `1 find_relevant_code + 16 Grep` pattern, and only the strengthened description changed the actual tool-call mix (`Grep` 16→8, `Read` 9→2, plus a follow-up `explain_symbol` call) and let it finish in 13 turns. One confirmatory run each, not repeated trials — treat as a real, mechanistically-explained fix (each transcript's terminal `"terminal_reason": "max_turns"` / tool-call histogram was inspected directly, not inferred from the score alone), but not immune to this project's own established ~13pp turn-cap noise floor until re-run more than once.
- [ ] The deeper fix — actual query decomposition (multiple sub-queries for one compound intent, merged results) — is a real pipeline change, not attempted here. Would need its own plan doc and the full lexical+keycloak(+ERPNext) validation cycle before shipping, per this project's established bar.

### Follow-up — Q23 diagnosis: `get_file_context` returns structure without substance

*Investigated why Q23 (a different ERPNext benchmark question, also a `max_turns` failure) didn't share Q05's root cause. It doesn't — direct transcript timestamps showed a real, unrelated API-retry storm (44 minutes of `api_retry` system events with growing backoff, `error: "unknown"`) inflated one retry's wall-clock to 2732s with no bearing on the actual failure; stripping that out, the real pattern is: `find_relevant_code` (1 call) + `get_file_context` (1 call) landed the agent on the exact correct anchor file every time, in both Q13/Q21/Q23 — retrieval is not the problem for these three at all. What burned the remaining ~15-20 turns was fact-mining: `get_file_context` on the real 93-symbol anchor file returned only `{name, kind, lines}` per symbol — no snippet, no docstring, no ordering. The three functions the question actually needed (`set_previous_operation_serial_batch`, `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch`, `_cap_pool_to_qty`) were verified present in that exact 93-item list, indistinguishable by name alone from 90 unrelated ones — forcing one `Read` per candidate to find out what each does.*

- [x] `get_file_context` now includes a `"signature"` field per symbol — the first non-blank line of its own span (typically its `def`/signature line, occasionally a leading decorator), truncated to 120 chars via the existing `truncate_snippet` helper. Sourced from the already-present `state.source_loader` (`SourceLoader::snippet`, already used elsewhere) — no new dependency. Purely additive to the response schema; `focused_symbol` gets the same field.
- [x] Degrades to `signature: null` (never errors) when the source file isn't readable at query time (stale index entry, deleted file) — verified both by a dedicated unit test and by the pre-existing integration tests, which use fake, unreadable file paths and continued passing unchanged.
- [x] A real implementation bug caught before shipping, not after: initial test fixtures assumed 1-indexed `span.start_line`/`end_line`; two of four new unit tests failed against the real implementation, which correctly uses tree-sitter's 0-indexed `.row` convention (confirmed against `codegraph-parser/src/extractor.rs`, matching `SourceLoader::snippet`'s direct 0-indexed array slicing). Fixed the test fixtures, not the implementation — the implementation was already correct.
- [x] Verified live against the real erpnext daemon on the actual anchor file that motivated this: all three target functions now show clear, correct one-line signatures (e.g. `def set_previous_operation_serial_batch(parent_doc, row):`) in the same single `get_file_context` call, with zero additional round-trips.
- [x] Tool description in `server.rs` updated to describe the actual current behavior (also dropped a stale claim — "and their relationships" — that the implementation never actually delivered; no callers/callees data has ever been in this response).
- [x] Full validation: `cargo test -p codegraph-mcp` (9/9 for this tool: 5 existing + 4 new), `cargo test --workspace` (36/36 suites), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (clean).
- [x] **Partially measured**: see the Q01/Q23 turn-cap re-test above — Q23 did eventually clear `max_turns` and hit 100% coverage, but only combined with a harness `MAX_TURNS` raise and a further `find_relevant_code` description change, not from this fix alone. Isolating this fix's individual contribution would need an A/B re-run with only this change reverted; not done.
- [ ] The unrelated API-retry-storm anomaly observed on Q23 (44 min of backoff, `error: "unknown"`) was not investigated further — it's an Anthropic-API/`claude -p` CLI layer issue, out of scope for this project's own code.

### Follow-up — Q01/Q23 turn-cap re-investigation (`-fixed` 30-question re-run)

*A later full 30-question re-run (`results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/`, harness `MAX_TURNS=14` at the time) still hit two live `max_turns` failures for the codegraph arm: Q01 (0% coverage, empty answer) and Q23 (0% coverage, empty answer). Both were re-diagnosed from scratch via direct transcript inspection (`transcripts/codegraph-Q0{1,23}.json`) rather than assumed to be the same already-fixed Q05 root cause.*

- [x] Confirmed via the transcript's own terminal event, not inferred: both carried `"terminal_reason": "max_turns"` at `num_turns` one above the configured cap, `stop_reason: "tool_use"` (cut off mid-`Read`), `is_error: true`. Tool-call histogram for both: **1** `find_relevant_code` call, then 12-16 unguided `Grep` calls and 8-9 `Read` calls — the already-shipped Plan 17 guidance ("call again... rather than switching to Grep") was present in the description at the time and did not change this behavior.
- [x] Cross-checked against every other run of the same two questions (`claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-sonnet-5-nosem`, `claude-sonnet-5-semantic`, original `claude-sonnet-5`, `deepseek-v4-pro`): codegraph scored 0.67-0.83 on Q01 in 4 of 6 runs — only the `-fixed` run and `deepseek-v4-pro` (a different provider, same failure signature) hit 0%, confirming this is a real but narrow turn-cap edge case, not a systematic retrieval defect.
- [x] `MAX_TURNS` raised 14→25 in `benchmarks/dataset/harness/run.py` — single shared module constant, used identically to build every arm's `--max-turns` CLI flag and system-prompt text, so this is a benchmark-methodology fix, not a codegraph-specific advantage. Justified empirically before changing: several already-*successful* runs in the same `-fixed` directory organically reached 19-26 turns under the old "14" setting with no cutoff at all (Claude Code's internal turn accounting is looser than the reported `num_turns`), so 14 was already non-binding in the common case and only biting at the margin.
- [x] Re-tested Q01 alone at the new cap: fixed, 0%→66.7% coverage, finished cleanly at 27 turns (`stop_reason: "end_turn"`, no cutoff).
- [x] Re-tested Q23 alone at the new cap: **did not fix it** — hit `max_turns` again at 26 turns, same `1 find_relevant_code + 16 Grep` pattern, now at higher cost (869K tokens / $0.66, up from 367K / $0.33). This ruled out "just raise the cap further" as the real fix and confirmed the bottleneck is behavioral (tool abandonment), not budget.
- [x] `find_relevant_code`'s description in `server.rs` strengthened further: explicitly states that reaching for Grep to keep exploring is a signal to call the tool again with a narrower intent, not evidence the tool already failed, and that Grep costs more turns per unit of ground covered than a second targeted call.
- [x] Rebuilt, safely swapped `~/.local/bin/codegraph` (`rm`-then-`cp`, fresh inode, full stop/start poll — same procedure as the earlier macOS SIGKILL pitfall fix), erpnext re-indexed fresh (3605 files / 17600 symbols) in ~4s (expected — description-only change, no retrieval/parsing code touched).
- [x] Re-tested Q23 a third time under the strengthened description (same `MAX_TURNS=25`): fixed, 0%→**100%** coverage, finished in 13 turns — well under the cap, no cutoff. Tool mix changed materially: `Grep` 16→8, `Read` 9→2, plus a new follow-up `explain_symbol` call (not the literal "call `find_relevant_code` again" the description suggested, but a real shift away from brute-force grep toward another targeted MCP tool).
- [x] **A fairness bug caught by `advisor()` before the numbers below were reported, not after**: the first pass of this fix retried *only* codegraph's two turn-cap failures at the raised cap, then regenerated `RESULTS.md` and drafted a topline (codegraph 74.8%, "win count 10/4/5") comparing codegraph-at-25-turns against graphify/code-review-graph still scored at cap 14 on the identical failure mode. A direct check (`grep`-equivalent over each arm's `-results.jsonl` for `returncode!=0 or empty answer`) confirmed graphify had the *same* `max_turns` failure on Q23 **and** Q26, and code-review-graph on Q23 — all silently still scored 0% while only codegraph got rescued. This is exactly the shape of self-reported-number-doesn't-survive-verification this file already warns about (see the keycloak section above) — caught here before publication rather than after.
- [x] Fixed by giving every arm's turn-cap failures the identical retry treatment (raised cap only — **not** the codegraph-specific `find_relevant_code` description change, which doesn't apply to graphify/code-review-graph's own tools): graphify Q23 (15→25 turns, 0%→91.7%) and Q26 (15→13 turns, 0%→50.0%); code-review-graph Q23 (15→15 turns, 0%→100.0%). Confirmed zero remaining turn-cap failures (`returncode!=0`/empty-answer) across all three arms after the retries.
- [x] Also fixed a related reporting-accuracy bug this surfaced: `harness/report.py`'s methodology line hardcoded `"max 14 turns"` regardless of the actual `MAX_TURNS` in effect. Added a `max_turns_cap` field to each row written by `run.py` (missing on pre-existing rows, which correctly default to the legacy value of 14 for this computation — not treated as "unknown" and silently dropped, which would have let a handful of new cap-25 rows misreport the *entire* run as cap 25) and made the methodology line report the true observed range, explicitly flagging a mid-run cap change rather than asserting a single wrong number.
- [x] **Corrected, fairness-restored topline** for `benchmarks/dataset/results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/`: codegraph mean coverage 74.8% (n=30), graphify 65.9% (was 61.1% pre-fix), code-review-graph 67.7% (was 64.4% pre-fix); head-to-head win count codegraph 9 / graphify 4 / code-review-graph 5 / 12 ties (was 10/4/5/11 in the not-yet-fair draft — codegraph's win-count edge shrank by one once competitors' equivalent failures were also given a fair shot, which is itself evidence the earlier draft number was inflated by the asymmetry, not a real codegraph-specific effect). `RESULTS.md`'s methodology line now reads "turn cap varies by task (14/25 — a subset of tasks were retried under a raised cap...)" instead of a flat, now-inaccurate "max 14 turns."
- [ ] **Caveat, stated plainly**: every retry above (codegraph x2, graphify x2, code-review-graph x1) is a single run, not a repeated trial. The mechanistic evidence (direct `terminal_reason` field, tool-call histogram deltas, direct emptiness/returncode checks) is stronger than a bare before/after score, but this project's own established ~13pp turn-cap noise floor means a single confirmatory pass per arm is not proof at the level of the lexical/keycloak multi-run validation bar elsewhere in this file. Don't cite these exact coverage numbers as guaranteed-reproducible without a repeat run.
- [ ] A caught-and-fixed operator mistake, noted for the record: the first retest attempt used `--provider claude` (this harness's default), which silently resolves to `claude-opus-4-8`, not `claude-sonnet-5` — briefly wrote wrong-model rows into the `claude-sonnet-5-fixed` results file before being caught and overwritten with the correct `--provider claude-sonnet-5`. `--out-dir` alone does not pin the model; always pass `--provider` explicitly when targeting a specific model's results directory.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-03 full codegraph re-run (repeatability check on the caveat above)

*A full, independent 30-question re-run of just the `codegraph` arm (`--provider claude-sonnet-5`, same `MAX_TURNS=25`, same erpnext index, binary unchanged since the Aug 31 build that already carries the Plan 17 fixes) — the first actual repeat trial the caveat above called for. graphify/code-review-graph rows were left untouched (already fair-retried); `grade.py --arm codegraph --regrade` re-judged only the new codegraph answers.*

- [x] All 30 tasks completed cleanly this time (`rc=0` on every row, no `max_turns` empties), cost $7.54.
- [x] **New topline**: codegraph mean coverage **70.2%** (down from 74.8% in the single prior run — a ~4.6pp swing on the identical binary/index/model), graphify 65.9% (cached, unchanged), code-review-graph 67.7% (cached, unchanged). Head-to-head win count codegraph 8 / graphify 7 / code-review-graph 6 / 9 ties (was 9/4/5/12) — codegraph's win-count margin over graphify shrank from +5 to +1 between the two runs, using the exact same competitor data both times.
- [ ] **This confirms, not just theorizes, the ~13pp turn-cap/run-to-run noise floor already flagged above** — a same-binary, same-index, same-model re-run moved the headline number by several points and materially changed the apparent win margin. Report at `benchmarks/dataset/results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/RESULTS.md` now reflects this second run (grades.json/RESULTS.md were overwritten in place, not kept side-by-side) — treat any single run's coverage/win-count figure from this harness as indicative, not a precise measurement, until multiple repeats are averaged.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-03 full 3-arm re-run + `get_file_context` relative-path bug found via the tool-flow report

*The user asked for a plain HTML report (not a published Artifact) showing every arm's step-by-step tool-call flow and token usage per question. Only `codegraph` had full 30-question transcripts (the prior graphify/code-review-graph runs had kept transcripts only for their turn-cap retries — 2/30 and 1/30 respectively). Both daemons (`:8090`, `:5555`) turned out to be up, so graphify and code-review-graph were also fully re-run (30 questions each, `MAX_TURNS=25` uniformly this time, not just the previously-retried subset) rather than shipping a report with 2 of 3 arms summary-only.*

- [x] Full re-run cost ~$14 (graphify + code-review-graph, 30 Q each), regraded with `--regrade`, `RESULTS.md` regenerated. **New topline, all 3 arms now on one consistent all-25-turn run**: codegraph 70.2%, code-review-graph 65.2%, graphify 64.7%; win count 9/7/5 (9 ties).
- [x] Built `benchmarks/dataset/results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/tool-flow-report.html` (plain static file, ~1.2MB) — summary table + per-question collapsible sections, 3 arms side by side, ordered tool-call flow with inputs/result previews, token table (input/output/cache-creation/cache-read), coverage badge, collapsible final answer.
- [x] **Real bug found by inspecting that report, not by a benchmark score**: the user spotted repeated `codegraph:get_file_context` → `"file not indexed"` failures. Confirmed systematic: 20 occurrences across 14 of 30 codegraph-arm questions, every single one a repo-relative path (e.g. `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`) — exactly the path convention Read/Grep/Glob accept everywhere else in the same agent session.
- [x] **Root cause**: `GraphDb::file_by_path` (`crates/codegraph-store/src/graph_db.rs`) does an exact `WHERE path = ?1` match against the always-*absolute* stored path; `get_file_context`'s own JSON-RPC schema (`server.rs`) actively claimed `"description": "Relative file path."` — the opposite of what the implementation required. `summarize_module`'s `files_in_prefix` already tolerated both absolute and relative paths via a `LIKE` pattern; `get_file_context` (and the REST `/api/file/*` handler, same bug) never got the equivalent treatment.
- [x] **Fix shipped**: new `GraphDb::files_by_suffix()` — exact match tried first (fast path, unchanged), then a suffix `LIKE` fallback (with `%`/`_`/`\` properly escaped — plain `_` is a single-char SQL wildcard and appears in most of these filenames, e.g. `delivery_note.py`; an unescaped version would have silently over-matched). Wired into both `get_file_context::handle` (via a new `resolve_file` helper) and the REST `file_handler`. 0 matches → the original "file not indexed" error (now with a hint); 1 match → resolved and used; 2+ matches → a distinct "ambiguous path" error listing every candidate rather than silently picking one. The response's `"path"` field now echoes the *resolved* absolute path instead of echoing back the caller's input, so an agent that starts with a relative path learns the real absolute path for later calls in the same session. Tool schema description corrected to state both forms are accepted.
- [x] 7 new tests (4 `codegraph-store` unit tests incl. the underscore-wildcard-escaping case and an ambiguous-match case; 3 `codegraph-mcp` integration tests incl. relative-path resolution and ambiguous-suffix-errors-not-silently-picks). Full gate: `cargo test --workspace` (all suites green, no regressions), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (clean).
- [x] Rebuilt, safely swapped `~/.local/bin/codegraph` (`rm`-then-`cp`, fresh inode, full stop/start poll), erpnext re-indexed fresh in ~20s (3605 files / 17600 symbols, unchanged). **Live-verified against the real daemon on all 3 of the exact failing paths the user pasted** (`erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`, `.../purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`, `.../purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`) — all three now resolve correctly.
- [x] **Measured** (third codegraph-arm re-run, same fixed binary, same erpnext index, cost ~$7): the fix itself is confirmed dead-on — **0** `"file not indexed"` and **0** `"ambiguous path"` errors across all 30 questions' transcripts (was 20/14 questions before). But the benchmark's own headline coverage number did **not** go up: 74.8% (run 1) → 70.2% (run 2, no code change, pure repeat) → **64.2%** (run 3, this fix). Win count also dropped: codegraph 9/7/5→**5/7/5** (13 ties) against the same graphify/code-review-graph data both times.
- [x] **Read correctly, this is not evidence the fix hurt anything — it's the ~13pp run-to-run noise floor this file already established, now observed a second time on the same binary.** Run 2 (70.2%) had zero code changes from run 1 (74.8%) and still moved ~4.6pp; run 3 (64.2%) has a real, verified-good fix and moved a further ~6pp in the *same direction* run 2 already was trending, landing within the same noise band (all three runs span 64.2%-74.8%, a 10.6pp range — consistent with, not larger than, the previously-measured noise). The fix's actual effect (turns/tokens no longer burned on 20 dead-end calls) is real and directly observed in the transcripts; the aggregate 30-question judged-coverage metric is simply too noisy at n=1-run to detect a signal of this size, exactly as this file's own repeated-run caveat predicted before this test was run. Do not read the 64.2% figure as "the fix regressed codegraph" — the mechanistic evidence (error count) is stronger than the coverage delta, per this file's established evidentiary hierarchy.
- [x] `tool-flow-report.html` and `RESULTS.md` regenerated to reflect this third run — both now show 0 tool-error rows for codegraph. Averaging 3+ repeats (not done here — would need another ~$15-20 and is the obvious next step if a precise topline number is ever needed) is what this file's own noise-floor caveat calls for before trusting any single run's coverage number at face value.

## Plan 18 — ERPNext Benchmark Competitive Gap Closure (vs Graphify)
*Full plan: `plans/18-competitive-gap-closure-erpnext.md`. Motivation: the near-tied 3-arm result above (codegraph 64.2%, graphify 64.7%, code-review-graph 65.2%) prompted a real per-question, per-fact audit — not another benchmark re-run — to find out why codegraph loses on 13/30 questions instead of guessing.*

- [x] **Audit complete.** Cross-referenced `grades.json`'s per-fact judge reasoning against actual tool-call transcripts for all 13 codegraph-loses-to-graphify questions, verified the top findings against primary source (live daemon queries, direct file reads, tree-sitter query source), not just transcript text.
- [x] **Finding 1**: `trace_impact` (3/30 uses) and `find_implementations` (1/30 uses) are described in `server.rs` as pre-edit/refactor tools ("before changing a function", "before adding new implementations"), so a read-only Q&A agent never reaches for them — even though Group B ("caller/callee trace + impact analysis") is `trace_impact`'s namesake use case and one of codegraph's two worst-losing groups (69.8% vs graphify's 79.2%). Rests on the usage-rate correlation and the description text itself — an earlier draft's "mechanistically confirmed on Q24" claim was checked (via a live daemon query) and retracted: Q24's savepoint sites all call `frappe.db.savepoint`, a method on the Frappe framework itself, which isn't checked out/indexed by this benchmark at all, so `trace_impact` had no symbol to target and could not have closed that gap. Caught by `advisor()` before this plan was presented, not after — see the plan doc's own corrected writeup.
- [x] **Finding 2**: the 5 worst losses (Q10, Q21, Q24, Q11, Q12, all −33 to −43pp) all show 1-3 `find_relevant_code` calls followed by 6-15 raw Grep/Read calls — the exact shape Plan 17's `concentration_note` targets, but it only fired for 1 of these 5 (Q10), and even there the agent ignored it. `concentration_note`'s directory-clustering heuristic doesn't catch "results collectively don't cover a multi-part question" when the gap spans different directories. **A co-cause found by `advisor()` review**: `harness/run.py`'s system prompt (identical across all 3 arms) literally requires only **one** MCP tool call before Grep is fair game — a stronger, harness-level explanation for this exact pattern than tool-description wording alone, meaning Fix 2's in-tool nudge is fighting an explicit competing instruction and should be expected to have a smaller effect than the Plan 17 Q23 precedent it's modeled on.
- [x] **Finding 3 (real parser gap, verified against source AND the live daemon — corrected in scope from an earlier draft)**: JS/TS object-literal properties of the **colon + function-expression** form (`{ render_item_prices: function (frm) {...} }`) are never extracted as symbols — confirmed by reading `javascript_lang.rs`/`typescript_lang.rs`'s tree-sitter query patterns (cover `function_declaration`, arrow/function-expression `variable_declarator`s, `class`, `method_definition` — nothing for a `pair` node). Verified concretely on Q12 (−33.3pp): `item.js`'s permission check (confirmed present at line 832 via `grep`) lives inside exactly this unindexed `render_item_prices: function(frm){...}` shape at line 826; a live `get_file_context` query on `item.js` confirms no returned symbol's span covers line 832. **Correction**: an earlier draft claimed this under-indexes JS "project-wide" — checking the same live `get_file_context` response shows the **ES6 shorthand** method form (`key(args) {...}`, e.g. `item.js`'s own `toggle_has_serial_batch_fields`/`valuation_method`/`warehouse_group`, in the same object literal) is already correctly extracted via the existing `method_definition` pattern. The real gap is narrower: only the colon-form syntax is missing, not object-literal methods in general.
- [x] **Finding 4** (minor): `get_file_context` (21 calls) is underused vs. raw `Read` with guessed line offsets (141 calls) — several losing transcripts show blind `Read(offset=995,...)`-style guessing instead of checking the file's symbol layout first.
- [x] **Finding 5** (background noise, explicitly not a fix target): 4 smaller losses (Q01, Q13, Q09, Q02) show reasonable tool usage but lose on answer-attribution precision — consistent with the established noise floor, not a systematic defect; flagged in the plan to not chase with scoring changes without new evidence.
- [x] **Fix 1** (Finding 1): reworded `trace_impact`/`find_implementations` descriptions in `server.rs` to be read/explain-neutral — `trace_impact` now frames itself as "the graph-derived, exhaustive answer to 'what calls this'... equally useful for read-only investigation... as for pre-change blast-radius analysis"; `find_implementations` now explicitly names "read-only questions asking you to enumerate every concrete case of an abstraction."
- [x] **Fix 2** (Finding 2 tier 1): strengthened `find_relevant_code`'s description — explicit "call again once per distinct aspect with a genuinely different intent," notes the `note` field's absence doesn't imply completeness, reframes reaching for Grep on a multi-part question as the signal to return to this tool.
- [x] **Fix 3** (Finding 4): `get_file_context` description now says "Call this before a blind Read on an unfamiliar file... instead of guessing at line offsets."
- [x] **Fix 4** (Finding 3): added the colon-form object-literal `pair` query patterns (`function_expression` and `arrow_function` variants) to both `javascript_lang.rs` and `typescript_lang.rs`. 8 new fixture tests in `crates/codegraph-parser/tests/extraction.rs`, including the exact `render_item_prices`-shaped frappe.io pattern and both false-positive guards (plain data `pair`, identifier-reference value) — all passed on first run. Full gate: `cargo test --workspace` (all suites green) + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (clean).
- [x] **Deployed and live-verified, not just unit-tested.** Rebuilt, safely swapped `~/.local/bin/codegraph` (`rm`-then-`cp`, full stop/start poll). **A real gotcha hit and fixed along the way**: a plain `codegraph index` (no source files changed, only the parser) silently skipped re-parsing everything — checksum-filtering applies even to the "force full re-index" path — so erpnext's symbol count came back unchanged at 17600 after the first attempt. Re-ran with `codegraph index --fresh` (clears checksums + vectors) to force genuine re-extraction: **17600 → 19340 symbols (+1740)**, confirming the colon-form pattern is common across the corpus, not an item.js-only fluke. Live `get_file_context` query on `item.js` confirms `render_item_prices` (lines 828-889, `signature: "render_item_prices: function (frm) {"`) is now present — the plan's own explicit success criterion — and `item.js` overall went from 16→50 extracted symbols. Other registered projects (`node`, `main-be`, `codegraph`, `lexical`) spot-checked unaffected/sane after the restart.
- [x] **Fix 5** (Finding 2 tier 2, conditional) — correctly not started per the plan's own gating ("only if Fixes 1-4 don't close enough of the gap"); no measurement exists yet to justify it.
- [x] **Validation protocol run**: 2 repeat 30-question codegraph runs on the Plan 18 binary — run 1 overwrote `results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/` (kept alongside graphify 64.7%/code-review-graph 65.2%, both unchanged), run 2 into a fresh `results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-post-plan18-run2/` (codegraph-only, cost ~$7 each, ~$14 total).
- [x] **Coverage**: run 1 mean 67.3%, run 2 mean 71.1% — **mean across both, 69.2%, range 67.3-71.1%** (a 3.8pp spread between repeats, notably tighter than the ~13pp whole-run noise floor seen on every prior repeat pair in this file, though still just 2 data points and not proof the noise floor itself shrank). Both individual runs land above graphify's 64.7% and code-review-graph's 65.2% baselines — the first time every one of this binary's repeat runs has cleared both competitors, vs. the prior pattern of straddling above and below.
- [x] **A nuance not to paper over**: run 1's mean (67.3%) is codegraph's *highest* of any run in this file's history, yet its **head-to-head win count is 5/30 — lower than graphify's 8 and code-review-graph's 8** (9 ties). A higher mean with a lower per-question win count means the gains are concentrated in a few questions moving by a lot, not a broad-based per-question edge — worth knowing before calling this a clean win. Full breakdown in the regenerated `results/hard/claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-fixed/RESULTS.md` and `tool-flow-report.html`.
- [x] **Mechanistic check — did tool usage actually shift, per Fix 1's own success criterion**: `trace_impact` usage rose from the pre-fix baseline of 3/30 to **8/30 (run 1)** and **5/30 (run 2)** — real, consistent, in the predicted direction, both runs above baseline. **`find_implementations` did NOT improve** — pre-fix baseline was already only 1/30, and it dropped to **0/30 in both post-fix runs**. At these low counts (0-1 out of 30) this is more likely noise than a real regression, but it should be stated plainly: the `find_implementations` half of Fix 1 has no positive evidence behind it yet, only the `trace_impact` half does.
- [x] **Honest read, per this file's own repeated lesson about not over-claiming from few runs**: this is genuinely the best-looking result this binary has produced against graphify/code-review-graph (both runs beat both competitors' means; the mechanistic trace_impact-usage signal moved in the predicted direction and correlates with the coverage gain, which is stronger evidence than the score alone). It is **not** proof the noise floor has been beaten down to certainty — 2 runs is the plan's stated minimum, not a large sample, and the win-count/mean-coverage divergence in run 1 shows the picture isn't uniformly positive. Treat "codegraph now beats graphify" as *supported*, not *settled* — a third+ repeat would firm this up further if a precise claim is ever needed for something like a public benchmark writeup.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-03 4th arm added: `codegraph-nosem` (no-semantic-model / stub-embedder)

*User requested a real no-semantic-model comparison arm, in its own separate codegraph project (not just a config flag on the existing one) so it can't cross-contaminate the real "erpnext" project's semantic results.*

- [x] **New project `erpnext-nosem`**: a local copy of the `erpnext` checkout at the identical pinned commit (`95212e5738...`, verified via `git rev-parse HEAD`), registered as its own codegraph project with `[embedding] model = "bge-small-en-v1.5-nosem-stub"` (an unrecognized name so `ensure_model()` skips its auto-download path entirely — no network access, no directory creation) + `model_path` pointing at a nonexistent file, so `BgeSmallModel::load()` falls through to the graceful zero-vector stub. Live-verified via `watch_status`: `embedding_degraded: true` for `erpnext-nosem`, `false` for `erpnext` — isolated correctly, confirmed not process-wide.
- [x] **A real setup bug found and fixed along the way, not just a slow run**: the first setup attempt corrupted the project — `codegraph init`'s automatic daemon restart began its own background full-index for `erpnext-nosem` using the *original* (real-model) config before the stub config edit landed, and that race against a subsequent manual `stop` + config-edit + `index --fresh` left the project's SQLite DB with **7306 files** (double the real 3605) — direct inspection showed rows from *both* `erpnext-nosem/erpnext/...` **and** `erpnext/erpnext/...` paths mixed into one database. This is also what made the run so slow (20+ min, killed manually) — not stub-mode being inherently slow, contrary to first appearances. `erpnext`'s own project data was checked and confirmed unaffected (3605 files, correct) — the corruption was isolated to the new project. Fixed by fully removing the corrupted project + its stray data directories and redoing the setup with the daemon never running until the stub config was already finalized (no init-triggered race). Clean redo: `codegraph index --fresh` completed in under 2 minutes (vs 20+ min corrupted) with the exact correct **3605 files, 19340 symbols** — matching `erpnext`'s own real-mode count exactly, confirming stub mode is genuinely ~3-5x faster than real embedding when it isn't racing itself, consistent with skipping ONNX inference entirely.
- [x] **Harness wired**: new `harness/mcp/codegraph-nosem.json` (points at `http://localhost:3030/erpnext-nosem/mcp`) + a `"codegraph-nosem"` entry in `harness/run.py`'s `ARMS` dict. 1-question smoke test passed cleanly (real MCP tool calls, grounded answer, no errors) before committing to the full paid run.
- [x] **Full 30-question run**: cost ~$6.39, **0** `"file not indexed"`/`"ambiguous path"` errors (the Plan 18 `get_file_context` fix carries over correctly to a freshly-registered project, as expected since it's a store-layer fix not tied to any one project). **Mean coverage 68.8% — the highest of all 4 arms** (codegraph 67.3%, code-review-graph 65.2%, graphify 64.7%), and cheapest.
- [x] **Same "higher mean, lower win count" nuance as the real-codegraph run above, worth flagging again rather than burying**: despite the highest mean, `codegraph-nosem`'s head-to-head win count is only **3/30** — well below graphify's 7 and code-review-graph's 5 (codegraph itself only 2/30, 13 ties total across all 4 arms). The gain is concentrated in a handful of questions, not a broad per-question edge — same pattern as codegraph's own real-semantic run, now observed a second time with a different arm, which weakens (slightly) the idea that it's codegraph-real-semantic-specific and suggests it may be a property of this particular repeat/question-set interaction more generally. Don't read either arm's high mean as a clean, uniform win without checking win count alongside it.
- [x] `RESULTS.md` and `tool-flow-report.html` regenerated to include all 4 arms (report.py auto-discovers arms via glob, no code change needed there; the HTML generator script needed its hardcoded 3-arm list extended to 4, done).
- [ ] Not yet done: a repeat run of `codegraph-nosem` (this file's own established bar for the noise floor applies here too — one run is directional, not proof) and a similar audit of *why* codegraph-nosem is losing head-to-heads despite a strong mean, mirroring Plan 18's own methodology for the real-semantic arm.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-04 5th arm added: `codegraph-colby` (github.com/colbymchenry/codegraph, external competitor)

*User asked to add a real external competitor — a separately-maintained, unrelated project that also happens to be named "codegraph" — as a benchmark arm.*

- [x] **A real collision risk found and mitigated before installing anything, not after**: the competitor's own CLI binary is also named `codegraph`, its installer's *default* paths are `~/.codegraph` (bundle) and `~/.local/bin` (symlink) — the **exact same paths** this project's own CLI and per-project state already use. Installing it naively would have risked shadowing our own `codegraph` on PATH and/or corrupting `~/.codegraph/projects.toml`. Installed instead to an isolated prefix (`<home>/.colby-codegraph/`, via the installer's own `CODEGRAPH_INSTALL_DIR`/`CODEGRAPH_BIN_DIR` env var overrides), never added to PATH, invoked only by absolute path everywhere. Verified after install: `which codegraph` still resolves to our own `~/.local/bin/codegraph`, our own daemon/projects unaffected.
- [x] Second collision avoided the same way as `codegraph-nosem`: the competitor also stores per-project state in a `.codegraph/` directory *inside the repo root* — identical convention to ours. Indexed a separate checkout (`repos/erpnext-colby/`, verified at the identical pinned commit) rather than `repos/erpnext`, so its `.codegraph/` can't collide with our own project's `.codegraph/config.toml` there. Their `codegraph init -y` indexed it in 11.2s (3,677 files, 31,127 nodes, 92,325 edges — file count differs slightly from our 3605 because of different file-type inclusion rules, not a bug).
- [x] **Harness change, not just config**: their MCP server is stdio (`codegraph serve --mcp --path <repo>`), single-tool design (`codegraph_explore` — the other 6 tools exist but are unlisted by default, matching this project's own consideration of tool-affordance issues in Plan 18). Since `run.py`'s subprocess `cwd`, `--add-dir`, and the system prompt's `{repo}` text were all hardcoded to the shared `ERPNEXT` constant for every arm, added a real per-arm `repo_dir` mechanism (`repo_dir_for(arm)`, defaulting to `ERPNEXT`, overridden to `COLBY_ERPNEXT` for this one arm) threaded through all three call sites — the first genuine harness code change beyond adding an MCP config + ARMS entry, needed because this arm's server can't just point at a URL like the others; it needs the agent's own cwd to match where its index lives.
- [x] Smoke test (1 question) passed cleanly before the paid run: 7/7 tool calls were `codegraph_explore`, zero Grep/Read fallback, grounded correct-looking answer.
- [x] **Full 30-question run**: cost ~$13 (higher per-question than our own arms, consistent with the smoke test), 0/30 failed rows (`returncode`/empty-answer both clean). **Mean coverage 65.7%** — landing between graphify (64.7%) and code-review-graph (65.2%), below both codegraph (67.3%) and codegraph-nosem (68.8%). Most consistent arm by stdev (0.184) of all 5.
- [x] **The smoke test's "zero Grep/Read fallback" did NOT hold across the full 30** — worth stating plainly since it directly contradicts the tool's own marketing claim ("file reads cut to zero on all seven repos" in their README's benchmark section, measured on their own harness/repos, not this one). Across all 30 questions: `Read` called 83 times, `Grep` 71 times, their own `codegraph_explore` 74 times — roughly even three-way split, only **3/30** questions had zero Grep/Read. Not a criticism of the tool's retrieval quality (65.7% coverage is competitive) — just a correction to not assume the smoke test's clean 7/7 result generalizes; this project's own history (Plan 17/18) already shows first-call-then-Grep-fallback is a common pattern across every arm in this harness, this one included, and this harness's system prompt licenses exactly one MCP call before Grep either way (see Plan 18's Finding 2 co-cause note).
- [x] `RESULTS.md` and `tool-flow-report.html` (including its stats-card row) regenerated to include all 5 arms — grid CSS widened from 4 to 5 columns with new breakpoints (5→3→2→1 depending on viewport).
- [ ] Not yet done: a repeat run (same noise-floor caveat as every other arm in this file) and a per-question audit of why `codegraph-colby` under/over-performs on specific question groups, mirroring Plan 18's methodology.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-04 Group F ("Trap / hallucination resistance") mini-audit: 2 real bugs found and fixed

*User pointed at the 5-arm HTML report's Group F rows (Q26-Q30) after noticing codegraph scored 0.0% on Q26 while `codegraph-colby` scored 62.5%. Traced the actual transcripts (not just the score delta) for the two worst rows, Q26 (0.0%) and Q30 (40.0%), mirroring Plan 18's methodology.*

- [x] **Q26 root cause (mechanistically confirmed, not inferred from the score alone)**: the trap is "does Stock Entry block submission on an expired batch" (correct answer: no). codegraph's transcript shows `trace_impact` called with `symbol: "erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py::validate_batch"` (a repo-relative-path-shaped guess, mirroring exactly what `find_relevant_code` echoes back) → **`symbol not found`**. The agent never retried with a corrected symbol; it fell back to `Grep`, found the *call site* (`self.validate_batch()` at line 310) by text, and — having never seen the function's actual body — concluded from the name alone that it must enforce expiry. Live-read the real body: `validate_batch()` only gates on `self.purpose` and delegates to `item.validate_batch()`; no expiry logic anywhere in the chain. Classic "grep found a name, agent trusted the name" hallucination, root-caused to a **symbol-resolution gap of the identical shape** as the file-path bug fixed earlier this session for `get_file_context`.
- [x] **Fix (Finding A)**: `resolve_symbol()` (`crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/mod.rs`) now recovers `<repo-relative-path>::<name>`-shaped input by suffix-matching the path portion against indexed files (reusing `files_by_suffix`, the same helper `get_file_context` already uses) and filtering that file's symbols by the name portion, **before** falling through to the existing bare-name/in-degree tiebreak — which also could never have matched a `path::name`-shaped input anyway, since a symbol's bare `name` field never contains `::`. Live-verified against the real `erpnext` project post-fix: `trace_impact` and `explain_symbol` on the exact Q26 input now resolve correctly; `explain_symbol`'s `definition` field returns the real 9-line body, which plainly contains no expiry check — the evidence that would have prevented the hallucination. 2 new regression tests in `crates/codegraph-mcp/tests/mcp_server.rs`.
- [x] **Q30 root cause (mechanistically confirmed)**: separate bug, same question group. `trace_impact` called on `validate_selling_price` (a real, uniquely-named, correctly-resolved symbol) returned **681 affected nodes / 275,960 characters — over the MCP client's per-result token limit — and the call errored out entirely**, discarding the whole response. `impact_graph`'s `affected` list had no cap at any layer. Forced (again) into a Grep-only fallback; Grep's raw text output happened to include the crucial gating line (`if self.get("is_return") or not frappe.get_single_value("Selling Settings", "validate_selling_price")`), but the agent's final answer undersold it as "can be turned off" rather than the graded fact "off by default" — coverage 40% (missing 3/5 facts, all variations on the off-by-default nuance).
- [x] **Fix (Finding B)**: `trace_impact` (`crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/trace_impact.rs`) now sorts `affected` by hop-depth (closest first) and caps the returned list at `MAX_AFFECTED = 200`, adding an honest `affected_count` (true total), `truncated` bool, and a `note` telling the agent to narrow `depth` or the symbol rather than silently truncating with no signal. Live-verified on the exact Q30 symbol: response shrank from 275,960 → 83,676 bytes (`truncated: true`, `"Showing the 200 closest of 681..."`), no longer aborts. 2 new regression tests (resolution-fallback case + a 250-node truncation case) in the same test file.
- [x] Both fixes are narrow, root-caused to a live transcript (not a guess from the aggregate score), and don't touch rerank weights, `top_k`, or `expand_hops` — consistent with this file's standing rule (Plan 18) not to touch those without dedicated measurement.
- [x] Rebuilt release binary, safely swapped `~/.local/bin/codegraph` (`rm`-then-`cp`, full stop/start poll — hit the known LTO-build-still-running-in-background gotcha along the way: an initial swap used a release binary built *before* the trace_impact.rs edit landed, caught by re-testing against the live daemon and seeing `truncated` still `false`; rebuilt and reswapped correctly). `cargo test --workspace` green (all crates, 0 failures) before and after the swap.
- [x] **Re-run through the actual harness (2026-09-04, `--task Q26,Q30`, both `codegraph` and `codegraph-nosem` arms, cost $0.27 + $0.54)**: coverage moved in the right direction on 3/4 pairs — **Q26 codegraph 0%→25%, Q26 codegraph-nosem 0%→50%, Q30 codegraph 40%→50%**; **Q30 codegraph-nosem 60%→30%** moved the wrong way (see noise-floor caveat below). Old grades for these 4 (task_id, arm) pairs were deleted from `grades.json` and re-graded fresh (not `--regrade`'d across all 150 pairs) so the LLM judge saw only the new transcripts.
- [x] **Retraction (caught same-session, before acting on it) — the "gold key_facts are wrong" claim below was wrong; the task is fine.** Original text (struck for the record, not deleted, per this file's own precedent for the Q24/Finding-3 retractions): ~~both new Q26 answers are substantively correct and more thorough than the original run... this is a benchmark-task-data bug the tool fix exposed, not a codegraph regression — Q26's key_facts/anchor_files should be corrected...~~ **What's actually true**: the question asks specifically about *"issuing or transferring stock"* — in ERPNext purpose terms, `Material Issue`/`Material Transfer` — which are exactly the two purposes **excluded** from `StockEntry.validate_batch()`'s gated list. So "no submit-time validation blocks an expired batch" is the *correct* answer for the scenario actually asked; the gold fact's conclusion was right all along, only its supporting sentence ("expiry/expired don't appear in stock_entry.py") was weak proof of a true claim. Confirmed by the grader's own reasoning on this exact pair: *"For the specific issuing/transferring scenario asked about, the candidate concludes a user can select and submit an expired batch successfully, matching the gold fact."* Both new answers concede this in their own bottom line — the code found in `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry_detail.py:88-99`, real, verified, not a fabrication) is accurate and relevant background, but the answers **led with "Yes"** on a question whose asked scenario is "No" — that's an answer-*framing* defect, not a retrieval-tool defect or a stale task. `tasks/erpnext_tasks.yaml` was correctly left untouched.
- [x] **Checked the one legitimate "our logic" candidate this raised before dropping it**: fact 2 (`get_non_expired_batches()`) was covered in codegraph-nosem's Q26 answer but missing from codegraph's. Live-queried `find_relevant_code` on both `/erpnext/mcp` and `/erpnext-nosem/mcp` with the same intent (`"filter out expired batches when suggesting or auto-selecting a batch"`): the semantic arm surfaces `get_non_expired_batches` directly in its top 8; the no-semantic agent's actual (25-turn, hit the turn cap) transcript found it too, just via a differently-worded query partway through its much longer exploration. Both arms can reach it through *some* phrasing — no retrieval asymmetry, no tool-layer bug. The difference is exploration depth/luck on that specific run, not something to chase at the tool layer, consistent with this file's standing rule against tuning retrieval without dedicated measurement.
- [x] **Net correction**: the Q26/Q30 mechanistic fixes (resolve_symbol suffix-match, trace_impact truncation) are still real, still verified, still worth having — 0%→25%/50% coverage and the tool calls that previously errored now succeed. What's retracted is only the *interpretation* that a benchmark task was broken. No further action needed on `tasks/erpnext_tasks.yaml` or on codegraph's retrieval logic for this specific gap; any future improvement here is an answer-framing/prompt concern for the harness, not a codegraph MCP-tool fix.
- [x] **Q30 stayed a genuine (if smaller) agent-reasoning gap, not a tool bug**: both new answers correctly find `validate_selling_price()`, the `is_return` skip, and that the setting "can be turned off" — but still don't state outright that it's *off by default*, the specific fact the trap is testing. Consistent with the original read: the data needed was already retrievable pre-fix (Grep alone surfaced the gating `if` line); this is about answer emphasis, not retrieval.
- [x] **The one same-direction-as-expected-but-still-a-caveat result**: `codegraph-nosem` Q30 dropping 60%→30% is a single non-deterministic agent run compared against another single non-deterministic agent run — exactly the kind of single-pair delta this file has repeatedly warned not to over-read (see the ~13pp whole-run / ~4-6pp per-question noise floor established throughout this file). Do not treat it as a regression without a repeat run; flagged, not chased.
- [x] **Full Group F (Q26-Q30) re-run across all 5 arms, 2026-09-04, ~$4.7 total** — user explicitly asked for the wider re-run after the Q26 retraction above, specifically to separate real signal from noise before drawing any further conclusion. This is the single most useful thing done in this follow-up, and the conclusion is a caution, not a win:

  | task | codegraph | graphify | code-review-graph | codegraph-nosem | codegraph-colby |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Q26 | 0.0→**37.5** | 12.5→12.5 | 12.5→**37.5** | 0.0→**12.5** | **62.5→12.5** |
  | Q27 | 75.0→**100.0** | 87.5→75.0 | 87.5→62.5 | 87.5→**100.0** | 62.5→**87.5** |
  | Q28 | 80.0→80.0 | 80.0→80.0 | 70.0→**100.0** | 80.0→90.0 | 70.0→**100.0** |
  | Q29 | 100.0→100.0 | 25.0→25.0 | 12.5→37.5 | **100.0→25.0** | 37.5→**100.0** |
  | Q30 | 40.0→50.0 | 40.0→**70.0** | 50.0→40.0 | 60.0→50.0 | 60.0→70.0 |
  | **mean** | **59.0→73.5** | 49.0→52.5 | 46.5→55.5 | **65.5→55.5** | **58.5→74.0** |

  **The headline result is not "codegraph improved 14.5pp" — it's that `codegraph-colby`, an arm this session made zero changes to, improved by a near-identical 15.5pp on the same 5 questions, while `codegraph-nosem` (also untouched by the Q26/Q30 fixes on Q27-29, and itself carrying the Q26/Q30 fixes same as `codegraph`) dropped 10pp.** Pulled `codegraph-colby`'s new Q26 answer specifically: it now scores 12.5% (down from 62.5%) because this run's answer *fabricates* a different, wrong validation mechanism entirely — a completely different failure mode from its own prior excellent, correctly-cited answer, on a tool/config that did not change between the two runs at all. That is unambiguous proof that single-question re-run variance on this harness is large enough (up to 50-75pp on an individual question, for a completely unrelated/unmodified arm) to fully explain the swings seen on `codegraph`'s own numbers too. **Verdict: the Group F mean-coverage improvement cannot be attributed to the `resolve_symbol`/`trace_impact` fixes from this evidence alone** — it's consistent with, but not distinguishable from, pure run-to-run/judge noise at this small a sample size (5 questions × 1 run). The two fixes remain justified on their own mechanistic merits (verified live, unit-tested, real bugs with real root causes) — just not provably validated at the benchmark-score level yet.
- [ ] Not yet done: a *repeated* Group F run (3+ repeats per arm, not 1) is the only way to actually separate signal from this much noise — a single 5-question pass, as just demonstrated, is not enough even to trust its own direction. `Q26`'s `key_facts`/`anchor_files` in `tasks/erpnext_tasks.yaml` were correctly left untouched (see retraction above) — no longer a candidate for change. `codegraph-nosem`'s Q29 100%→25% drop and `codegraph-colby`'s Q26 fabrication are both individually interesting failure-mode examples if anyone wants to dig into *why* a specific run degenerates, but neither was investigated further here since the headline finding (noise dominates at this sample size) already explains them structurally.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-04 full 30-question × 5-arm mechanistic audit: 2 more real MCP-tool bugs found and fixed

*User asked to review the full 30-question set across all 5 arms for real improvements — not another score comparison (already shown to be noise-dominated above), but a mechanistic audit like the Q26/Q30 one, at full scale. Grepped every `codegraph` transcript for error-shaped MCP tool results rather than reading score deltas.*

- [x] **Baseline measured**: 89 `mcp__codegraph__*` calls across the 30 real (non-benchmark-noise) transcripts; **10 (11.2%) returned an error-shaped result** (`symbol not found` / `file not indexed` / `no indexed files found under` / `ambiguous`). Tool mix: `find_relevant_code` 47, `explain_symbol` 26, `get_file_context` 10, `trace_impact` 5, `summarize_module` 1 (`find_implementations`/`watch_status`: 0 — the Plan 18 underuse finding on `find_implementations` still holds).
- [x] **Finding C (recurring, 3+ instances across different questions)**: agents guess dotted symbol names — `Class.method` (`StockController.make_gl_entries`, Q01) or a full Python import path (`erpnext.stock.doctype.stock_entry.stock_entry.StockEntry.validate`, Q26; `general_ledger.make_gl_entries`, Q06) — neither of which matches this index's `path::name` qualified-name shape or a symbol's bare `name` (which never contains `.`). All 3 failed with `symbol not found` even though the target was indexed and reachable by its own bare name.
- [x] **Finding D**: `summarize_module {"path": "erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py"}` (Q01) — a specific file, not a directory — failed with `no indexed files found under:`. Root cause: `files_in_prefix`'s relative-path pattern is `%/{prefix}/%`, which requires something *after* the prefix; a leaf file has nothing after it, so the query can never match even though the file itself is indexed. A directory input works fine (already relative-path-aware from an earlier session); only the file-shaped input silently returns empty.
- [x] **Fix C**: `resolve_symbol` (now `resolve_symbol_verbose`, `crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/mod.rs`) adds a third fallback tier — when input has no `::` and isn't an exact/suffix match, split on the last `.` and retry the bare-name lookup with just that final segment. Only tried after the more precise `::`-shaped resolution has already failed, so it never fights that path.
- [x] **Safety measure, not skipped**: a bare last-dot-segment guess can collide with an unrelated same-named symbol in a different file (`make_gl_entries` exists in `payment_entry.py`, `stock_controller.py`, and elsewhere) — silently picking one by in-degree would be exactly the kind of confident-wrong-context answer Group F targets. `resolve_symbol_verbose` now returns `(Symbol, Option<String>)`; whenever the picked match had 2+ same-named candidates (from *any* fallback tier, not just the new one — the pre-existing plain-bare-name tail gets this for free too), a `resolution_note` is attached and surfaced as a new field on `explain_symbol`'s and `trace_impact`'s JSON response (not `find_implementations`, which already treats `resolve_symbol` as best-effort and swallows errors). Live-verified this actually fires and is correct: querying the real `erpnext` index for `StockController.make_gl_entries` now resolves — but to `payment_entry.py::make_gl_entries`, a *different* file than the class name implied — with the note correctly flagging it as a same-name guess to double-check, instead of silently handing back the wrong file's source as if it were certain.
- [x] **Fix D**: `summarize_module` (`crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/summarize_module.rs`) falls back to the parent directory of a single matched file (via `file_by_path` then `files_by_suffix`) when the directory-prefix query comes back empty. Live-verified: the exact Q01 failing input now returns `file_count: 33, symbol_count: 738` for `erpnext/controllers/` instead of erroring.
- [x] 6 new regression tests (`crates/codegraph-mcp/tests/mcp_server.rs`): dotted-guess resolution (unique + ambiguous-with-note cases) for `explain_symbol`, and file-path-fallback + still-errors-on-truly-unknown-path for `summarize_module`. `cargo test --workspace` green, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Rebuilt release binary, safely swapped `~/.local/bin/codegraph` (`rm`-then-`cp`, full stop/start poll), all 4 fixes from this session (Q26/Q30 resolver + truncation, plus these 2) live-verified together against the real `erpnext` index post-deploy.
- [x] **What was checked and explicitly NOT fixed**: the remaining ~4 of 10 error instances are legitimate — `get_last_sle` (Q20) genuinely doesn't exist under that name (agent guessed wrong, not a tool bug), `file not indexed: .../delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py` (Q01) is a file that plausibly doesn't exist (agent pattern-matched from a sibling doctype's real file), and `Supplier Scorecard` (Q28) is a doctype name with a space passed to `explain_symbol` — a wrong-tool-choice (should have been `find_implementations`), not a resolver gap; a considered space-splitting fallback for this shape was deliberately not added since, unlike dots, a space-separated last token has no evidenced connection to how symbols are actually named here and risks spurious unrelated matches.
- [ ] Not yet done (correctly, per this session's own noise-floor lesson): no benchmark re-run was used to "validate" these two fixes — they're justified purely on live mechanism verification against the real index, the same standard applied to the Q26/Q30 fixes before the Group F noise finding above showed why a single-question score check would have been misleading either way. If a future re-run happens to show Q01/Q06 improve, that's corroborating, not proof; if it doesn't, that doesn't undo the fix — the mechanism itself is independently verified.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-04 investigated: why is `find_implementations` still 0/89 calls after Plan 18's Fix 1?

*User asked to investigate directly, rather than accept the 0-usage number at face value. Read all 30 tasks in `tasks/erpnext_tasks.yaml` in full (not just Plan 18's earlier keyword-scan pass) specifically looking for any question shaped like "what implements/extends/inherits from X" — `find_implementations`'s actual reason for existing per its own description ("every class, struct, or module that implements or inherits from a given interface, trait, protocol, or base class").*

- [x] **Finding: none of the 30 questions ask that.** Full read of all 30, group by group: A (5, lifecycle/data-flow tracing across doctypes), B (5, "what would changing this function affect" impact analysis — `trace_impact`'s job), C/D (10, edge-case business-rule behavior), E (5, concurrency/DB-portability mechanisms), F (5, yes/no trap questions). The closest near-misses — Q06 ("enumerate the main document types and modules that call it") and Q20 ("where is it applied? Name concrete functions and files") — are both about *callers/usage sites* of a function, not *type-hierarchy implementations* of an abstraction. Not one task in this set has an interface/trait/base-class enumeration shape.
- [x] **Corroborated by cross-arm evidence, not just an inference**: none of the 4 competitor arms even carry a dedicated "find implementations" tool at all — `graphify` (`query_graph`/`get_node`/`get_neighbors`), `code-review-graph` (`semantic_search_nodes`/`get_minimal_context`/`query_graph`), and `codegraph-colby` (single tool, `codegraph_explore`) all fold that capability, if it exists at all, into a general graph-query tool rather than a specialized one. If this benchmark actually rewarded dedicated implementation-enumeration, a specialized competitor tool doing that job would show it — none exists to check.
