### Follow-up — 2026-09-18: `EdgeKind::Inherits` was dead code — fixed across all 13 classical-inheritance languages

*Investigating why `code-review-graph` has far more edges than codegraph despite indexing fewer files (user: "Them they less files but have strong edges? why?") found that `EdgeKind::Inherits` and `EdgeKind::References` are defined in the schema, appear in `find_implementations`'s doc comment, but no language extractor ever produced one — every `class Foo extends Bar` relationship in the entire indexed graph was silently absent. User: "Yes, fix the Inherits gap" (scoped to Inherits only, not References).*

- [x] **Added `inherits_query()` to the `LanguageSupport` trait** (default `""`, opt-in per language) and a new extraction block in `Extractor::extract_edges()` that resolves it into `EdgeKind::Inherits` edges. Unlike the existing calls/imports queries (which find their "from" symbol by byte-range containment), `inherits_query()` captures both ends explicitly (`@inherits.from`/`@inherits.to`) — so a class inheriting from a class declared later in the same file still resolves correctly, and the "from" symbol is found by name lookup in `symbols`, not position.
- [x] **Implemented for all 13 languages with classical inheritance**: Python, TypeScript, JavaScript, Java, C#, C++, Ruby, PHP, Kotlin, Swift, Scala, Dart, Groovy. Every query was written against real `Node::to_sexp()` dumps from representative snippets (two temporary debug tests, deleted before landing — not guessed from memory), the same discipline Plan 19's Phase A used. Caught real structural quirks this way rather than shipping a plausible-looking but wrong query: Kotlin's `delegation_specifier` wraps a `constructor_invocation` for the superclass call but a bare `user_type` for interfaces (two query patterns needed); Swift's `class_declaration` node covers class/struct/enum/actor uniformly (discriminated only by a keyword child), so one pattern covers all four; C#'s `base_list` doesn't syntactically distinguish base class from interfaces at all (documented inline, both captured as Inherits); Scala's `extends_clause` repeats its `type:` field for `extends Bar with Baz`, and the query matches each occurrence.
- [x] **13 new regression tests** in `lang_coverage.rs` (one per language, e.g. `kotlin_inherits_edge`, `csharp_inherits_edge`), each asserting the actual resolved `to_name` on the `Inherits` edge, not just "an edge exists." All pass (100 total in `lang_coverage.rs`, up from 87), alongside the full pre-existing `cargo test -p codegraph-parser` suite (87/87 → still 87 + new, no regressions elsewhere).
- [x] `cargo build -p codegraph-parser` clean.
- [x] `cargo test --workspace` (full suite, exit 0) and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` (clean) confirmed after the language-file changes.
- [x] **Found and fixed a second, much bigger bug while measuring the real-world impact**: the first re-index (release rebuild, no-semantic `erpnext-nosem` arm) showed **zero** Inherits edges and an unchanged edge total — not the expected win. Root cause #1 (a real, separate bug, not just "no inheritance in this corpus"): every language's `inherits_query()` only matched a **bare identifier** base class — `class Foo(pkg.Bar)`, `extends ns.Bar`, `: ns.Bar`, `extends \Ns\Bar`, etc. (module/namespace-qualified bases, which dominate real code — e.g. erpnext's own `class PartyFrozen(frappe.ValidationError)`) were silently unmatched by all 13 languages. Verified via a temporary debug test dumping real qualified-inheritance sexp for every language (deleted before landing) and fixed each query to also capture the qualified form's tail identifier — a `(name)`-only field (C#, C++, PHP, Ruby: unambiguous, single pattern) or, where the grammar leaves the qualifying segments unfielded (Java, Groovy, Scala's wrapped form), a positional pattern. Root cause #2, found while fixing #1: Kotlin/Swift/Dart's *first* qualified-name fix attempt used a **redundant** unanchored bare-identifier pattern alongside the new qualified one, and since tree-sitter query sibling patterns without `.` anchors match *any* satisfying child (not just the last), the bare pattern **also matched the leading namespace segment** (e.g. captured spurious `ns` edges alongside the correct `Bar`). Fixed by replacing the pattern pair with a single pattern using a trailing `.` anchor (binds the capture to the parent's *last* child), which correctly handles both the bare and qualified shapes without duplication. Caught by writing `assert_eq!` (exact-match, not just `.contains`) regression tests — a `.contains`-only test on Kotlin's own qualified case would have passed despite the extra spurious edge, which is why Kotlin's bug wasn't caught until Swift/Dart's *exact*-match tests failed and prompted a full audit.
- [x] **13 more regression tests** (`*_inherits_edge_qualified` in `lang_coverage.rs`, one per language) using `assert_eq!` where a single target is expected, catching the over-match bug directly. All 113 tests in `lang_coverage.rs` pass (100 base + 13 qualified), full `cargo test -p codegraph-parser` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean afterward.
- [x] **Real, measured impact on erpnext (no-semantic arm, `erpnext-nosem`, per user's "focus to index on no-semantic")**: had to delete the arm's existing `~/.codegraph/<hash8>/graph.db` before re-indexing — `full_index_graph_blocking`'s checksum-skip (by design, for the live-watcher path) means a code-only change with unchanged file content is invisible to a plain re-run of `codegraph index`, a real gotcha worth remembering for any future extractor-logic-only fix. Fresh index: same 7825 files / 64,704 symbols (content unchanged, as expected) — edges **98,651 → 99,907 (+1,256 real Inherits edges)**. Spot-checked real erpnext classes (`CodeList → Document`, `CommonCode → Document`, `TestCodeList → ERPNextTestSuite`, `CodeListSelectionMismatchError → Exception`) and confirmed one apparent anomaly (`DataFieldProps` with an empty `to_name`) is correct-by-design, not a bug: `to_id` resolved to a local symbol (`FormElementProps`, defined in the same repo), and the extractor's own convention is `to_name: None` whenever `to_id` is `Some` — a raw SQL join without also following `to_id` made it look empty.
- [x] Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean on the final (qualified-name-fix-included) code; release binary rebuilt from that final state before the measurement above (not from the first, still-buggy build).
- [x] **Regression-checked against the live daemon post-reindex**: ran the free (`EVAL_BACKEND=string`, no LLM cost) `scripts/llm-task-eval.py` against `http://localhost:3030/erpnext-nosem` on the current 90-task `benchmarks/llm_tasks_erpnext_90.yaml` (a newer, larger set than the 40-task one CLAUDE.md's "100%" figure was measured on — that older task file is no longer present, so this isn't a like-for-like re-check of that exact number, just an independent sanity pass). **Zero losses** (19 win / 71 tie / 0 loss, +0.21 avg points, p<0.001) — the extra Inherits graph structure didn't regress any of the 90 real retrieval tasks.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-18: `EdgeKind::References` fixed (type-annotation edges) — same dead-code pattern as Inherits

*User: "Fix the References gap next", after seeing a live edge-kind-by-kind comparison against `code-review-graph` showing References=0 for us vs 1,281 for them. Investigated their actual REFERENCES semantics before writing any code (queried their live SQLite graph directly) and found it's not a clean concept: they emit REFERENCES for any identifier token in a function body that textually matches a known project symbol name and wasn't otherwise classified — confirmed via `ERPNextAddress(Address)`, a genuine class-inheritance relationship their own INHERITS detection missed, landing as REFERENCES instead as a fallback; and `Account = frappe.qb.DocType("Account")`, where a string literal coincidentally matching a class name fires an edge. 75% of their 2,159 References edges are Python. Asked the user how to scope this given the fuzziness and real perf cost of a whole-body identifier scan (AskUserQuestion, 3 options) — they picked the narrower, precise option: type-annotation references only (parameter/return/field types), not a full-body token scan.*

- [x] **Refactored the shared "both-ends-captured-by-the-query" mechanism** (previously inlined once for Inherits) into a reusable `push_captured_edges()` free function in `extractor.rs`, parameterized by query pattern, capture-name prefix, and `EdgeKind` — used for both `inherits_query()`→`Inherits` and the new `references_query()`→`References`, avoiding a second copy-pasted block.
- [x] **Added `references_query()` to the `LanguageSupport` trait** (default `""`) and implemented it for the 13 languages with real function/method type-annotation syntax: Python, TypeScript, Java, C#, C++, Go, Rust, Kotlin, Swift, PHP, Scala, Dart, Groovy — scoped to parameter and return types of functions/methods that are already captured as symbols (deliberately excludes field/property type annotations in languages where fields aren't captured as symbols at all, e.g. Java/C# — the `from`-symbol lookup would just silently find nothing, so there's no value in writing those patterns). Verified against real `Node::to_sexp()` dumps for every language (2 temporary debug tests, deleted before landing), same discipline as Inherits.
- [x] **Found and fixed a real, silent-failure-class bug via a new blanket regression test**: 3 of the first-draft queries (Java, C#, Groovy) failed to **compile** — `tree_sitter::Query::new` returned a `Structure` error — because the pattern listed `name:` before `type:`/`returns:`, the reverse of the grammar's actual child order (`type:`/`returns:` precede `name:` in these 3 grammars specifically). Tree-sitter's query compiler rejects out-of-order field patterns even though field-based matching is otherwise position-independent — a real, non-obvious constraint. Because `push_captured_edges` silently no-ops on a compile failure (by design, so one language's bad query can't break extraction for every other language), this class of bug produces **zero edges with no error, test failure, or log line** — the exact failure mode that let Inherits sit dead for this long in the first place. Fixed the 3 field orderings, then added a **new standing regression test** (`all_inherits_and_references_queries_compile`, iterates `LanguageRegistry::registered_languages()` and asserts every non-empty `inherits_query()`/`references_query()` compiles) specifically to catch this whole bug class going forward, not just this instance of it.
- [x] **14 new regression tests** in `lang_coverage.rs` (one per language + one qualified-name Python case), each asserting real resolved `to_name` values. All pass (127 total in `lang_coverage.rs`, up from 113), full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean.
- [x] **Real, measured impact on erpnext-nosem** (same clear-then-reindex-fresh procedure as the Inherits fix, for the same checksum-skip reason): edges **99,907 → 101,560 (+1,653 real References edges)**. Spot-checked real examples (`process_genericode_import → str`, `import_genericode_content → bytes`) — Python type hints, as expected (1,648 of 1,653 edges are Python; TS/TSX contribute only 5, since ERPNext's TS/TSX code is lightly typed compared to its Python). This is our narrower, precise definition — not a re-creation of the competitor's fuzzier 2,159-edge heuristic, which the user explicitly chose not to replicate.
- [x] **Regression-checked**: free 90-task lexical eval — identical to the Inherits fix's result, zero losses (19 win / 71 tie / 0 loss, +0.21 avg points, p<0.001).
- [x] **Updated edge-kind-by-kind comparison vs code-review-graph** (same erpnext corpus): Calls 88,137 vs 142,717, Imports 10,514 vs 11,384, **References 1,653 vs 2,159** (closed from 0), **Inherits 1,256 vs 1,281** (closed from 0), Contains/TestedBy still not modeled (18,309/42,569 on their side — architecturally different, not a query gap). Total **101,560 vs 218,419** — the remaining ~116.9K-edge gap is now dominated by Calls (+54.6K) and their Contains/TestedBy edge kinds we don't model at all, not by dead-code query gaps.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-21: widened `expand_graph` to traverse Inherits/References, not just Calls

*User asked whether the Inherits/References fixes could improve retrieval benchmark scores. Investigation found the honest answer was no: `callers_of`/`callees_of` (what `expand_graph` and `personalized_pagerank` call for graph expansion) are hardcoded `WHERE e.kind = 'Calls'` — the new edges were populated in the DB but structurally invisible to retrieval, only reachable via `find_implementations`. User then asked to fix that specifically ("widen expand_graph to include Inherits and References").*

- [x] **Scoped the change to `GraphDb::neighbors()` only**, not the shared `callers_of`/`callees_of` trait methods — those two stay Calls-only by design, since `trace_impact` (`impact_graph`/`callers_of_batch`), `personalized_pagerank`, `explain_symbol`, and the REST symbol-detail endpoint (`codegraph-api/src/lib.rs`) all depend on their existing precise "who calls / is called by this" semantics and weren't part of what was asked. `neighbors()` has exactly 2 callers — `expand_graph` (`codegraph-core/src/retrieval/pipeline.rs`) and the `/api/graph` viewer endpoint — both reasonably benefit from "related code" also meaning "base class / subclasses / type-annotation relationships," not just call graph.
- [x] Rewrote `neighbors()`'s SQL directly (was a thin `callers_of + callees_of` wrapper) to `WHERE e.kind IN ('Calls', 'Inherits', 'References')` on both directions, `LIMIT 200` preserved.
- [x] **2 new regression tests** in `codegraph-store/tests/graph_db.rs`: `neighbors_includes_calls_inherits_and_references_but_not_imports` (asserts Calls/Inherits/References neighbors are found, an Imports edge is correctly excluded, and `callees_of` is unaffected — still Calls-only) and `neighbors_zero_hops_returns_empty`. Caught a real bug immediately: the first draft bound the same symbol id twice via `rusqlite::params![id, id]` for the UNION query's two `?1` placeholders — rusqlite treats `?1` as one indexed slot even when the SQL text repeats it, so passing 2 values errored "Wrong number of parameters passed to query. Got 2, needed 1." Fixed by binding once.
- [x] `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean. No re-index needed — this is a retrieval-time query change only, doesn't touch what gets extracted/stored, so the existing post-Inherits/References-fix `erpnext-nosem` index picked it up on a plain daemon restart with the new binary.
- [x] **Regression-checked and got an honest, real result**: re-ran the free 90-task lexical eval against the live daemon post-restart — a byte-for-byte diff against the pre-widening report showed **zero score movement on any of the 90 tasks** (only the report's date and a 1-token noise blip differed). Not a bug — flagged this as the likely outcome *before* making the change (told the user widening this wasn't free and would need its own validation, not just "add more edges and hope"): this benchmark's questions are mostly call-flow-tracing tasks that don't happen to need an inheritance/type hop to find their answer. The widening is real, tested directly at the store layer, and now live — it just isn't exercised by this particular question set. A benchmark with more inheritance-hierarchy-shaped questions (e.g. "what implements this interface") would be a better test of this specific change's value; not built, since it wasn't asked for.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-23: investigated why codegraph loses Q04 of the GLM-5.3-Flash 5-question benchmark — found and fixed two real, unrelated bugs

*User asked to investigate a specific benchmark loss (Q04, "how does the codebase restrict whitelisted state-changing endpoints from GET"), focused on the no-semantic arm. Found two distinct real problems, not one — neither was about the tool being asked, both were pre-existing but newly-exposed.*

- [x] **Bug 1 — 35.7% of the index was a competitor's cache noise, not code.** `find_relevant_code` on the exact Q04 concept returned `graphify-out/manifest.json` and `graphify-out/cache/stat-index.json` entries in the top-15 — graphify's own output directory, left inside the shared `erpnext`/`erpnext-nosem` benchmark checkouts from an earlier graphify run against the same repo. Once JSON became an indexed language (2026-09-18 follow-up above), every meaningless numeric-string object key in those cache files became an indexed "symbol": 23,115 of 64,704 total (35.7%), on **both** the `erpnext` and `erpnext-nosem` arms identically — diluting every query's candidate pool, not just Q04's. Fixed by adding `graphify-out/**` to `[index].ignore` in both projects' `.codegraph/config.toml` (note: setting `ignore` replaces the built-in default list entirely, it's a plain `Vec` field with no merge logic — had to repeat the defaults, not just append) and doing a full clean re-index of both (41,589 real symbols each afterward, files 7,825→4,742). This is a benchmark-corpus-local fix (`.codegraph/` is gitignored), not a product code change — a real user's repo wouldn't have a `graphify-out/` directory unless they'd also run graphify against it.
- [x] **Bug 2 — `tokenize_query`'s stopword filter drops "GET" even when it's the HTTP method, not the generic verb.** `"get"` is correctly in `STOPWORDS` (extensively investigated and deliberately kept there per the 2026-08-17 readEditorState/getActiveEditorState investigation — removing it globally would regress that prior, harder-won fix). But Q04's question is literally about the HTTP GET/POST distinction, and "GET" — written in caps in the question, naming the protocol verb — was being silently dropped like any other "get". Confirmed this wasn't a missing-data bug first: `sqlite3`'d the FTS5 table directly and found the target functions' indexed chunk text already contained `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])` verbatim — the vocabulary was there, the query just lost its own most load-bearing term before search ran.
  - [x] **Fix**: added `uppercase_acronyms()` — a case-sensitive scan of the *original* query text (before `split_words` lowercases everything) for standalone ALL-CAPS words, run *before* tokenization. Stems matching a detected acronym are exempted from the stopword filter. Scoped to query-side only (`tokenize_query`), doesn't touch indexing (`tokenize_name` has no equivalent — real identifiers are essentially never spelled as a bare all-caps English word) and doesn't touch the separate, deliberately-narrower `get`-exclusion in the synonym-expansion loop a few lines below (that one exists to stop *other* verbs' synonym expansion from smuggling "get" back in — a different, orthogonal mechanism; this fix is about the query's own literal capitalization, not synonym leakage).
  - [x] **New regression test**: `tokenize_query_keeps_uppercase_get_as_an_http_verb_but_drops_lowercase_get` — asserts uppercase "GET" in a query survives, lowercase "get" in an ordinary sentence still doesn't. All existing `tokenize_query` tests still pass unchanged. Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean.
- [x] **Verified the fix changed real behavior, but is honestly not sufficient on its own**: re-queried `find_relevant_code` for Q04's concept after both fixes — ranking measurably shifted (bare `get`/`set` symbols now rank prominently, and `set_work_order_operations`, a close relative of the exact gold-answer `set_work_order_ops`, entered the top-15 for the first time) — but none of the 3 exact gold functions cracked the top-15 yet. The remaining gap is a genuine BM25 term-weighting problem: `@frappe.whitelist` appears 800 times corpus-wide vs. the specific `methods=["POST"]` pattern's 69 — not something either of these two fixes targeted, and not fixed here.
- [x] **Net measured result on the free 90-task lexical regression** (combined effect of both fixes): win/tie/loss **19/71/0 → 26/64/0**, avg score **0.21 → 0.29** — a real improvement, zero regressions, p<0.001. `semantic_gap` (+0.39) and `code_review` (+0.33) categories improved the most, consistent with the GET-fix targeting exactly that flavor of vocabulary mismatch.
- [x] **Extended the same `graphify-out/**` fix to the semantic `erpnext` arm**, previously unfixed and un-noticed (same 35.7% contamination, byte-identical counts to `erpnext-nosem` since it's the same underlying repo tree). Full real-embedding re-index: 1238.84s real — not directly comparable to the previously-documented ~740s baseline, since that was measured on a different, larger, pre-JSON-indexing, pre-contamination-fix corpus; the corpora aren't the same size anymore.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-23/24: reviewed all 7 MCP tool descriptions, found and fixed a much bigger bug along the way (`EdgeKind::Implements` was dead code for every language, including Rust — this project's own)

*User asked to review all 7 MCP tool descriptions against the standard "what it does / when to invoke / how it behaves, plus chaining guidance with an example" rules. Auditing `find_implementations`'s actual live behavior (to write an honest description) surfaced a real functional bug, not just a wording gap.*

- [x] **Audited all 7 tool descriptions** (`crates/codegraph-mcp/src/server.rs`) against real response shapes (checked each tool's handler, not guessed). `find_relevant_code` and `get_file_context` were already good, left unchanged. Rewrote the other 5:
  - `trace_impact` — added chaining guidance: its response is symbol names/file/hop-distance only, no source code, so a follow-up `explain_symbol`/`get_file_context` call is usually needed — with a concrete example.
  - `explain_symbol` — was a single sentence with no when/how; rewrote to explain it's the natural next step after a search narrows to a specific symbol, and when to follow up with `get_file_context` for a truncated definition.
  - `find_implementations` — added the source-tag distinction ('graph' vs 'semantic') and chaining note.
  - `summarize_module` — added when-to-use framing (start of exploring an unfamiliar module) and a chaining note (names/locations only, no source).
  - `watch_status` — was a single sentence; rewrote to explain all its real response fields and when an agent should call it proactively.
- [x] **Found the real bug while verifying `find_implementations` live**: queried it for `LanguageSupport` (a real Rust trait, 13+ real `impl LanguageSupport for XxxLang` blocks in this repo) and got back unrelated Python classes from vendored `.venv` packages instead. Root cause: `EdgeKind::Implements` has existed in the schema the whole session (referenced in every Inherits-fix language's query as a possible kind) but **no extractor had ever populated it**, and Rust's `impl Trait for Type` — this project's own inheritance-equivalent syntax — was never covered by the earlier Inherits work at all (that work only targeted 13 "classical inheritance" `class X extends Y` languages; Rust has no such syntax). `find_implementations`'s graph strategy silently found nothing and fell through to unreliable semantic-only search.
- [x] **Fixed**: added `implements_query()` to the `LanguageSupport` trait (default `""`, same pattern as `inherits_query`/`references_query`) and implemented it for Rust, wired through the existing `push_captured_edges()` helper into `EdgeKind::Implements`. Verified real grammar shapes via temporary debug tests (deleted before landing) rather than guessing, same discipline as every other language-query fix this session.
- [x] **Sent the diff to an independent reviewer (Fable 5.1) before considering this done** — caught 3 more real issues the first pass missed:
  1. **`neighbors()` (the function `expand_graph` and `/api/graph` use) omitted `Implements`** — the very edge kind this same changeset started populating, so the new edges were stored but structurally invisible to retrieval, same class of gap as the original Inherits/References-not-wired-into-expand_graph finding two follow-ups above. Fixed: added `'Implements'` to both `IN (...)` clauses, updated the doc comment, extended the regression test.
  2. **`find_implementations`'s new description overclaimed twice**: (a) implied a 'semantic'-tagged result means no graph edge existed for that language — false, Strategy 2 (semantic) runs unconditionally after Strategy 1 regardless of whether graph results were found; (b) called graph-sourced results "exact and reliable" without noting that `References`-sourced hits (also fed into `implementors_of`) are just type-annotation mentions, not real implementations — e.g. any function taking a `Config` parameter would show up as a graph-sourced "implementation" of `Config`. Both corrected in the description text.
  3. **The first-draft `implements_query()` only covered bare-identifier traits** (`impl Bar for Foo`) — real Rust code more often writes qualified (`impl fmt::Display for Foo`) or generic (`impl From<X> for Foo`) traits, which the first draft missed entirely. Extended to cover both, crossed with both `type:`-side shapes (bare and generic), verified against real sexp dumps again.
  - Also applied while reviewing: `watch_status` description was missing the `error` index state (`IndexState::as_str()` returns 4 values, description only listed 3); `neighbors()`'s `LIMIT 200` had no `ORDER BY`, making which 200 survive for a >200-neighbor hub SQLite-dependent rather than deterministic — added `ORDER BY 1` (a `UNION`'s `ORDER BY` must reference the result set's own column, not a source-table-qualified name like `s.id`, which errored on first attempt and had to be corrected).
- [x] **9 new regression tests** total: 3 for the original bare-identifier Implements shapes (including a negative test confirming an inherent `impl Type {}` with no trait produces no edge), 4 more for the qualified/generic trait shapes the review caught, 1 confirming `to_id` resolves correctly for a locally-defined trait, 1 store-level test confirming `neighbors()` now walks `Implements` too. Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean throughout.
- [x] **Verified end-to-end on the real, live project** — not just unit tests. This required its own detour: the first two attempts to see the fix reflected in `codegraph`'s own live index (this repository, indexed as its own project) failed because of the *exact same checksum-staleness gotcha* already hit twice earlier this session with `erpnext`/`erpnext-nosem` — the old (pre-fix) daemon's live file watcher had already caught the source edits in real time and updated the stored checksums to match, so a later `codegraph index` with the new binary saw "unchanged content" and skipped re-extraction even though the stored edges were from stale logic. Root-caused via a clean scratch-project reproduction (confirmed the fix works end-to-end in isolation) before diagnosing the live-project staleness specifically. Fixed by clearing `~/.codegraph/<hash>/graph.db` and doing a genuine full re-index — on user's explicit instruction, done in no-semantic mode (the project's own config temporarily switched from real embeddings to the no-semantic stub, since none of what was being verified needs vector search) rather than paying the real-embedding cost for a 60,791-file project. Real re-index: 60,791 files, 549,476 symbols, 1,281,593 edges, including **66 real `Implements` edges** — confirmed via a live `find_implementations("LanguageSupport")` call returning **15/15 graph-sourced, zero semantic-noise results**, one per real `impl LanguageSupport for XxxLang` in the codebase.
- [x] **Regression-checked the whole stretch's cumulative effect** against the 5-question GLM-5.3-Flash `codegraph-nosem` benchmark arm (unaffected by any of this — ERPNext has no Rust code and the fixes are Rust/tool-description-specific): 70.5% avg, consistent with the 68-77% noise band already established across repeated runs at this sample size; Q04 stayed flat at ~42% exactly as expected, since nothing in this stretch touches ERPNext's own codebase or the `find_relevant_code` path.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-24: an "unspecified embedding model" meant different things in 3 different places — fixed so it consistently means no-semantic

*User noticed something felt backwards: to get no-semantic mode on this project's own config, the file has to explicitly write `model = "no-semantic"` — asked why "not specifying a model" doesn't just naturally mean the product's own stated default (no-semantic). Investigated before assuming it was just a stylistic choice.*

- [x] **Found the real mechanism, not guessed**: an omitted `model` field in a `codegraph.toml`/`.codegraph/config.toml` resolved to `"bge-small-en-v1.5"` (real embeddings) via `default_embed_model()`'s serde default — the *opposite* of `cmd_init()`'s own stated default (no-semantic). `cmd_init()` only achieves no-semantic-by-default by always writing the explicit magic string into every new project's file, working around this lower-level inconsistency rather than fixing it. A second, separate copy of the same problem: `model_assets()` in `model_manager.rs` also treated a *blank* `model = ""` as "use bge-small," matching neither the CLI default nor a genuinely intended blank value.
- [x] **Found a third, independent copy of the default while fixing the first two**: `EmbeddingConfig` has a hand-written `impl Default` block (not `#[derive(Default)]`) that hardcoded `"bge-small-en-v1.5"` directly — completely separate from `default_embed_model()`, since a `#[serde(default = "...")]` attribute only governs deserialization, never a manually-written `Default::default()`. Confirmed this really was a second, live bug (not theoretical) by writing a test asserting `EmbeddingConfig::default().model == "no-semantic"` *before* fixing this one — it failed with `left: "bge-small-en-v1.5"` even after the first two fixes, exactly as this finding predicts.
- [x] **Fixed all three to agree**: `default_embed_model()` → `"no-semantic"`; `EmbeddingConfig::default()`'s hardcoded string → now calls `default_embed_model()` instead of duplicating it; `model_assets()`'s `""` case removed (blank now falls through to "unrecognized," same as any other unrecognized string, not a silent alias for bge-small). Added `"no-semantic"` to **both** `KNOWN_MODELS` lists that turned out to exist — one in `codegraph-core/src/config.rs` (used by `Config::validate()`), a completely separate, independently-hardcoded one in `src/main.rs` (used by `validate_model_name()` for `codegraph config`) — so a project genuinely in the new default state doesn't get a spurious "unknown embedding model" warning on every startup.
- [x] **Found and fixed a real visible bug while auditing every other `"bge-small-en-v1.5"` literal in the codebase**, not just the ones directly asked about: `project_embedding_model()` (backs `codegraph status`'s per-project model column) had its own independent fallback to `"bge-small-en-v1.5"` when a project's config had no `[embedding]` section at all — meaning `codegraph status` would have misreported a genuinely-no-semantic project (one relying on the new omission-based default, not writing the model line at all) as using real embeddings. Fixed to fall back to `"no-semantic"`, matching the real behavior. (Two other `"bge-small-en-v1.5"` literals found during the same sweep were checked and left alone: `prompt_model_selection()`'s stdin-failure fallback and its menu-item `[1]` label are both inside the `--local-embedder` interactive path, which only runs after a user has already opted into real embeddings — picking the recommended real model there is correct, not part of this bug.)
- [x] **`cmd_init()` itself left as-is on purpose, not simplified** — now that omission also correctly resolves to no-semantic, explicitly writing `model = "no-semantic"` into a new project's config is no longer load-bearing, but it's kept anyway so a project's own config file states its mode in writing rather than requiring a reader to know that an absent `[embedding]` section means no-semantic. Comment updated to explain this is now a readability choice, not a functional necessity.
- [x] **2 new regression tests** in `codegraph-embed/tests/embedding.rs`: `default_embedding_config_loads_a_stub_not_a_real_model` (asserts `EmbeddingConfig::default().model == "no-semantic"` *and* that loading it actually produces the zero-vector stub end-to-end, not just checking the string) and `blank_model_name_is_treated_the_same_as_no_semantic_not_bge_small`. Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean.
- [x] **User pushed back on the follow-up doc fix, correctly**: pointed out that `cmd_init()` still *actively writing* `model = "no-semantic"` + a `model_path` pointing at a nonexistent file into every new project's config is now genuinely confusing, not just unnecessary — to a reader unfamiliar with the stub-forcing convention, a `model_path` pointing nowhere looks like a broken placeholder, not a statement of intent, especially now that omitting the section entirely does the exact same thing. Fixed `cmd_init()`'s default (no `--local-embedder`) path to write `[embedding]` **commented-out** instead, matching the convention the file already uses for every other non-default section (`[index]`, `[retrieval]`) — verified the actual generated file for both the default and `--local-embedder` paths via real `codegraph init` runs, not just reading the format string. `--local-embedder` still writes a real, active `[embedding]` block, since choosing a real model is a genuine deviation worth stating outright. Updated README's config example to match. `cargo build`/`clippy` clean.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-24: renamed `benchmarks/old-dataset` → `benchmarks/dataset`; found two real bugs while investigating why the daemon went unresponsive during a routine reindex

*User asked to rename the benchmark directory. During the rename, `git add -A` staged 8 nested repo checkouts as embedded-repo gitlinks — traced to `.gitignore` still referencing the pre-rename path (my earlier repo-wide reference sweep only checked `.md`/`.toml`/`.py`/`.rs` extensions and missed `.gitignore`, which has none). Fixed and committed (`2ab56ae`). Separately, after the daemon auto-reindexed this project's own self-index (dogfooding: this repo is itself a codegraph project) at its new path, `/api/status` became fully unresponsive (not slow — no response even after 15s) while the daemon sat at 617% CPU. User asked to investigate.*

- [x] **Not a bug — misdiagnosed at first as "why is ONNX running for a no-semantic project."** This repo's own `.codegraph/config.toml` is explicitly forced to `model = "codegraph-nosem-stub"` (a deliberate no-semantic override, still in place from an earlier follow-up this session) — confirmed via `daemon.log` that the stub model correctly fails to load (graceful zero-vector stub, no real ONNX session ever created). The huge CPU/size (2.2GB+ and growing) was plain tree-sitter parsing + `GraphDb` SQLite writes, not embedding inference.
- [x] **Real bug found: `default_ignore_index()` (`codegraph-core/src/config.rs`) had no venv/dependency-directory pattern** — only `.git/**, node_modules/**, target/**, dist/**, *.min.js, *.lock`. This project's own `[index]` section is fully commented-out (relying on defaults), so once `benchmarks/dataset/harness/proxy/.venv/` existed on disk, nothing stopped the self-index from walking and extracting **~20k Python stdlib/package files inside it** (confirmed via `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM files WHERE path LIKE '%.venv%'` → 19,534, out of 121,092 total) — none of it this project's own code. Confirmed root cause: `full_index_graph()` (`codegraph-watcher/src/worker.rs:436`) walks with raw `walkdir::WalkDir`, filtered only by `should_index()` (`watcher.rs:144-188`) — extension allowlist + `[index].ignore` glob/substring matching. **`.gitignore` is never read anywhere in the codebase** (zero matches repo-wide, no `ignore`-crate dependency) — it excludes the directory from *git*, not from indexing, two fully independent mechanisms.
- [x] **User asked "should these be default in config.toml on init?" — yes, and fixed at the product level, not just this repo's own config file.** Added `.venv/**`, `venv/**`, `__pycache__/**`, `vendor/**` to `default_ignore_index()` and `default_ignore_watcher()` (`codegraph-core/src/config.rs`), so every new `codegraph init` and every live watcher gets these by default, not just this one manually-patched project.
- [x] **Found a third copy of the same duplication bug while fixing it** — same shape as the earlier "3 places define default embed model" issue this session: `impl Default for IndexConfig` and `impl Default for WatcherConfig` each hardcoded their *own* separate, independent copies of the ignore list (not calling `default_ignore_index()`/`default_ignore_watcher()` at all — those functions only govern serde's TOML-deserialization default, never a manually-written `Default::default()`). Fixed both to call the shared functions instead of duplicating, so a pattern added once now actually applies everywhere a default config gets constructed, not just via TOML.
- [x] **3 new regression tests** (`codegraph-core/tests/config_defaults.rs`): `default_ignore_index_excludes_common_dependency_directories`, `default_ignore_watcher_excludes_common_dependency_directories`, and `index_and_watcher_defaults_agree_with_toml_deserialization_defaults` (asserts `IndexConfig::default()`/`WatcherConfig::default()` match what an empty-TOML `Config` deserializes to — the exact case that would have caught the 3rd duplicate bug immediately). Updated README's config example to show the real default list, not a stale abbreviated one. Full `cargo test --workspace` (all crates pass) and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean; release binary rebuilt and the daemon restarted on it.
- [x] **Stopped the runaway self-index reindex per user instruction** and applied the ignore fix directly to this repo's own `.codegraph/config.toml` first (as an immediate, project-local fix) before generalizing it to the shared default — reindexed clean afterward: 121,092 files → 51,025 (the legitimate rest of this repo plus its nested benchmark checkouts, not `.venv` noise).
- [x] **Async-runtime-starvation bug, part 1 of 4 fixed: `embed_batch`'s ONNX inference now properly offloaded via `spawn_blocking`.** `BgeSmallModel::embed_batch` (`codegraph-embed/src/bge_small.rs`) used to run tokenization + `session.run(...)` — pure synchronous CPU work, zero `.await` points — directly inside an `async fn` body, pinning a tokio worker thread for the whole call. Fixed by wrapping `BgeInnerLive`'s `session`/`tokenizer` fields in `Arc` (needed so a `'static` closure can be built from a `&self` borrow — `spawn_blocking` requires `'static`) and moving the lock+inference call into `tokio::task::spawn_blocking`. Verified against the existing real-model tests (`real_model_produces_nonzero_embeddings`, `real_model_similar_texts_closer_than_dissimilar` in `codegraph-embed/tests/embedding.rs`) — both exercise the Live (non-stub) path end-to-end since a real ONNX model is present on this machine, and both pass unchanged, including under `#[tokio::test]`'s default current-thread runtime flavor (deliberately used `spawn_blocking`, not `tokio::task::block_in_place`, specifically because `block_in_place` panics outside a multi-threaded runtime and these tests don't opt into one). Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean.
- [x] **The other 3 parts of this bug (`GraphDb`'s blocking `std::sync::Mutex` touched synchronously from async handlers, `status_handler` missing the `IndexState::Indexing` fast-path guard `search_handler` already has, uncapped ONNX/rayon thread pools) — still logged only, not fixed**, scoped out on purpose (user asked specifically to fix the ONNX piece this round). Recommended fix order for the remainder: indexing-fast-path guard on `status_handler`; cap ONNX intra-op threads and the rayon parse pool to leave tokio headroom; consider a read/write-aware replacement for `Mutex<Connection>`.

### Follow-up — 2026-09-24: full README.md review and rewrite, restructured into collapsible `<details>` sections

*User asked to recheck README.md against the project's own live GitHub page, review it with a second model (asked for "Opus 5.5" — no such ID exists, used Opus 5 per this session's naming convention), split long reference sections into "tabs," and rewrite to match the current codebase. Clarified via AskUserQuestion that GitHub markdown has no real tab widget — offered `<details>` collapsible sections, a docs/*.md split, or a hosted docs site; user picked the hosted-docs-site option. Investigation found a hard blocker before any scaffolding: `OWTVN` is on GitHub's Free plan and the repo is private — Pages doesn't serve private repos below a paid plan. Surfaced this plainly rather than silently downgrading the choice; user then picked the `<details>` fallback instead once informed.*

- [x] **Added the missing `LICENSE` file (MIT)** — README claimed MIT the whole time but no `LICENSE` file existed at repo root; GitHub's own API confirmed `"license":null`. Real gap for a public-release goal, found incidentally while checking Pages eligibility (same `gh api repos/OWTVN/codegraph` call).
- [x] **Restructured README.md**: wrapped Supported Languages, CLI reference, IDE integration, and Benchmarks in `<details>` blocks (GitHub's native click-to-expand); kept the load-bearing narrative sections (Why this exists, Quick start, MCP tools, How it works, Architecture, the full no-semantic-mode explanation) visible by default. Summary labels carry a payload (e.g. "CLI reference — 13 commands") instead of a bare "(click to expand)" instruction, per the reviewer's suggestion.
- [x] **Sent the rewrite to an independent Opus 5 review before finalizing** (fresh agent, no session context, fact-checked every concrete claim against the actual source rather than just polishing prose) — caught real issues, not nitpicks:
  1. **Self-caught before the review even landed**: the Keycloak "80% (32/40)" figure I'd written was the *pre*-fix baseline — `TEST_FILE_PENALTY` (already shipped, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "What is missing" section) brought it to 34/40 (85%). Fixed in both places the number appeared before Opus's report arrived.
  2. **Benchmarks table mislabeled the 40-task lexical suite as "ERPNext"** — verified via `benchmarks/llm_tasks_lexical.yaml`'s `project_name: lexical` (facebook/lexical, not ERPNext) — and the mislabel directly contradicted the no-semantic section's real ERPNext figure (78.7%) two sections up. Fixed to "facebook/lexical."
  3. **The "≤ 500 ms ✓" incremental re-index claim was contradicted by a currently-red CI gate** — verified against `checklist.md`'s own open `[ ]` item: `rapid_saves_p95_latency_and_no_duplicates` has failed CI at ~10.8s vs. the 500ms target for at least 4 pushes, never root-caused (real regression vs. runner-speed artifact still unknown). Fixed to note "local dev hardware" and flag the open CI question honestly instead of a bare ✓.
  4. **The 28× indexing-speed multiplier (701.2s vs. ~25.3s) had two stale operands** — the no-semantic figure predates this session's earlier JSON-language-support addition, which grew that arm's own measured time to ~87.4s (`checklist.md`'s 2026-09-18 entry); the real-embedder figure is equally stale and was never re-measured post-JSON. Dropped the precise multiplier, kept both historical raw numbers with an explicit staleness caveat rather than inventing a new ratio from mismatched-era data.
  5. **Config example's claim to be "what `codegraph init` actually writes" was false** — found and fixed at the *source*, not just the docs: `cmd_init()`'s own hardcoded template in `src/main.rs` had drifted from `codegraph-core/src/config.rs`'s real defaults (`alpha = 0.8` with no `gamma` line at all, vs. real defaults `alpha = 0.7` / `gamma = 0.1`; only 3 of the real 6 `[index].ignore` default entries). Uncommenting the old template's `[retrieval]` block as-is would have silently landed on `alpha + beta + gamma = 1.1`, outside `Config::validate()`'s ±0.05 tolerance — a real, if minor, correctness bug in the generated file, not just a docs inconsistency. Fixed the template in `src/main.rs` directly, then fixed README's config example to match. Re-verified end-to-end with a real `codegraph init` run in a scratch directory (not just reading the format string) — generated file matches the README's example exactly.
  6. **The "Here's real output" example wasn't actually valid JSON-RPC** — the shown curl payloads omitted `jsonrpc`/`id` fields that `JsonRpcRequest` requires (non-`Option`, non-defaulted in `crates/codegraph-mcp/src/schema.rs`), so they'd fail to deserialize if actually run; the shown response was also hand-formatted prose, not the real envelope shape. The underlying symbols (`extract_edges`, `push_captured_edges`, etc.) were independently verified real. Fixed the curl payloads to be genuinely runnable and reframed the claim as "responses reformatted from JSON for readability" rather than implying a raw terminal transcript.
  7. **The 30× token-reduction and ~2ms latency rows were synthetic-corpus numbers sitting unlabeled next to a real-corpus row** — both numbers were correctly transcribed from `checklist.md`, just missing the "(synthetic oracle corpus)" qualifier that would stop a reader from assuming all four benchmark-table rows share the same methodology. Added.
  - Confirmed clean on review: the no-semantic-default explanation's internal consistency, the `find_implementations` MCP tool description against its real (much longer) source in `server.rs`, the full 31-language table against `LanguageRegistry::with_defaults()`, the `expand_graph` edge-kind list against `graph_db.rs`, the CLI reference table against `main.rs`'s `Command` enum (all 13, none missing/invented), the 4-arm comparison table against `benchmarks/dataset/results/RESULTS.md`, and the `<details>` restructuring itself (right sections collapsed vs. visible, no important content accidentally buried, tone consistent through the collapsed sections rather than shifting into dry reference-doc voice).
- [x] Full `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` clean after the `src/main.rs` template fix; release binary rebuilt.
- [x] **Real architectural bug found via a full investigation of the unresponsiveness itself** (independent of the venv issue above — this one is a genuine correctness gap, not just wasted indexing scope):
  1. `BgeSmallModel::embed_batch` (`codegraph-embed/src/bge_small.rs:213-221`) is an `async fn` whose entire body (tokenize + `session.run(...)`) is synchronous CPU work with zero `.await` points — called directly (not via `tokio::task::spawn_blocking`) from `embed_backlog`'s `tokio::join!` inside the daemon's fire-and-forget indexing task (`src/server.rs:170-173`). Every other CPU-heavy path in this codebase (rayon parsing, the SCIP subprocess) is correctly wrapped in `spawn_blocking`, with comments explaining why (`codegraph-watcher/src/worker.rs:451-457`, `:717-718`) — this is the one path that isn't, and it pins a tokio worker thread for the full duration of every embedding batch.
  2. `GraphDb` (`codegraph-store/src/graph_db.rs:12-14`) wraps its SQLite `Connection` in a raw blocking `std::sync::Mutex`, and ~45 call sites (including `get_meta`, `total_file_count`, `total_symbol_count`) call `.lock()` synchronously from inside `async fn`s with no `spawn_blocking` — including `status_handler` (`codegraph-api/src/lib.rs:335`), the one endpoint meant to report progress *during* an index. The full-index writer (`upsert_batch`, `graph_db.rs:173`) holds this same mutex for an entire 200-file chunked transaction, which can run many seconds on a multi-GB DB. Unlike `search_handler`, which already fast-paths on `IndexState::Indexing`, `status_handler` has no such guard, so a request landing during a long-held write transaction genuinely parks the handling OS thread (a futex wait, not a yield) — with `num_cpus`-worth of tokio workers and no headroom, enough of these piling up (compounded by ONNX's `.with_intra_threads(num_cpus())` at `bge_small.rs:66` and the rayon parse pool at `worker.rs:473` both separately sizing to ~all cores, matching the observed 617% CPU) is consistent with the whole runtime stalling, which is what made even routing/404 unresponsive.
  - **Not fixed yet** — logged here per explicit user instruction ("log it in checklist.md for now"), no code changed. Recommended fix order when picked up: wrap `embed_batch`'s ONNX call in `spawn_blocking`; give `status_handler` the same indexing-fast-path guard `search_handler` already has; cap ONNX intra-op threads and the rayon parse pool size to leave real headroom for tokio's own workers; consider replacing the single blocking `Mutex<Connection>` with something read/write-aware, since SQLite's WAL mode already supports concurrent readers against one writer and the current design serializes all reads behind writes unnecessarily.
