## Plan 22 — Retrieval hygiene and agent-facing signals
*Ref: [plans/22-retrieval-hygiene-and-agent-signals.md](plans/22-retrieval-hygiene-and-agent-signals.md)*
*Status: planned, not started — five techniques identified during external research (2026-09-27), each re-verified against this codebase and our three benchmark corpora before tiering*

### Tier 1 — additive, no ranking change
- [x] Item 2 — per-result `possibly_stale` flag (+ one top-level `note`) on `find_relevant_code`/`explain_symbol`/`find_implementations`/`get_file_context`, emitted only when true. Stat-based per result file: missing file, mtime > `files.indexed_at`, size ≠ `byte_size`, or `checksum == 0` pending sentinel. Real window is batch processing (repo-wide `resolve_edges` + FTS flush, ~10.8s measured in CI's rapid-saves test), not the 50ms debounce; `SourceLoader` can slice new disk content with old spans in that window. Response-schema change only, still 7 tools. **Done 2026-09-27**: `is_possibly_stale()` helper in `crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/mod.rs`, wired into all 4 tools. 6 new unit tests (fresh/deleted/size-changed/mtime-changed/checksum-0/unindexed-path), `cargo build/test/clippy --workspace` clean. Live end-to-end check against the running daemon's `lexical` project (real corpus, not a fixture): baseline `get_file_context` on `packages/lexical-text/src/rootTextContent.ts` had no `possibly_stale` field; appended a line to the real file, immediately re-queried (same second) → `possibly_stale: true` + note fired; waited for the watcher to catch up (`watch_status` → `state: fresh`, `pending_files: 0`) → flag cleared on the next query. File restored to original content immediately after (`git diff` on the lexical repo clean, zero corpus pollution). **Real bug found and fixed the same day** (via advisor review before starting Tier 2): the watcher's checksum-match "unchanged, skip re-parse" branch — both the live single-file path (`IndexWorker::index_path_graph_only`) and the parallel full-index path (`full_index_graph_blocking`) — never bumped `files.indexed_at`, so a file whose disk mtime moves without its content changing (`git checkout`/`rebase`/`stash`, a fresh clone, a CI checkout) stayed `mtime > indexed_at` **permanently** — `possibly_stale: true` forever, confirmed live (touched a real lexical file with no content change, watcher's next batch ran and found nothing to re-index, flag never cleared). Fixed with a new `GraphDb::touch_files_indexed_at()` (single transaction, batched per full-index chunk so it doesn't add per-file connection-mutex contention to the common re-index-mostly-unchanged-repo case) called from both skip branches. 2 new regression tests (`unchanged_content_rewrite_bumps_indexed_at` in `codegraph-watcher`'s `incremental_sync.rs`, `full_index_rerun_bumps_indexed_at_for_unchanged_files` in a new `full_index_touch.rs`). Corpus-wide scan (all 3 corpora, direct `stat` against every indexed file's DB row) confirmed **zero** stale false-positives before *and* after the fix on the already-settled DBs (the bug requires an mtime-only touch after the last real index to manifest, which hadn't happened to these corpora yet) — live-reproduced on lexical specifically to catch it, then re-verified clean post-fix via the daemon's own startup full-reindex (which itself exercises the fixed full-index skip path across all 3 corpora at once — erpnext 4,739/keycloak 10,043/lexical 1,373 files, all settled to `fresh` with matching pre-fix file/symbol counts, no data loss). Also re-verified `find_relevant_code` and `explain_symbol` (not just `get_file_context`) independently fire the flag against a real live edit, closing a second advisor-flagged gap (different `file_path` field per tool, confirmed to resolve to the same DB row).

### Tier 2 — ranking changes, full three-corpus strict-scorer bar
- [x] Item 3 — file-path pinning: detect path/filename substrings in the query, resolve via existing `files_by_suffix` (new `GraphStore` method, empty default), admit up to `PATH_PIN_MAX_SYMBOLS` per file as direct hits + provisional `PATH_PIN_BONUS`; ambiguity policy (1 / 2–3 / >3 matches), PPR seeded by own lexical score, pinned files exempt from test/generated penalties. Real subset: 16/90 erpnext + 4/40 lexical tasks name a file (diagnostic only; ship decision on full suites). Arms: strip vs. keep path text. **Mechanism implemented and unit-tested 2026-09-27** (commit `73ba380`): `PathPinConfig` (mode off/strip/keep, bonus, max_symbols_per_file). 25 new tests (9 `codegraph-store`, incl. a regression guard for the `billing_status.py`-matching-bare-`status.py` hazard flagged in review — already correctly anchored; 7 `codegraph-core` mock-pipeline; 9 pure-logic detection). Full workspace build/test/clippy clean. **First measurement (2026-09-27, ~14:00-15:00) was WRONG and is RETRACTED**: it concluded "zero movement, not shipped" — actually a config-loading bug, not a real result. All three corpus roots have a pre-existing `.codegraph/config.toml` (from earlier sessions) that silently shadows the top-level `codegraph.toml` the sweep was editing (`load_project_config` checks `.codegraph/config.toml` first, with no warning) — every "arm" measured, including the bonus-0.6 plateau check, was actually running `path_pin.mode = Off` throughout. Caught by an independent second-opinion review of the transcript (the bonus=0.6 erp-48 result didn't look boosted at all; the off-vs-bonus-0.6 score deltas were ~1e-7, floating-point noise, not bonus-scale). **Corrected same day, SHIPPED as the new pipeline default** (`PathPinMode::default() == Strip`, no config needed — matches Item 1's always-on pattern, not Jev's opt-in one): fixed by editing the real `.codegraph/config.toml` files and adding a permanent startup log (`src/server.rs`: "loaded project config" / "effective retrieval config") so this class of bug is a 2-second log check from now on. Also found and fixed a second, related silent-default bug (`PathPinConfig::default()` hardcoded `Off` instead of calling `PathPinMode::default()` — a table specifying only `bonus` and omitting `mode` would have silently resolved to `Off`; regression test added). **Real, reproduced (2 runs) three-corpus result for the shipped arm A/"strip" @ bonus 0.15**: lexical 17→**18**/40 pass@5 (MRR 0.2362→**0.2496**), erpnext 23→**24**/90 pass@5 (MRR ~0.170→**0.173**), keycloak unchanged (32/40, 0.5570 — 0 keycloak tasks name a file, as the plan predicted). Every task that moved is one of the plan's own pre-identified file-naming tasks except one (`erp-24`, ±1 rank) confirmed to be pre-existing float/hash-order jitter unrelated to pinning (detection fires on `annotation/constructor` in its text but resolves to 0 files — a true no-op for that task, verified via the startup log). Arm A beats arm B ("keep") on lexical (no regression vs. keep's small one) and ties on erpnext. Plateau checked at bonus 0.075/0.15/0.3 — real, stable, every value beats baseline (kept the default at 0.15). Latency: path-pinning itself adds ~0 measurable overhead (off vs. strip on 25 real keycloak queries: 859.4ms vs. 858.6ms p95, within noise) — but this surfaced a real, pre-existing, **not a Plan 22 regression** finding: keycloak's actual p95 is ~8.6x over the CLAUDE.md 100ms ship gate regardless of path-pin, flagged as load-bearing input for item 4's own latency work. The `$`-prefixed lexical strict-scorer artifact diagnosis from the retracted report still holds (independently true, unrelated to the config bug); the "erpnext file already dominates FTS" explanation is retracted along with the rest of that report. Full corrected account: `bench-reports/plan22-item3-CORRECTED-shipped-2026-09-27.md` (supersedes `bench-reports/plan22-item3-path-pin-not-shipped-2026-09-27.md`, kept only as a record of the mistake). **Arm C (tokenizing `chunk_fts.file_path`) is no longer deferred** — the "no motivating case" reasoning that deferred it doesn't hold now that A/B shows real value; next up.
- [x] Item 3 arm C (separate measurement) — **finding**: `chunk_fts.file_path` is written raw while `qualified_name`/`chunk_text` go through `tokenize_name`, so stemmed query terms can't match the 2.0-weighted path column. Tokenize it consistently via migration `010` + backfill; measured on its own. **Implemented, measured, and NOT SHIPPED 2026-09-27**: small real gains on lexical (MRR 0.2496→0.2537) and erpnext (0.1729→0.1749), but a **severe, reproduced regression on keycloak** (pass@1 14→2/40, pass@5 32→26/40, MRR 0.557→0.301) — broad and systemic, not a few tasks, root-caused to keycloak's deep, highly repetitive Java package-path convention (`org/keycloak/...`): appending decomposed path tokens to all 92,013 symbols floods the `file_path` FTS column with near-zero-IDF common segments (`org`, `keycloak`, `models`, `services`, …) and shifts BM25's per-column length normalization corpus-wide, a scaling failure the plan's own hypothesis didn't anticipate. Fully reverted (code, migration, the two unit tests it required rewriting) — nothing was committed, a clean `git checkout` sufficed. **Recovery required more than the revert**: `backfill_fts_index`'s by-design skip of `ChunkBuilder`'s graph-context prefix meant restoring via the cheap backfill path left keycloak still regressed (MRR 0.306) even with the code reverted; a genuine `codegraph index --fresh` on all three corpora was needed, which restored (and marginally exceeded, via fresh SCIP re-ingestion) the original quality — keycloak settled at pass@5 33/40, MRR 0.5887, reproduced twice. Arm A ("strip", the shipped item 3 default) is unaffected — it never touches `chunk_fts` content. Full account: `bench-reports/plan22-item3-armC-not-shipped-2026-09-27.md`.
- [x] Item 1 — generated-file soft down-rank (`GENERATED_FILE_PENALTY`, provisional 0.15, `max` not sum with `TEST_FILE_PENALTY`, query-gated). Conservative filename rules + banner match **only in the leading comment block**. Corpus scan: a loose banner rule would hit 514/3,642 erpnext files (all false positives — an in-class `auto-generated types` region) and 2 lexical false positives; strict-rule true positives are only 38 JAXB files in keycloak, no task targets them. Current corpora = regression gate only; value needs a codegen-heavy fourth corpus. Regression fixtures pin the erpnext/lexical/JAXB shapes. **Shipped (unconditional, no config gate) 2026-09-27**: `is_generated_filename` (next to `is_test_file`) + `SourceLoader::is_generated` (content-banner scan, cached alongside the snippet content it reuses). **Two real gaps found and fixed before shipping, both verified against actual corpus file content, not guessed**: (1) the plan's same-line generated+"do not edit" co-occurrence spec would have missed keycloak's real JAXB banner entirely — it says "will be lost upon recompilation," never "do not edit," and the two phrases sit on different lines; widened to whole-leading-block co-occurrence plus a "will be lost" alternative phrase. (2) a bare `'` in the universal comment-token list would have made `sidebars.js`'s real `'use strict';` line look like a comment, letting the scan continue into unrelated prose containing "generated" — dropped `'` (no registered language here uses it as a real line-comment marker). 16 new tests (12 in `source_loader.rs` using verbatim real corpus text for every fixture — erpnext's indented region, `sidebars.js`, keycloak's actual JAXB banner, protoc-gen-go, `_pb2.py`, `@generated`, `<auto-generated>`; 4 in `tests/pipeline.rs` — query-gate on/off with a confound-free isolated comparison, `max`-not-sum combination). Full workspace build/test/clippy clean. **Regression gate passed exactly**: two reproduced runs, all three corpora, pass@5 and MRR bit-for-bit identical to the pre-item-1 baseline on every metric (lexical 17/40 MRR 0.2362, erpnext 23/90 MRR 0.170, keycloak 32/40 MRR 0.5570) — zero leaking, the plan's specific named hazard. Full account: `bench-reports/plan22-item1-generated-file-penalty-2026-09-27.md`.

### Tier 3 — empirical sweep
- [x] Item 4 — `PPR_ITERATIONS` sweep {2,4,8,12,25} + L1-delta early-stop variant. At α=0.25, 4 iterations leaves (0.75)^4 ≈ 32% un-restarted mass; <1e-3 needs ~24. Step 0: add latency capture (no real-corpus p95 exists; `strict-rank-eval.py` records no timing). Coupled with Plan 21 item 16 (β/damping tuned at 4 iterations); SCIP state fixed per corpus; determinism check (HashMap float-accumulation order); Plan 21 sweep process lessons apply. **Done 2026-09-27, decision: 4 stays.** Step 0: `PPR_ITERATIONS`/`DIRECT_HIT_GRAPH_CREDIT_DAMPING` moved into config (`PprConfig`, defaults reproduce pre-item-4 behavior exactly, verified by a new test); `strict-rank-eval.py` now captures real per-call p50/p95; startup log extended with the new fields (closes the exact config-shadowing failure mode item 3 hit — every sweep point verified via the log before trusting its measurement). **First-ever real-corpus latency numbers**: none of the three corpora meet the 100ms p95 gate at any iteration count tested (lexical 557ms, erpnext 331ms, keycloak 804ms at the shipped default) — a pre-existing condition, unaffected by iteration count (2 through 25 and early-stop all land within ~15ms of each other; `enrich()`'s per-candidate work dominates, not PPR). **Sweep result**: a real, reproduced convergence plateau at 8-25 iterations (all functionally identical to each other) that beats the current default on lexical (MRR 0.2783→0.2911) and keycloak (0.5887→0.5953) but **regresses erpnext** (pass@5 23→22/90, MRR 0.1737→0.1660) — reproduced twice, not noise. A Phase-B damping check at iterations=8 (±0.25, per the plan's own "don't run the full joint grid" discipline) found neither direction fixes erpnext without giving back the other gains. 2 iterations clearly worse than 4 on all three, matching the plan's own math. Shipped default (4) verified to reproduce exactly after the sweep; all corpus configs restored byte-exact. 8-25 flagged as a real, verified-safe candidate for a future joint β/γ/damping+iterations re-tune (Plan 21 item 16, still open) — out of scope for this iteration-only sweep. Full account: `bench-reports/plan22-item4-ppr-iterations-sweep-2026-09-27.md`.

### Tier 4 — design discussion only
- [x] Item 5 — session-aware response dedup: blocked by transport (plain `POST /mcp`, protocol `2024-11-05`, no session id, one daemon shared by all agents) and a correctness risk from client-side context compaction. Step 0: measure the real duplicate-snippet rate from `codegraph_mcp::tool_call` logs/transcripts; deliverable is a go/no-go design note, not code. **Done 2026-09-27, verdict: NO-GO, stronger than the plan anticipated.** Step 0 measured across 128 real agent transcripts (`benchmarks/dataset/results/**/transcripts/codegraph-*.json`, genuine benchmark runs, not synthetic): 6.8% of snippet bytes are byte-identical repeats within a session (27.3% of sessions have ≥1 repeat) — real but modest, not a dramatic finding. Verified the plan's own flagged uncertainty via a live web search: the MCP `Mcp-Session-Id` header the plan speculated might justify a transport upgrade existed in spec revisions 2025-03-26 through 2025-11-25 but was **removed** in the current 2026-07-28 revision — the protocol moved toward fully stateless transport (any request servable by any instance, no session affinity), the opposite direction from what this item would need. A future transport upgrade would have *less* session machinery to build on, not more. Combined with the plan's own flagged correctness hazard (client-side context compaction making a back-reference resolve to content the agent no longer holds), recommendation is a clear no-go — revisit only on a much larger duplicate-rate finding or a concrete context-loss incident, and prefer the two cheaper stateless alternatives (tighter `truncate_snippet` limits, a caller-supplied `exclude_symbols` argument) first. Full account: `bench-reports/plan22-item5-session-dedup-design-note-2026-09-27.md`.

## Erpnext corpus re-pin — a real benchmark-hygiene bug found via competitor comparison (2026-09-27)

*User asked for a fresh head-to-head LLM-graded benchmark against a direct competitor tool (aliased `codegraph-colby` throughout this project's history, per its own "no naming the competitor" convention) using this project's own established harness (`benchmarks/dataset/harness/`), 5 questions (Q01-Q05), erpnext corpus. codegraph mean coverage 64.0%, competitor 68.8% (n=5; codegraph won/tied 3 of 5 but lost the other 2 by larger margins). User asked to investigate the two clear losses (Q04, Q05) and look for a learnable, fixable lesson.*

- [x] **Transcript investigation (2 parallel forks, real transcripts + real grading verdicts)**: Q04's loss was a wash — both agents abandoned MCP retrieval for raw `Grep` and the miss came down to which hand-picked file glob each happened to narrow to; not a codegraph-vs-competitor signal. Q05 (the big 35.7pp loss) is real: our `find_relevant_code` returned a wrong top-1 result at 0.96 confidence (`SubcontractingInwardController.validate_material_transfer`, an unrelated subsystem) for a query about excess-transfer validation, and our agent's fallback manual file read anchored on that wrong location and missed the real target 60+ lines earlier in the same file. Their agent's read happened to start further back and caught it — not better retrieval, a luckier anchor (neither tool surfaced the real target by name).
- [x] **Root-cause check before attempting a fix**: hand-verified via the real `tokenize_name` output (a throwaway Rust binary against the actual `codegraph-core` crate) that the wrong candidate scores a perfect 1.0 name-token match (`valid`/`materi`/`transfer`, all 3 present in the query) while the true target scores 0.667 (`excess` never appears in the query — it says "more … than … requires", not "excess"). **This is a vocabulary gap, not a weighting problem** — no reweighting of matched tokens can fix a token that was never matched. Confirmed the true target is **absent even at `top_n=100`** — a recall-stage miss, not reachable by any rerank-stage change. This closed off the originally-proposed "own-token-fraction name score" fix before writing any code — that exact fix shape was already tried and reverted once in this project's history (checklist.md line 721, Plan 16) for regressing the full suite despite fixing its own targeted anecdote.
- [x] **Prevalence check**: of 90 erpnext strict-scorer tasks, only 4 are genuine rerank-stage misses (target in the pool, ranked 31-100) — a rerank-only fix has a ceiling of at most 4/90 tasks, the same "helps one, redistributes everything else" shape that regressed the suite twice before. **35/90 are recall-stage misses** (target absent even at top-100) — investigated further per the next item.
- [x] **`top_k` widening test (temporary diagnostic, reverted before shipping)**: temporarily raised `find_relevant_code`'s hardcoded `top_k` (candidate-generation width, independent of the `top_n` argument callers actually control — a real, separate finding on its own: `top_n` only widens the final truncation, not what candidates are ever considered) from 20 to 100 and re-ran the full 90-task recall check. **Reproduced the exact historical failure mode**: recovered only 1 previously-absent target into the findable range while pushing 5 *previously-correct* targets out of the top-30 zone (crowding/noise from a wider, noisier raw pool) — a 5:1 unfavorable trade, net -4. Confirms the prior "ungated recall widening" reversions were correct, not unlucky. Reverted immediately, rebuilt, re-verified baseline reproduces exactly.
- [x] **The real discovery — checked whether the 35 recall-miss targets exist in the corpus at all**: **16 of 35 (46%) don't exist anywhere in the indexed erpnext checkout**, confirmed via direct `grep -r` across the whole repo (not renamed, not moved — genuinely absent). Traced each one's `_source_sha` (the commit `llm_tasks_erpnext_90.yaml`'s extractor mined it from) against the pinned/indexed commit via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`: **14 of 16 source commits are not ancestors of the old pin** (`95212e5`, 2026-07-08) — dated 2026-07-09 through 2026-07-17, i.e. *after* it. The task file was mined from erpnext history past the point the benchmark corpus was actually pinned at. This had been silently capping every erpnext strict-scorer number measured all session (Plan 22 items 1/3/4's erpnext baselines all inherit this ceiling) — a benchmark-construction bug, not a retrieval-quality one.
- [x] **Fixed — re-pinned to the minimal correct commit**: found the chronologically latest of all 90 tasks' `_source_sha` values (`1887825ce5c6fe2fbd5366ab52e4c73de24d23fa`, 2026-07-17) and confirmed via `git merge-base --is-ancestor` that **all other 89 source commits are its ancestors** — the smallest possible re-pin that makes every task's target reachable, without pulling in more drift than necessary. Updated `benchmarks/dataset/setup/setup-erpnext.sh` and `benchmarks/dataset/README.md` to the new pin (both documented the old one). Re-indexed from scratch (`codegraph index --fresh`: 4,739→4,770 files, 43,704→44,062 symbols, a modest ~9-day delta as expected), SCIP re-ingested automatically on daemon restart. **34/35 targets now exist**; the remaining 2 (`erp-29`, `erp-86`) confirmed as genuine later renames (`_query_opening_balance_duckdb`→`get_opening_balances_duckdb`, `get_billed_qty_against_purchase_order`→`get_billed_qty_amount_against_purchase_order`) — not a pin problem, left as-is.
- [x] **Real, reproduced result (2 runs, bit-for-bit identical)**: erpnext strict-scorer pass@5 **23/90 → 30/90** (+7 tasks), MRR **0.1737 → 0.1910**. Lexical and keycloak unaffected (unchanged, as expected — only erpnext's checkout moved). This is the single highest-value, lowest-risk fix found in this whole investigation — it doesn't touch the retrieval pipeline at all, it just makes the benchmark honestly measure what it claims to.
- [x] **Competitor comparison re-run against the corrected corpus (2026-09-27)**: re-pinned the competitor's own separate `erpnext-colby` checkout to the same corrected commit and re-indexed it with the competitor's own binary (fairness — both tools now index the identical corpus), then re-ran Q01-Q05 for both arms and re-graded (idempotent per-`(task_id, arm)` grading required `--regrade` since results were freshly overwritten; caught and handled a `grade.py` transient 180s judge-call timeout on an unrelated task from the same full-arm regrade sweep — recovered by re-running only the missing task_ids, since `grades.json` is written incrementally). **Result: the gap nearly closed** — codegraph 64.0%→**67.1%**, competitor 68.8%→**68.3%**, gap **+4.8pp→+1.2pp** (within noise at n=5). Q03/Q04 flipped which side won between the two corpus states (corpus-content shift, not a systemic tool difference). The one *consistent* loss across both runs is Q05 (57.1% vs 78.6%) — the same diagnosed vocabulary-gap/recall-stage issue above, unaffected by the corpus fix. **Read: the original apparent competitor edge was mostly a stale-corpus artifact**, not a real retrieval-quality gap — this is what motivated the deeper architecture investigation in the Plan 23 section below.
- [ ] **Not yet done**: Plan 22 items 1/3/4's own shipped-default validations were all measured against the *old*, incomplete erpnext pin — their pass@5/MRR numbers in this file's earlier sections are still accurate *as measurements of what was true then*, but a future re-verification of those decisions against the corrected corpus would be worth doing (not expected to change any ship/no-ship call, since the fix only adds previously-unreachable targets back into the pool rather than changing ranking behavior, but not yet confirmed). The harness's own 30-question `erpnext_tasks.yaml` (used for the codegraph-vs-competitor LLM-graded comparison, a separate file from the 90-task strict-scorer corpus) was not checked for the same staleness pattern — flagged as an open question, not yet investigated.

## Plan 23 — MCP payload sizing and tool consolidation (2026-09-28)
*Ref: [plans/23-mcp-payload-sizing-and-tool-consolidation.md](plans/23-mcp-payload-sizing-and-tool-consolidation.md)*

*User asked for a deep dive into the competitor's architecture after the re-pinned-corpus comparison above narrowed the quality gap to near-parity (+1.2pp) while codegraph still used far more tool calls (51 vs 32) and cost ($8.17 vs $5.38) for the same 5 questions — "can we reduce the tools call... optimize indexing and retrieval pipeline as is the competitors... since they are opensource... Clone full the repo to our env then deep on it." A research fork cloned the competitor's real source and read it alongside ours.*

### Phase 1 — payload sizing (research + implementation)
- [x] **Research fork**: cloned the competitor's repo (scratchpad-only, never named in any tracked file — alias `codegraph-colby` preserved throughout), read their actual MCP/indexing/retrieval source. Found their substrate is the same family as ours (SQLite + FTS5 + graph, no embeddings/vector search/LLM reranker) — the efficiency gap is entirely response-shaping and tool-surface design, not a smarter algorithm. Two mechanisms: (1) their main tool returns near-full source per result within a repo-size-tiered character budget, so the calling agent rarely needs a follow-up Read; (2) they ship 8 tools internally but list far fewer by default, citing their own internal A/B testing that more listed tools causes more agent mis-picks.
- [x] **Design validated by a Plan agent pass**: grepped every real call site of the current tool names across the whole repo (not just docs), surfacing several code-level breakage risks the original design missed (see Phase 2 below) — refined before any code was written.
- [x] **Implemented**: `response_budget()`/`size_snippets_fair_share()`/`truncate_snippet_on_line_boundary()` in `crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/mod.rs` — a repo-size-tiered character budget (<500/500-5000/>5000 files → 4000/2500/1500 chars per item, 24000 total, 300 floor) that shrinks later/larger results toward a floor rather than ever dropping one, always truncating on a line boundary. Applied to `find_relevant_code` (replacing the flat 600-char cap) and `explain_symbol` (replacing the flat 800-char cap — an explicit scope addition beyond the originally-approved item list, justified because `explain_symbol`/`inspect`'s symbol mode would otherwise return *less* source than a fuzzy search hit for the same symbol). `get_file_context` gained an opt-in `include_source` mode (`SourceLoader::full_text()`, a thin public wrapper around the existing private `load()`) returning the whole file as 1-based-numbered text (matching `Read`'s convention, deliberately inconsistent with the pre-existing 0-based `lines` field elsewhere in the same response), centered on `focus_line` when given via a balanced outward-expansion window.
- [x] **Verified bit-identical retrieval quality**: `scripts/strict-rank-eval.py` on all three corpora reproduced the pre-change baseline exactly — lexical MRR 0.2783/17-40, keycloak 0.5887/33-40, erpnext 0.1910/30-90 — confirming this phase changes only response presentation, never ranking. 13 new unit tests (`tools/mod.rs`'s budget/truncation/fair-share logic, `get_file_context.rs`'s windowing). Full workspace clean (`cargo build/test/clippy --workspace`). Live-verified against the running daemon: a real lexical function's snippet went from a flat 600-char cap to 2444 chars (full body); `get_file_context(include_source=true)` returned a 2093-line file's full text, correctly 1-based-numbered and budget-truncated. Committed separately from Phase 2 (`82dfb1f`) so the harness's tool-calls-per-question metric can attribute any change to the right cause.

### Phase 2 — tool consolidation
- [x] **Grouping designed by "what shape of question the agent has," not "what shape of answer the tool returns"** — the latter axis, tried first, produced a `trace_impact`+`summarize_module` merge that shared nothing conceptually and created two tools with an ambiguous `symbol`/`path` discriminator; discarded in favor of: `search_code` (merges `find_relevant_code`+`find_implementations`, `intent` xor `implements`), `inspect` (merges `explain_symbol`+`get_file_context`+`summarize_module`, `symbol` xor `path` — `path` auto-classifies via a new shared `resolve_file_lookup`/`PathLookup` helper: resolves to one file → file-context behavior; not a file → module-summary behavior; ambiguous multi-file match → **hard error, never a silent fallback**, the one place presence-inference could otherwise quietly answer the wrong question), `trace_impact` (left unmerged — reads as a single unambiguous verb), `watch_status` (unchanged, structurally the outlier).
- [x] **Implemented as a pure routing layer**: new `tools/search_code.rs`/`tools/inspect.rs` route to the existing, **unmodified** `find_relevant_code::handle`/`find_implementations::handle`/`explain_symbol::handle`/`get_file_context::handle`/`summarize_module::handle` — none of the six underlying handler modules' retrieval/graph logic was rewritten. `handle_tools_list()`/`handle_tools_call()` rewritten to the new 4-name surface.
- [x] **Real code-level breakage found and fixed** (beyond the MCP server itself, via the validation pass's whole-repo grep — not just docs): `scripts/strict-rank-eval.py` and `scripts/llm-task-eval.py` (this project's own MRR ship-gate scripts — highest-severity item in this plan, since breaking them silently could be misdiagnosed as a retrieval regression rather than a rename), `scripts/agent-bench.py`/`scripts/vs-graphify.py`'s wire-level `_mcp_call`/`tool_*` functions, `src/main.rs`'s `codegraph init` setup-hook (`SETUP_HOOK_BLOCK` renamed to the new 4 tools; `SETUP_HOOK_MARKER` deliberately decoupled from any single tool name — was `"find_relevant_code"`, now the block's own stable heading text — so a future rename can't silently break `codegraph init`'s own idempotency detection the same way again), and `.claude/settings.local.json`'s own MCP permission allowlist (5 old `mcp__codegraph__*` entries → 4 new ones). `crates/codegraph-mcp/tests/mcp_server.rs`: renamed/fixed the tool-count tests (including a pre-existing bug where `http_tools_list_body_contains_5_tools` was misnamed but actually asserted 7 — now correctly named and asserting 4), updated ~13 `call_tool`/`dispatch` call sites to the new names and argument shapes, added 3 new tests for the merged tools' xor-argument error paths (both given, neither given).
- [x] **`CLAUDE.md` updated following its own established convention** (annotate with a correction, not delete the old text — matching how it documented Plan 20's vector-search removal): the "MCP tools stay at 7" hard invariant, the crate-map bullet, the now-superseded "8th flagship tool" framing (the direction reversed to consolidation instead), the ship-gate changelog line, and the top-of-file "Working rules for agents" tool-usage instructions (the literal instructions this and every future agent session follows — the highest-priority fix in the whole doc pass, since a stale version would actively mislead future sessions).
- [x] **Verified**: `cargo build/test/clippy --workspace` clean; manual `tools/list` (exactly 4, correct schemas) and `tools/call` exercises for every branch of `search_code`/`inspect` against the live daemon; re-ran `scripts/strict-rank-eval.py` on all three corpora post-consolidation — bit-identical to the Phase-1-verified baseline (only the tool name/arg-passing changed, never ranking).
- [x] **Final verification, done 2026-09-28 — a real, honest null result on the metric this whole plan targets.** Re-ran the erpnext Q01-Q05 `codegraph` arm twice against the new 4-tool surface (competitor arm not re-run — its own tool set is untouched by this plan, so its numbers stand). Both runs hit the same transient `grade.py` 180s judge-call timeout on an unrelated task from the same full-arm `--regrade` sweep seen earlier this session — same recovery both times (backfill the missing task_ids only, `grades.json` writes incrementally).
  - **Tool calls**: old 7-tool baseline 51 → new 4-tool rep1 **55**, rep2 **52** (avg 53.5). **Tool calls did not go down — if anything, slightly up.** This directly contradicts the plan's central hypothesis (drawn from the competitor's own documented rationale: bigger per-call payloads + fewer listed tools should mean fewer round trips).
  - **Coverage**: 67.1% → rep1 62.4%, rep2 69.8% (avg 66.1%) — within the noise band already established this session (individual task swings of 15-20pp between runs are normal for this harness at n=5), not a real regression, but not an improvement either.
  - **Cost**: $8.17 → rep1 $7.72, rep2 $8.09 (avg $7.90) — roughly flat.
  - **The competitor's own advantage is essentially unchanged**: 32 tool calls / $5.38 / 68.3% coverage, vs. our ~53.5 / ~$7.90 / ~66.1%.
  - **Honest read, not oversold**: Phase 1/2 shipped real, independently-valuable engineering (bigger usable snippets, an `include_source` Read-substitute, a smaller/cleaner agent-facing tool menu, zero retrieval-ranking regression, several real code-level bugs fixed along the way) — but **the specific mechanism this plan bet on (bigger payloads + fewer tools ⇒ fewer agent tool calls) did not transfer to our own agent/tool-description quality in this real end-to-end test.** Plausible reasons, not yet investigated: our original 7-tool descriptions were already unusually thorough/cross-referencing (this project's own convention), so there may have been little mis-pick waste for consolidation to recover in the first place; a bigger snippet surfaces more caller/callee names worth following up on, which can *prompt* more exploration rather than less; and neither this baseline nor the old one was itself run twice, so some of the apparent "51" baseline precision was always a single data point. **Not reverted** — the payload-sizing and consolidation changes stand on their own correctness/ranking-parity merits, but the tool-call-reduction claim is not supported by this measurement and should not be repeated as a result without the caveat above.
- [x] **Root cause found and fixed, 2026-09-28** — a transcript-level investigation (real `inspect`/`Read` tool-use blocks from the erpnext Q01/Q02/Q05 transcripts, cross-checked directly and independently confirmed) found the actual mechanism: **`include_source` was never once used**. 0/6 path-mode `inspect` calls across Q01/Q02/Q05 passed `include_source: true`, and every one of those 6 was immediately followed by a raw `Read` of that exact same file — one file (`subcontracting_controller.py` in Q02) was `Read` three separate times in one question despite being `inspect`'d first. Root cause is the tool description itself: it told the agent to "call this before a blind Read," explicitly framing the default response as a pre-Read step rather than a Read substitute — the agent did exactly what it was told. **Confirmed pre-existing, not a Plan 23 regression**: the identical `get_file_context(path) → Read(same path)` pattern exists 1:1 in the pre-Plan-23 transcripts (`git show f7dc07e:...`) — the capability that was supposed to close this gap was new, but at 0% adoption it changed nothing, meaning the original hypothesis was never actually exercised by the first measurement. No evidence of wasted calls from the `search_code`/`inspect` merge itself (zero xor-argument errors in any transcript). **Fixed**: `include_source` now defaults to `true` for `inspect`'s single-file path-mode branch (routing-layer default in `tools/inspect.rs`, not `get_file_context.rs`'s own module-level default — kept at `false` there for its own unit tests / any other internal caller), removing the redundant-Read dependency on the agent choosing correctly rather than relying on a prompt nudge. Tool description rewritten to state this plainly ("THIS IS THE DEFAULT... do not follow this call with a Read of the same path") and to explain the remaining `include_source: false` opt-out case (cheap signature-list-only scanning across several candidate files). 2 new tests (`http_inspect_path_mode_includes_source_by_default`, `..._include_source_false_omits_source`, both against a real temp file so `SourceLoader::full_text` genuinely exercises) pin both directions so a future change can't silently flip the default back. Full workspace clean (`cargo build/test/clippy --workspace`), bit-identical `strict-rank-eval.py` MRR confirmed unaffected (this fix only changes `inspect`'s payload default, not `search_code`'s ranking). **Re-measured against the competitor harness (2 reps, 2026-09-28) — a real drop, with a real cost/quality trade-off, both reported honestly:**

| | tool_calls | cost | coverage |
|---|---|---|---|
| old 7-tool baseline | 51 | $8.17 | 67.1% |
| pre-fix 4-tool (avg of 2) | 53.5 | $7.90 | 66.1% |
| **post-fix 4-tool (avg of 2)** | **39.5** | **$9.23** | **63.6%** |
| post-fix rep1 | 45 | $9.07 | 69.8% |
| post-fix rep2 | 34 | $9.39 | 57.4% |
| competitor (unchanged) | 32 | $5.38 | 68.3% |

Tool calls dropped a real, reproduced ~26% (53.5→39.5 avg), closing most of the gap to the competitor's 32 — the fix works as intended and the effect reproduces across both reps (45, then 34; not a one-off). Cost went up somewhat ($7.90→$9.23 avg) — fewer round trips but each `inspect` call's response is now a full file dump by default, so total tokens per session rose even with fewer calls; this part is a genuine, expected trade-off, not investigated further.
- [x] **rep2's apparent coverage dip investigated at zero additional spend (2026-09-28)**, using only already-collected transcripts/grades (user explicitly declined a further paid re-run) — see `bench-reports/`-style findings folded directly in here since no separate report file was written. **Two real causes found, neither a structural problem with the `include_source` fix:**
  1. **A real grading-pipeline bug, not a content gap**: `grade.py`'s verbatim-quote audit rejected a judge verdict whose quote used `"..."` to elide a genuinely verbatim middle portion — the judge's own `reasoning` said *"Matches the gold fact's function name, file, formula, and error message exactly,"* and the real answer text confirmed it (independently verified by reading the raw answer directly). The whole-string check had no tolerance for a common, honest judge quoting style. **Fixed**: `_quote_is_verbatim()` (new, `harness/grade.py`) splits a quote on `"..."`/`"…"` and requires each fragment to be an exact, in-order substring — still rejects a genuine paraphrase-wrapped-in-real-edges or out-of-order fragments (both regression-tested), just stops treating elision itself as automatic hallucination. `JUDGE_SYSTEM` prompt updated to explicitly permit this quoting style under the same exactness rule. 9 new tests in `harness/test_grade.py` (no pytest dependency, plain assertions — this harness has none), including the exact real Q05 case that motivated the fix. **Retroactively corrected 5 already-recorded task/arm grades at zero additional API cost** (re-applying the fixed, deterministic audit logic to judge quotes already on disk, no re-invocation of the paid judge): `Q08/graphify` 83.3%→100%, `Q17/baseline` 75.0%→83.3%, `Q15/codegraph` 58.3%→75.0%, `Q05/codegraph` 57.1%→71.4%, `Q01/codegraph` 66.7%→75.0%. The other 7 audit-downgraded verdicts across the dataset were checked and correctly still fail (genuine whitespace/formatting mismatches in code-snippet quotes — a different, unrelated issue, not touched). **Corrected rep2 mean coverage: 57.4%→61.9%** — narrows but doesn't fully close the gap to rep1 (69.8%) or the competitor (68.3%).
  2. **The remainder is a pre-existing, already-documented retrieval gap plus ordinary exploration-breadth variance — not caused by this fix.** Q04's low score (both reps) traces to the agent abandoning `search_code` after one call for a decorator-pattern query (`@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`) and falling back to raw `Grep` — the exact failure mode already found and written up earlier this session (`bench-reports/erpnext-recall-gap-investigation-and-repin-2026-09-27.md`, "What's still open"); both the old and new tool surfaces hit it identically. Q01/Q02's remaining misses are query-formulation gaps (explored a plausible-but-wrong file; never asked about cancellation logic at all), not cases of a big `inspect` dump causing premature stopping — checked directly: turn usage was nowhere near the 30-turn cap in any question (5-12 of 30), and `inspect` calls were confirmed returning large (21-36KB) real content as designed.
  - **Corrected net picture**: tool_calls 53.5→39.5 avg (real, reproduced ~26% drop); coverage 66.1%→65.8% avg (post-correction — essentially at parity, not a regression); cost $7.90→$9.23 avg (a real, expected trade-off from bigger per-call payloads). **The original tool-call-reduction goal is achieved and reproduced, coverage holds at parity once the grading bug is corrected, and the only clear remaining cost is higher token spend per session** — a materially better final result than the pre-correction numbers suggested.
- [x] **Cost driver found and fixed, 2026-09-28** — user asked to close the remaining cost/coverage gap to the competitor. Measured real per-call `tool_result` sizes in the current transcript: every `inspect` path-mode call was 20,000-36,000 chars (5,000-9,000+ tokens) — `get_file_context`'s `include_source` branch was using `ResponseBudget::total_chars` (24000, designed to be *shared* across several ranked results in `find_relevant_code`/`explain_symbol`) as a *per-call* cap for one call returning one file. **Fixed**: new `ResponseBudget::single_file_chars` tier (8000/6000/4000 by repo size, same shape as `per_item_chars` but sized for a genuinely single-file response), wired into `get_file_context.rs` in place of `total_chars`. Verified live before spending on a re-run: erpnext `inspect` calls dropped from ~24-36K chars to ~6000 chars (matching the mid-size tier), bit-identical `strict-rank-eval.py` MRR (only `inspect`'s payload size changed, not ranking). New regression test (`single_file_chars_scales_with_repo_size_like_everything_else`) pins the tiering and that it always stays below `total_chars`. **Re-verified end-to-end (paid re-run, user approved)**: erpnext Q01-Q05, `codegraph` arm — tool_calls 43, cost **$7.70** (back below the original 7-tool baseline's $8.17, down from the uncapped fix's $9.07-9.39), coverage 65.2% (0 grading-audit corrections needed this run). **Final settled picture**:

    | | tool_calls | cost | coverage |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | old 7-tool baseline | 51 | $8.17 | 67.1% |
    | pre-`include_source`-fix avg | 53.5 | $7.90 | 66.1% |
    | `include_source`-fix avg (uncapped) | 39.5 | $9.23 | 65.8% |
    | **+ single-file size cap (final)** | **43** | **$7.70** | **65.2%** |
    | competitor (unchanged throughout) | 32 | $5.38 | 68.3% |

  Tool calls down ~16% from the original baseline (51→43), cost now *below* the original baseline, coverage within the noise band already established this session (individual tasks swing 15-20pp run to run). The residual gap to the competitor (43 vs 32 calls, 65.2% vs 68.3% coverage) traces to the two already-diagnosed, pre-existing causes (Q04's decorator-pattern retrieval gap, Q01/Q02 exploration-breadth misses) — neither caused by or fixable through further payload/tool-surface tuning; closing them would need a separate, dedicated retrieval investigation, explicitly not attempted here given this project's history of two prior reverted attempts at similarly-scoped "fix one anecdote" retrieval changes.
- [x] **User asked to close the remaining gap further ("we still need less cost than them"), 2026-09-28.** A token-accounting check found total tokens scale *worse* than call count (1.66x vs 1.34x) — every additional tool call re-sends the whole accumulated conversation, so round-trip count matters more than any single response's size at this point. Real transcript evidence (5 real erpnext transcripts, 11 actual `search_code` intent calls) found **91% of calls (10/11) had at least one file appearing 2+ times** among their results — `find_relevant_code`'s flat per-symbol list was the reason a same-file follow-up `inspect`/`Read` was so common (a separate measurement found 28% of all 43 tool calls were plausible round-trip-elimination candidates targeting an already-surfaced file).
  - **Implemented (item #5 from the original investigation, previously deferred as too risky to bundle into Plan 23 — now done properly with its own plan+validation pass)**: `search_code`'s intent mode groups results by file instead of a flat per-symbol list. Each group carries one merged `source` field (the union of all matched symbols' own line spans in that file, budget-capped) instead of N separate small snippets. A **design-validation pass caught two real correctness bugs before any code was written**: (1) an off-by-one — `Symbol.span` is 0-based (tree-sitter convention) but the intended renderer took a 1-based `focus_line`; (2) centering one window on only the best-ranked symbol in a group had no guarantee of covering a second matched symbol far away in the same file, silently dropping a ranked result's body — both fixed by a new `spans_source()` (unions all matched spans, drops lowest-rank first when a group overflows, truncates rather than drops the group's own best span) instead of a window centered on one symbol. New `size_file_sources_fair_share()` caps each group at `min(single_file_chars, symbol_count * per_item_chars)` before fair-sharing `total_chars` — a multi-symbol group earns real room, a single-symbol group stays close to what a single snippet used to cost (not inflated to a flat per-file ceiling it doesn't need). 15 new unit tests (`crates/codegraph-mcp/src/tools/mod.rs`) plus 2 new/updated integration tests (`mcp_server.rs`, including a new real-multi-file fixture exercising actual grouping, not just the pre-existing single-file stub). Live-verified against erpnext: a real multi-symbol group (`LandedCostVoucher` class + a method) correctly filled its budget and flagged `source_truncated: true` rather than silently omitting content.
  - **A second real bug found and fixed during regression verification**: `strict-rank-eval.py`'s `find_rank()` (the MRR ship-gate) returned a stable, reproduced (3 runs) 30→29 pass@5 regression on erpnext specifically (lexical/keycloak stayed exactly bit-identical) — traced to grouping's group-ordering (by min-rank) no longer guaranteeing a strictly rank-ascending walk when a file's own matches interleave with another file's (e.g. fileA@rank1, fileB@rank2, fileA@rank3 walks as rank1, rank3, rank2), so "return the first name match found" could pick a worse-ranked duplicate/overloaded symbol (erpnext's `#N` overload-disambiguated names, e.g. `get_pending_work_orders#1`/`#2`, made this a real, reproducible issue there specifically). **Fixed by changing `find_rank()` to return the best (lowest) rank among all matches**, not the first one encountered — a correctness improvement that would have been silently masked before only because the old flat list happened to always be strictly rank-ascending. Root-caused via a systematic diagnostic (checked every "NOT FOUND" task at `top_n=100` for an exact vs. suffix-only vs. genuinely-absent match) before touching any code, not guessed at. Re-verified exact match to the established baseline (30/90, MRR 0.1910) after the fix, reproduced. Also updated `llm-task-eval.py`'s judge-context loop for the new grouped shape (and fixed a pre-existing, unrelated dead-code path in the same touched loop — a `file`-field `.rsplit("::")` that could never fire since `file` never contained `::`, now correctly reads the symbol's own qualified name).
  - **Re-run (2 reps, paid, 2026-09-28) — a modest, noisy improvement, not a clean win.** rep1: 46 calls/$8.09/60.7% coverage; rep2: 38 calls/$7.71/68.3% coverage (an exact tie with the competitor on quality). Avg: **42.0 calls / $7.90 / 64.5%** vs. the pre-grouping single-file-cap baseline's 43 calls/$7.70/65.2%, and the competitor's 32 calls/$5.38/68.3%. **Honest read**: file-grouping's effect is within the run-to-run noise this exact harness has shown all session (individual reps have swung 34-55 calls under an *unchanged* pipeline) — the averaged tool-call count barely moved (43→42.0) and cost/coverage are statistically flat. Grouping is not a regression (both reps land inside the established variance band, and rep2 alone matches the competitor's coverage exactly), but it is **not the clear win the 91%-same-file-duplication evidence predicted** — plausibly because per-question variance from what the agent chooses to explore dominates over the marginal round-trips grouping actually saves in practice. **Not reverted** — the code is correct (verified via the design/validation process, live-checked against real erpnext data, zero ranking regression), and grouping remains a sound architectural choice (matches the same real-source-per-call principle as the competitor's own tool) even though its measured effect on this specific 5-question sample is inside the noise floor. Further tool-call reduction, if pursued, would need either more repetitions to separate signal from this harness's inherent noise, or a different lever entirely — not assumed to be free from more grouping/payload tuning alone.
- [ ] **Not yet done, flagged for a separate decision, not pursued**: the competitor's adaptive/reduced *listed* tool surface per repo size (list all tools but show fewer for small repos) was investigated and explicitly recommended as a separate, future decision — this plan implemented a flat consolidation (always 4), not a size-adaptive listing.
